#2954. Dutch Bros' 2027 Outlook: Accelerating New Store Openings to Drive Growth

This under-the-radar consumer discretionary stock boasts a Superscore of 80 from our Hidden Gems Primary database, part of The Motley Fool's Moneyball Database system. Here's why.

By Robert Izquierdo – Updated Nov 25, 2026 at 1:43PM EST

10 stocks we like better than Dutch Bros ›

Picture the average morning at a Dutch Bros (BROS +4.04%) location. A line of cars wraps around the building, yet the flow remains steady as staff deliver drinks with an energy that feels like a choreographed performance rather than a transaction.

This drive-thru-only beverage retailer has scaled from a regional curiosity to a national powerhouse with over 1,200 locations in 27 states. The stock, trading at $34.27 as of Nov. 19, has faced a volatile year with a 31.8% decline over the past 12 months as investors weigh its rapid expansion against broader market pressures.

Our proprietary Hidden Gems scoring system assigns Dutch Bros an overall Superscore of 80 out of 100, placing it in the Strong category. The Superscore is an AI-powered score that evaluates a company's overall strength by combining financial performance, product market position, technological capabilities, leadership quality, and relative valuation. It represents the unification of all our scores into a single score for public companies, with five rating bands: Exceptional (90-100), Strong (75-89), Above Average (60-74), Average (40-59), and Cautious (0-39).

An 80 places the company in the Top ~10% of every company we score. This score serves as a data-driven signal for further investigation, and the following analysis highlights the tension between its operational growth and the risks that keep the score from moving higher.

Dutch Bros is a highly capital-efficient business, as evidenced by its strong return on net tangible assets. Because it earns a significant profit on a relatively small base of hard assets, each point of revenue growth has the potential to translate into outsized returns, which partially explains why investors are willing to pay a premium for the company's shares despite the inherent execution risks.

The Hidden Gems Superscore reflects The Motley Fool's proprietary AI-driven evaluation of a company across product, financial, leadership, and valuation pillars as of the article date and may change over time. Performance figures are point-in-time. Past performance does not guarantee future results.

You may want to keep researching before buying if...

This Superscore is one data-driven signal worth investigating, but it should not be considered a stand-alone buy recommendation; please weigh these points against your own research, long-term financial goals, and personal risk tolerance before making any investment decisions.

With impressive 30% year-over-year sales growth to $549.8 million in the third quarter of 2026, Dutch Bros has delivered blistering top-line expansion. It aims to continue the trend by opening at least 185 stores this year, and accelerating to about 250 new locations in 2027.

Management has established a real estate pipeline that puts it on a path to 2,029 shops by 2029. This development positions Dutch Bros to continue business expansion for the next several years.

While its stock sports an elevated trailing P/E ratio of 45.6, this represents a low point for the past year. The share price fell after the company reported Q3 results on Nov. 4, which showed slowing same-store sales growth. Dutch Bros reported a Q3 year-over-year increase of 4.4% in systemwide same-shop sales compared to 5.8% in Q2. Management also forecasted a softer 2026 full-year outlook to the low end of prior guidance amid customer choppiness as gas prices rose.

These short-term headwinds are likely to ease over time once the conflict in the Middle East winds down. With a multi-year roadmap of store openings, a capital-efficient operation, and a popular menu that extends into proprietary energy drinks, Dutch Bros is well positioned for years of business growth.

#2952. Top Wall Street analysts recommend these 3 dividend stocks for higher returns

Dividend-paying stocks can enhance portfolio returns by providing a steady income stream to investors and the potential for capital appreciation. Several investors seek exposure to dividend stocks in a volatile backdrop to ensure stable income.

In this regard, recommendations of top Wall Street analysts can help shortlist attractive dividend stocks, backed by strong financials and cash flows to support consistent dividend payments.

Here are three dividend-paying stocks that are highlighted by Wall Street's top pros, as tracked by TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

Energy Transfer (ET) is a diversified midstream energy company with about 140,000 miles of pipelines and associated energy infrastructure in 44 states. The company made a quarterly cash distribution of 34 cents per common unit for the second quarter of 2026. At an annualized payment of $1.36 per common unit, ET offers a yield of 6.3%.

Recently, JPMorgan analyst Jeremy Tonet reiterated a buy rating on Energy Transfer stock and slightly raised his price target to $25 from $24. The analyst noted that the company delivered a strong Q2 beat across all key metrics.

Tonet highlighted that Energy Transfer raised its 2026 adjusted EBITDA guidance to $18.8 billion-$19.1 billion from $18.2 billion-$18.6 billion, reflecting robust base results and cost optimization. The 5-star analyst added that the company "tightened" its capex to $5.6 billion–$5.9 billion and expects capex above $5 billion annually through 2029, given solid opportunities.

About the Hugh Brinson pipeline, Tonet noted Phase 1 (1.5 bcfd) reaching full capacity by September 1 and Phase II capacity (0.7 bcfd) going online by Q1 2027. The analyst also noted completion of a 14-mile Hugh Brinson lateral in Abilene, Texas, and final stages of talks for an additional 250 mmcfd of Oklahoma power plant demand.

"All in, we see ET well positioned to continue capitalizing on organic growth opportunities across the value chain," said Tonet.

Tonet ranks No. 922 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 57% of the time, delivering an average return of 9%. See Energy Transfer Financials on TipRanks.

This week's second dividend pick is independent oil and natural gas company Permian Resources (PR). The company declared a base dividend of $0.16 per share for the third quarter, payable on September 30, 2026. At an annualized dividend of $0.64 per share, PR offers a yield of about 2.7%.

Recently, Goldman Sachs analyst Neil Mehta reiterated a buy rating on Permian Resources stock and increased his price target to $27 from $22, citing an improved outlook on oil production growth and operational efficiencies.

The 5-star analyst noted that while PR stock has outperformed its peers so far in 2026, he sees further upside. Mehta is confident about the company's operational improvements and ability to deliver incremental free cash flow per share through the strategic ground game program. He added that the program has completed about $1.05 billion in bolt-on deals this year (as of August 5).

"We see the continued success of PR's ground game as constructive toward future inventory replenishment and increasing working interest over time," said Mehta.

The analyst also emphasized Permian Resources' solid track record of generating growth in free cash flow (FCF) per share. Mehta expects the company's free cash flow per share to grow at a 20% CAGR (compound annual growth rate) from 2025–2028.

Mehta ranks No. 401 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 64% of the time, delivering an average return of 12.8%. See Permian Resources Statistics on TipRanks.

Moving on to Sempra Energy (SRE), a utility holding company. Earlier this month, the company declared a quarterly dividend of $0.6575 per share, payable on October 15. At an annualized dividend of $2.63 per share, SRE stock offers a yield of about 3.1%.

Recently, Jefferies analyst Julien Dumoulin-Smith upgraded SRE stock to Buy from Hold but slightly lowered the price target to $97 from $101. The 5-star analyst noted that Sempra stock is trading at a price/earnings multiple that is 14% below its electric peers due to concerns about Texas transmission capital expenditure and the failure of California legislation.

Smith noted that the derating of weaker California peers PG&E (PCG) and Edison International (EIX) is weighing on the sum-of-the-parts (SOTP) valuation but is becoming less relevant given their materially higher fire risk compared to Sempra Energy.

Furthermore, Smith expects Sempra Energy's Texas capex plan to stay in place despite the opposition to transmission and data centers, though delays to 765-kV projects are likely. While a full recovery for SRE stock will need a clearer outcome from the Texas legislative session, Smith believes that the stock may already be an early opportunity for patient investors.

"We are early on the upgrade but see limited downside at current levels as the market is pricing in little transmission upside," said Smith.

Smith ranks No. 945 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 61% of the time, delivering an average return of 8.2%. See Sempra ETF Exposure on TipRanks.

#2949. Top Wall Street analysts find these 3 stocks attractive as long-term investments

Investors have been grappling with stock market volatility, weighing elevated oil prices, persistent geopolitical tensions, the Federal Reserve's rate hike and a debate about AI safety. For all that, it helps to look beyond short-term noise and focus on stocks with strong long-term growth potential.

Top Wall Street analysts' recommendations can help investors gain clarity, as these experts conduct in-depth research to pick stocks with attractive growth prospects.

Here are three stocks favored by some of Wall Street's top pros, according to TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

Enterprise software and cloud infrastructure company Oracle (ORCL) recently announced better-than-expected results for the first quarter of fiscal 2027. The quarter benefited from solid growth in cloud infrastructure revenue.

After hosting a series of investor meetings with Ken Bond, Oracle head of investor relations following Q1 earnings, Guggenheim analyst John DiFucci reiterated a buy rating on the stock with a price target of $400. Oracle remains a Guggenheim Best Idea, he said.

"We continue to believe ORCL is a decade stock, driven by the massive (and profitable) opportunity ahead for AI training and inferencing," said DiFucci. He also cited the traditional Public Cloud business and additional AI-enabled cloud database and cloud applications for his bullish stance. DiFucci expects all the factors to drive revenue and profit acceleration over the next few years.

While Oracle delivered 850 MW of data center capacity in Q1 FY27, Bond cautioned that Infrastructure as a Service revenue in any quarter depends more on the timing of when that capacity comes online, than on the headline MW number. DiFucci expects IaaS growth to stay strong, with full-year growth expected above Q1 FY27's 120% constant currency rate. Management expects total revenue growth to accelerate sequentially throughout fiscal 2027, as the faster-growing infrastructure business makes up a larger share of the mix.

DiFucci addressed concerns regarding customer concentration, saying OpenAI's share of total remaining performance obligations has dropped from 80% last June to about 50% now, and is expected to keep falling as new contracts are signed with new and existing customers.

DiFucci ranks No. 263 among more than 12,490 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 62% of the time, delivering an average return of about 17.5%. See Oracle Insider Trading Activity on TipRanks.

Space systems and launch services provider Rocket Lab (RKLB) was recently initiated as a buy with an $80 price target by Raymond James analyst Brian Gesuale.

"RKLB has assembled launch, spacecraft, components, payloads and optical communications, with Neutron and Iridium completing medium-lift and applications/spectrum in 2027," said Gesuale.

The 5-star analyst highlighted that with the completion of the Iridium acquisition, Rocket Lab will become the only vertically integrated space company with positive free cash flow (FCF). The deal will add a deployed constellation valued at more than $3 billion, globally coordinated L-band spectrum and about $500 million in earnings before interest, taxes, depreciation, and amortization (EBITDA) and $300 million in free cash flow (FCF).

Gesuale highlighted RKLB's strong revenue and backlog growth and progress toward positive EBITDA and FCF in 2027/2028. Rocket Lab is moving from a heavy investment phase into monetization, with estimated revenue growth of 59% in 2026, the analyst said, noting that RKLB's backlog has more than doubled to almost $2.4 billion from $1.1 billion in Q3 2025.

Given strong growth projections, the analyst expects RKLB's gross margins to expand from about a 35% trough in 2027 to about 47% by 2030, while R&D spending is estimated to decline from ~35% of sales in 2026 to about 10% in 2028, creating substantial operating leverage as the platform matures.

Gesuale ranks No. 369 among more than 12,490 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 63% of the time, delivering an average return of about 17%. See Rocket Lab Statistics on TipRanks.

J.P. Morgan analyst Doug Anmuth recently upgraded Meta Platforms (META) to buy from hold and raised his price target to $820 from $640. Despite the year-to-date underperformance of META, the 5-star analyst still sees significant upside, as Meta is in the early stages of launching frontier models and AI-powered offerings beyond advertising, particularly the Muse AI agent and Meta Model API access.

"Frontier models are at the core of Meta's product and monetization pipeline over a multi-year period, and its path toward superintelligence," said Anmuth.

In the summer of 2025, Meta set a goal to rebuild its Meta Superintelligence Labs team and deliver frontier AI models within a year, the analyst said. The team appears to have met its goal, rapidly advancing from the rollout of Muse Spark 1.1 in July to Muse Spark 1.3, which is competitive with Claude and GPT models, Anmuth said.

Meanwhile, Meta's Watermelon product will expand opportunities in consumer products, business intelligence, and Family of Apps engagement, the analyst said. The product will enhance internal operations and overall efficiency, said Anmuth, who's confident of Meta's ability to bring consumer-driven AI products to its extensive user base of 4 billion and views its distribution network as a competitive advantage.

While Meta has focused on frontier models and AI product development, Anmuth also sees solid opportunity in its AI infrastructure capacity, which he believes will support future compute needs.

Anmuth ranks No. 832 among more than 12,490 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 57% of the time, delivering an average return of about 9.40%. See Meta Platforms Financials on TipRanks.

#2947. NuScale Earns More on Interest Than It Does on Developing Reactors. Here’s the Math.

NuScale needs to get to the commercialization stage, pronto.

By Thomas Niel – Nov 25, 2026 at 1:45PM EST

10 stocks we like better than NuScale Power ›

NuScale Power (SMR -1.21%) can still brag that it has the only small modular reactor (SMR) design approved by the Nuclear Regulatory Commission. Still, it's not as if this achievement has given way to NuScale entering the commercialization stage.

In fact, as it stands now, the early-stage nuclear technology company is earning more from interest on bank deposits than from reactors. This needs to change, fast. Otherwise, the stock, already down nearly 82% over the past year, could plunge further.

Take a look at NuScale's latest quarterly results, and one could make the argument, albeit a stretch, that this stock is basically a certificate of deposit (CD) that happens to own an unprofitable nuclear start-up. That is, last quarter, the company generated just $281,000 in revenue from its operating business. Meanwhile, the company reported $19.1 million in investment income, aka income generated from its $933 million in reported cash and short-term investments.

This interest income is hardly a silver bullet for NuScale. While it may have generated nearly 68 times as much from its cash as it did from its operating activities, the $19.1 million only partially covered NuScale's $64.8 million in operating losses during the quarter. Worse yet, as cash burn depletes its war chest, barring another capital raise, investment income will undoubtedly decline in the coming quarters.

The exclusive commercialization partnership with ENTRA1 Energy is a major hurdle for NuScale. ENTRA1 may have entered a collaboration agreement with the Tennessee Valley Authority to supply it with SMR-generated nuclear power, but the agreement is not yet binding. Add in other hurdles, and it may not be until the 2030s that this deal yields significant revenue for NuScale Power.

Barring faster-than-expected progress with this catalyst, or the emergence of new commercial partnerships, consider it best to stay away from this stock. It may differ from other nuclear energy stocks, but with NuScale, the risk/reward proposition remains unfavorable.

#2943. Top Wall Street analysts like these 3 dividend stocks for steady income

Stock markets remain volatile as investors weigh uncertainty in the Middle East, rising interest rates and concerns about the artificial intelligence (AI) trade.

Given this volatile backdrop, investors seeking a stable income stream can enhance their portfolios with the addition of dividend stocks. In this regard, tracking recommendations of top Wall Street analysts can help shortlist attractive dividend stocks that are backed by strong financials.

Here are three dividend-paying stocks that are highlighted by Wall Street's top pros, as tracked by TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

Integrated oil and gas giant Chevron (CVX) is the first dividend pick. Earlier this month, the company paid a quarterly dividend of $1.78 per share. At an annualized dividend of $7.12 per share, CVX offers a dividend yield of about 3.5%.

After a meeting with senior leadership, Goldman Sachs analyst Neil Mehta reiterated a buy rating on CVX and raised his price target to $240 from $225. Among the key takeaways Mehta emphasized was that Chevron is building a solid pipeline of promising international exploration projects across Latin America, the Middle East, West Africa and the Eastern Mediterranean. In particular, he mentioned the focus on Venezuela, where Chevron expects to more than double gross production to 600 kbd from about 280 kbd by 2031, using three joint ventures.

Mehta also noted Chevron's efforts to deploy new technologies, especially in shale and tight-oil operations. CVX is focused on boosting productivity and efficiency to maximize free cash flow, he added.

Initiatives such as artificial lift optimization, AI and machine learning, and advanced chemical treatments are expected to improve recovery by about 10% in new wells. Consequently, capital spending per barrel in U.S. shale is projected to fall 25% in 2026, with total Permian spending dropping below $3.5 billion.

Mehta also said that Chevron sees power as a growth opportunity and is using its U.S. natural gas portfolio, key equipment and partnerships to meet rising demand. Notably, Chevron recently signed a 20-year power purchase deal with Microsoft (MSFT) to supply 2.67 GW of behind-the-meter capacity. This project, called Kilby, is expected to see its first power delivery in 2028 and generate mid-teens returns and long-term cash flows.

Mehta ranks No. 536 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 61% of the time, delivering an average return of 11.3%. See Chevron Statistics on TipRanks.

Enterprise Products Partners (EPD) is a master limited partnership (MLP) that provides midstream energy services to producers and consumers of natural gas, natural gas liquids (NGLs), crude oil, refined products and petrochemicals. With a quarterly cash distribution of 56 cents per common unit ($2.24 per common unit annualized), EPD offers a yield of about 6%.

Following a recent call with the company, RBC Capital analyst Elvira Scotto reiterated a buy rating on EPD with a price target of $42. The analyst slightly lowered her estimates for the second half of 2026, citing margin and volume normalization, though demand dynamics remain strong.

While the 5-star analyst expects a sequential decline due to normal seasonality and normalization following high spreads in the first half of the year, she still expects EPD to report another solid quarter (Q3 2026) and enter 2027 with a favorable setup. Ahead of Q3 earnings, Scotto expects investors to focus on export demand durability, EPD's ability to profit from spot cargo sales and growth in Permian volumes.

Scotto continues to expect $150 million of common unit buybacks per quarter in the second half of 2026. She expects buybacks to increase to $200 million per quarter in 2027, as capex spending declines and free cash flow improves.

In total, Scotto is bullish on EPD and views it as a core MLP holding that has both offensive and defensive characteristics. The analyst praised EPD's diverse asset base as ensuring stable cash flows. "Furthermore, the partnership's multi-year organic growth backlog helps provide visibility on long-term distribution growth," Scotto said.

Scotto ranks No. 199 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. Her ratings have been successful 67% of the time, delivering an average return of 15.9%. See Enterprise Products Partners Financials on TipRanks.

Brookfield Infrastructure Partners (BIP) operates a diversified portfolio of high-quality utilities, transport, midstream and data assets. The company declared a quarterly distribution of 45.5 cents per unit, payable on September 29. At an annualized distribution of $1.82, BIP offers a yield of 5.2%.

"Layering in the benefits from the proposed corporate simplification, a multiple well below historical levels and an attractive yield, we believe BIP offers a compelling risk/reward and it remains a preferred idea," said Dodge.

With more organic growth projects in the pipeline and new partnership investment opportunities, Dodge believes that BIP has clearer visibility into where it will deploy capital. That should support continued double-digit growth in funds from operations (FFO) per unit.

Dodge said the semiconductor foundries that BIP is developing with Intel (INTC) are expected to be fully commissioned by the end of 2026, with returns improving through Q4 2026. Dodge expects the joint venture to boost FFO by 300-400 basis points.

#2941. David Tepper Sold All of His AMD Stock. His Biggest Position Is Micron.

AMD is pricey, while Micron is dirt cheap.

By Keithen Drury – Nov 25, 2026 at 2:00PM EST

10 stocks we like better than Micron Technology ›

There has been a ton of news in the stock market recently. Nvidia is making headlines with its CEO controversy and third-quarter earnings. Newly public Anthropic went public with a lot of fanfare. However, there was another event that occurred this week that flew under the radar of most investors: 13-F releases.

While looking at Security and Exchange Commission (SEC) documents may sound a tad boring, these documents reveal what trades billionaire hedge fund managers made in the third quarter. Those are valuable pieces of information, as they show what the smart money is doing.

One billionaire that I follow is David Tepper and his fund, Appaloosa Management. Tepper made some interesting moves during Q3. The most noteworthy was loading up on Micron Technology (MU -4.83%) shares while completely exiting his Advanced Micro Devices (AMD -1.46%) position.

That's quite the call, but is it the right one?

David Tepper. Image source: Getty Images.

Information available from 13-Fs naturally lags the market. The SEC requires funds with over $100 million in investments to disclose their end-of-quarter holdings and make those public 45 days after the quarter ends. This gives some buffer between filing and release date, which protects the fund's investments.

However, investors need to be careful not to follow these documents too closely, as the landscape may have shifted from the end of the quarter to now.

AMD is in that boat, because it has lost about 20% of its value since Q3 ended. That justifies Tepper's move and makes it appear that his fund took gains before a sell-off occurred. The reason? Likely AMD's valuation.

AMD's stock has had a great year this year, but not all of that success has come from business growth. Instead, the market has chosen to give AMD a far higher premium this year than it has in the past. Reaching about 35 times next year's earnings at the end of Q3, it signaled that it was time to sell the stock.

AMD PE Ratio (Forward 1y) data by YCharts

Tepper did exactly that, and I think that was a smart move, because there was just too much hype baked into AMD's stock price. Buying Micron was an interesting pivot, and I think it may turn out to be the right move.

Pivoting from AMD to Micron is a huge change in mindset. While AMD is potentially overvalued, Micron is very cheap. It trades for just 3.9 times fiscal-year 2027 earnings.

That's a very cheap price tag, and it conveys that the market thinks something is wrong with the company. However, that's far from the case.

Micron operates in the memory chip industry, which has a history of being cyclical. The current artificial intelligence (AI)-driven cycle is massive and unlike anything investors have ever seen, and will eventually come to an end. The market is worried about it coming to an end sooner rather than later.

However, memory chip companies have sold out production capacity for years in advance, and Micron's management team told investors during its latest conference call that the memory chip market conditions will be worse in 2027 and 2028 than they were in 2026, meaning that prices will stay elevated, boosting Micron's stock.

This is likely why Tepper invested in Micron, as he sees upside when the rest of the market is bearish. Tepper is so bullish on the stock that he has made it his largest position, accounting for about 17.5% of his portfolio. That's a very concentrated position, but if Micron can see its valuation expand to more reasonable levels, Tepper will make a huge profit from his Micron shares.

If you're an AMD shareholder, I think the right move is to follow in Tepper's footsteps and roll the profits from that investment into Micron, as it could be due for a huge run over the next few years as the market realizes that the AI buildup isn't slowing down anytime soon.

#2940. Top Wall Street analysts are upbeat about the prospects for these 3 stocks

September ended on a mixed note, with the S&P 500 and Dow Jones Industrial Average falling 0.5% and 4.3%, respectively, while the Nasdaq Composite climbed 1.9%. Markets remained on edge with higher oil prices and Treasury yields weighing on investor sentiment and triggering fears of additional interest rate hikes.

In this climate, investors with a long-term horizon can track recommendations from top Wall Street analysts to find stocks with solid growth prospects.

Here are three stocks favored by some of Wall Street's top pros, according to TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

Neocloud company CoreWeave (CRWV) is benefiting from robust AI-driven demand for cloud computing.

Recently, JPMorgan analyst Samik Chatterjee upgraded CoreWeave to buy from hold, raising his price target to $125 from $120. The analyst cited favorable pricing, driven by solid demand, for the upgrade.

Chatterjee said CoreWeave is now more willing to sign premium priced, shorter-term contracts, which lift his revenue and margin estimates. CRWV stock performance has been underwhelming as investors worry about high capital spending, the analyst noted. That said, he believes that higher prices and increased margins will more than offset the rise in debt to fund capital spending, improving the stock's valuation.

Chatterjee noted the 25% price hike in July across CoreWeave's product offerings and frequent price increases by rivals like Nebius (NBIS), adding that some peers are charging nearly three times more for short-term compute contracts than what CoreWeave charges on longer-term deals.

"CoreWeave's leverage of the opportunity [is] evident in its recent press release highlighting contracts signed in F3Q at ~$40M/MW," said Chatterjee. JPMorgan said higher pricing is already contributing 5-10 percentage points to contract margins versus earlier deals.

Chatterjee ranks No. 4 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 75% of the time, delivering an average return of 55.3%. See CoreWeave Statistics on TipRanks.

Palo Alto Networks (PANW) is a provider of cybersecurity solutions across network, cloud, security operations, AI and identity. Following a discussion with management, BTIG analyst Gray Powell reiterated a buy rating on PANW and raised his price target to $425 from $404.

After a review of Palo Alto's product-level growth catalysts, Powell sees clear upside to both revenue and next-generation security (NGS) annual recurring revenue (ARR) in fiscal 2027. He expects PANW to deliver pro forma revenue growth of more than 17%, above the consensus estimate of 14.6%, and NGS ARR growth of more than 26% versus the Street's expectation of 24.5%.

Over the past five years, the Palo Alto business mix, has moved to higher-growth product areas and new markets. from what was earlier viewed as a hardware-centric network security company, Powell said. The 5-star analyst estimates that hardware firewalls and attached subscriptions now account for less than 50% of revenue.

Meanwhile, high-growth solutions, including Prisma SASE, software firewalls, Cortex XSIAM, Chronosphere and Idira now represent more than 35% of PANW's revenue. "Investors may not fully realize the growth potential within PANW's platform story," Powell said.

BTIG is optimistic about the prospects of Chronosphere in observability, CyberArk in identity and software firewalls and Prisma AIRS in the network and AI security business.

Powell ranks No. 320 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 61% of the time, delivering an average return of 17.3%. See Palo Alto Networks KPIs on TipRanks.

In a research note on September 30, Rosenblatt analyst Scott Devitt reiterated a buy rating on e-commerce and cloud computing platform Amazon (AMZN), raising his price target to $360 from $335. Fears that agentic commerce poses a major threat to Amazon's advertising business seem "overstated," he said.

Devitt noted that the growing adoption of personal agents and AI shopping assistants has sparked concern about displacement of Amazon's retail media business. But the 5-star analyst remains bullish, given Amazon's ability to adapt to changing consumer behavior, as it's done in the past.

Additionally, Devitt argues that even if an AI agent helps a shopper discover a product, Amazon can still handle the purchase and delivery, keeping much of the transaction value. He highlighted that management believes that existing Amazon shoppers will likely continue to start their shopping journey on the platform because it has better personalization, accurate pricing and inventory information that third-party agents still lack.

Overall, Devitt expects Amazon's retail business to prove a net beneficiary of agentic commerce. Fewer clicks would not hurt the company if the value of each remaining click and overall volumes keep rising, he said. The argument is that even if Amazon loses some sponsored ad revenue over the next few years, it would likely be only a small part of the company's total business and could be offset by growth in other areas.

"Consistent with our AI framework, we continue to view Amazon as one of the most resilient leaders in our coverage, combining owned compute infrastructure with a physical fulfillment moat," said Devitt. His bullish view is supported, Devitt said, by expectations that Amazon Web Services (AWS) will exit the year at a more than 45% growth rate with continued margin improvement.

Devitt ranks No. 765 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 49% of the time, delivering an average return of 12%. See Amazon Ownership Structure on TipRanks.

#2934. Goldman Sachs Sees 52 More GW of Data Center Power in 2028. These 3 Stocks Look Best Positioned to Cash In.

A $50 billion AI data center is worthless without one basic thing, and these stocks supply it.

By Neha Chamaria – Nov 25, 2026 at 2:06PM EST

10 stocks we like better than Bloom Energy ›

The artificial intelligence (AI) boom is rapidly running into a hard physical constraint: electricity. In a research note cited by Reuters, Goldman Sachs (GS -0.62%) projected that up to 52 gigawatts (GW) of additional data center power capacity could come online in the U.S. through 2028.

Goldman noted that U.S. data center power capacity and demand have already climbed nearly 30% year-to-date, accounting for more than one-third of the country's total electricity demand growth. It expects natural gas consumption to rise and is bullish on gas prices.

What's fueling this demand, and which stocks could win?

AI data centers need 24/7 stable, uninterrupted power, even when the sun sets or the wind dies down. That gives an edge to power sources that can run on demand, and natural gas is the biggest of them.

In 2025, natural gas made up 40% of U.S. utility-scale generating capacity, compared with 31.5% for all renewables combined and 13.3% for coal, according to the U.S. Energy Information Administration. When Texas set its all-time demand record on July 22, 2026, gas supplied 48% of the power and solar supplied 32%.

Speed matters too, as hyperscalers and data center operators can't wait years for a grid connection. Delays are costly. At an event in October, Nvidia (NVDA -3.94%) CEO Jensen Huang estimated that a 1-gigawatt AI data center can generate around $40 billion a year in revenue, and a three-month delay pushes back roughly $10 billion in revenue.

That urgency makes reliable fuel and fast power delivery critical, pushing developers toward natural gas. Three stocks could cash in, as they span the entire power value chain.

Energy Transfer (ET +0.75%), which sits along major natural gas corridors supplying power generation and industrial demand. It owns and operates nearly 140,000 miles of pipeline infrastructure, connecting major producing basins and markets.

Since the start of 2025, Energy Transfer has signed more than 9.5 million MMBtu (one million British thermal units) per day of agreements with hyperscalers, data centers, and power plants. These include agreements with Oracle (ORCL -5.14%) for three data centers, two of which are in Texas.

The company says it is in "advanced negotiations" with multiple customers to supply natural gas along its pipeline network, which includes Texas, Arkansas, Oklahoma, Ohio, Louisiana, and Illinois. These are also among the largest data center markets today.

Its new Hugh Brinson Pipeline entered full service in September ahead of schedule and carries Permian gas about 400 miles to the Dallas-Fort Worth area.

Energy Transfer earns primarily under long-term fee-based agreements and pays a steady, growing dividend. The stock yields 7.3%.

Bloom Energy (BE -1.98%) directly targets the power bottleneck by making solid-oxide fuel cells that provide modular, on-site power at facilities. Its fuel cells generally run on natural gas today, and growth has been explosive.

Third-quarter revenue surged 138.1% to a record $1.2 billion, with fuel cell system sales surpassing $1 billion in a single quarter for the first time. Bloom raised full-year 2026 revenue guidance for the third time in the year, projecting 122% growth. Management didn't give backlog numbers, but said it is growing faster than revenue.

Bloom Energy has secured massive partnerships, including a $25 billion financing deal with global asset management giant Brookfield Asset Management (BAM +2.60%).

GE Vernova (GEV -0.84%) makes large gas turbines, and almost 25% of all the electricity generated globally passes through its equipment. It also makes high-voltage transformers, switchgear, and control systems needed to connect data centers and buildings with the transmission lines.

While the industry lead times for some of these equipment now stretch into years, turbine buyers are paying up to book factory slots through 2032. Its latest agreement to acquire Trench Group for $9 billion is a calculated move to vertically integrate and secure parts supplies for transformers and other equipment.

Backlog hit $197 billion in the third quarter of fiscal year 2026. Until months ago, the company expected to hit $200 billion in backlog in 2028. Scarcity is giving the company incredible pricing power.

CEO Scott Strazik said full-year 2026 order pricing per kilowatt is on track to rise 50% from last year. With higher prices and volumes, you should expect bigger sales, margins, cash flows, and returns on capital.

#2932. Abercrombie & Fitch Grew Sales for the 16th Straight Quarter as Earnings Jumped 62%. Here's What's Behind the Streak.

The company effectively serves a key customer demographic.

By Eric Volkman – Nov 25, 2026 at 2:16PM EST

10 stocks we like better than Abercrombie & Fitch ›

Abercrombie & Fitch (ANF +5.30%) set a new record and extended an impressive winning streak in its third quarter. So it was little wonder that investors couldn't wait to pounce on the stock following the earnings announcement Tuesday morning. On the back of that demand, it closed the trading day nearly 20% higher in price.

While not every one of the quarter's many growth figures was as remarkable as it looked, there's no denying the power of the aforementioned streak. Read on for more about the clothing retailer's memorable third frame, and how it extended that already-long run.

The new all-time high Abercrombie set was third-quarter net sales, which in the 2026 version rocketed 11% higher year over year to $1.4 billion. It didn't hesitate to mention that this was no less than the 16th consecutive quarter of growth. This was powered by both of the company's leading brand families, its namesake Abercrombie & Fitch, whose sales rose by 12%, and Hollister, up 11%. Meanwhile, the company's overall comparable sales notched an impressive, though smaller, 6% gain.

Equity analysts had underestimated Abercrombie. On average, they were modeling net sales of $1.37 billion.

As for profitability, attributable net income climbed by a meaty 45% to over $164 million, or $3.82 per share. The company emphasized that the latter figure represented a 62% year-over-year increase. Yet some of this was due to a sharp reduction in share count; the weighted-average basic shares outstanding figure for the quarter was almost 42.3 million, compared with third-quarter 2025's 46.8 million-plus. That's because Abercrombie replenished its stock repurchase program last March, with its board authorizing a massive $1.3 billion for the purpose. Ever since, it has been steadily executing on that initiative.

Another factor to consider when examining the company's bottom line is the impact of tariff refunds. During the quarter, it was reimbursed by the federal government for duties it paid under a set of 2025 tariffs that were later ruled illegal by the Supreme Court. This added $0.40 to the company's per-share net income figure. Adjusting profitability by that amount reduces it to $3.42, bringing the year-over-year improvement down to "only" 45%.

That figure still crushes the consensus analyst projection of $3.09. In other words, while tariffs made a bit of difference to profitability, Abercrombie did smashingly well in the quarter regardless.

Abercrombie's fashion is, as ever, geared toward younger consumers. It blankets that demographic rather well, with the Abercrombie & Fitch brand group targeting older, theoretically more choosy and sophisticated customers, and the relatively casual Hollister lines aimed at their younger counterparts.

Both manage to stay in fashion and on the wish list of such people, buttressed by clever marketing. When a clothing company's wares are in vogue, it has less of a need to discount them (a common practice in the business). In its earnings release, Abercrombie also pointed to good discipline in its inventory management as a secret to its third-quarter success. Hence the double-digit improvements in both sales and profitability.

The timing of this is fortuitous, because the consumer discretionary sector is entering the eternally important holiday season. With its considerable growth momentum, the company felt confident enough to raise its annual guidance for the second quarter in a row.

It's now modeling sales growth of 6% over the 2025 result, up from the preceding prediction of 5%. EPS should come in at $13.70 to $13.90, encouragingly above the former range of $13.10 to $13.60. Management maintained its projection for the impact of tariff refunds; these should add 220 basis points to its operating margin. Guidance for yearly spend on those share-count-reducing stock buybacks was also kept level, at $500 million.

Sales growth streaks are hard to achieve and often harder to maintain. That goes double for the ever-fickle retail fashion industry. But if any company's going to keep one alive, it's Abercrombie, which always seems to have its finger on the pulse of what its youthful customers like to wear. I'd be a buyer of this stock, particularly considering its modest forward P/E of 12.5, even after Tuesday's monster pop.

#2931. Recap: Wizards fall to the Celtics, 131-116

WASHINGTON, DC – NOVEMBER 24: AJ Dybantsa #4 of the Washington Wizards plays defense during the game against the Boston Celtics as part of the 2026 Emirates NBA Cup on November 24, 2026 at Capital One Arena in Washington, DC. NOTE TO USER: User expressly acknowledges and agrees that, by downloading and or using this Photograph, user is consenting to the terms and conditions of the Getty Images License Agreement. Mandatory Copyright Notice: Copyright 2026 NBAE (Photo by Stephen Gosling/NBAE via Getty Images) | NBAE via Getty Images

This one started out ugly and it got ugly in a fast way. As mentioned during the broadcast, the Wizards had perhaps the biggest spotlight on their team since this young group has been assembled and this was a chance to potentially advanced to the semifinals of the NBA Cup for the first time as a franchise.

With all of this in mind, the team was flat to start this. The defense was porous, giving up 80 points in the first half, versus a team that had just scored 87 points for an entire game the last time they were on the floor. Celtics led by as many as 34 points in this one and walked into the locker at halftime up, 80-49.

For the half, the Celtics shot 15 for 26 behind the arc. Too many open shots for a team, struggling to generate consistent offense, is not a recipe for winning.

Fortunately, the Wizards woke up in the 2nd half and made a game of it. The Wizards were able to get as close as seven points, but then the Celtics hit back to back three towards the end of the 4th quarter to put the Wizards out of it.

Besides the poor overall team effort, AJ Dybansta (20 points, 11 rebounds) and Kyshawn George (24 points, 9 rebounds) both had good games. There were some encouraging parts to this one especially in the 2nd half, but the Wizards dug themselves too big of a hole to climb out of and now their 3-game winning streak has been snapped.

#2926. Walmart+ Members Spend 4 Times As Much As Non-Members. The Retailer’s New $120 Bundle Could Unlock More Growth.

The perks just became sweet enough that one of its businesses could begin stealing a rival’s coveted customers.

By James Brumley – Nov 25, 2026 at 2:30PM EST

For most of the industry's existence, retailing was simply a matter of offering the right product at the right price in the right place at the right time. Shoppers would find your store, and gladly give you their money.

The internet has changed the increasingly competitive retailing landscape, however. People are empowered, with access to many choices. Simply opening a store's doors isn't enough. In this environment, the key is building an ecosystem that makes your stores the top-of-mind choice for meeting consumers' shopping needs.

Walmart's (WMT -0.22%) ecosystem just got a serious upgrade. Investors should take note, as it offers some much-needed growth potential.

In October, Walmart combined the benefits of Walmart+ (like free shipping) with membership in its club-based warehouse retailer Sam's Club. Subscriptions that individually would normally cost $98 and $60 per year, respectively, can now be combined for an annual cost of only $120.

The real opportunity with this offering, however, is a chance to poach some of the affluent paying members of Sam's Club's rival Costco Wholesale (COST -0.45%).

Walmart was already doing pretty well with this crowd, to be clear. With even higher-earning households feeling the pinch of lingering inflation, most of the company's market share gains since 2022 have come from America's more affluent households.

The proverbial prize here, however, is a portion of Costco's 84.1 million paid memberships. Costco's average member lives in a household with an average annual income of over $100,000. The typical Walmart customer's comparable income is just over half that amount.

Sam's Club alone isn't apt to encourage much defection. If Walmart can add value to its Sam's Club offering by adding the convenience of Walmart stores' wider and less-bulk inventory selection at an annual price in the ballpark of Costco's membership fees, it's got a chance of winning over at least some of these coveted higher-spending customers.

The average Costco member spends about $3,000 per year, by the way, versus roughly half that for Sam's Club members, and just a little more than that for the average Walmart store customer.

Most Costco members aren't apt to make such a switch, to be clear. However, it wouldn't take many defectors to make a noticeable difference in Walmart's top and bottom lines, now that paid access to both sets of perks is affordably accessible.

#2924. Qualys: 3 Key Takeaways for Long-Term Investors

Automated patching is turning Qualys's platform bet into a magnet for customer spending, proving enterprises will pay for fixes at scale.

By The Motley Fool Team – Nov 25, 2026 at 2:34PM EST

On its third-quarter 2026 earnings call, held on Nov. 9, 2026, Qualys (QLYS +2.24%) reported that artificial intelligence threats are accelerating customer demand for automated risk remediation across corporate networks. Enterprise buyers are moving away from passive vulnerability scanning toward platforms that patch security exposures automatically.

That shift expands the software spending Qualys can capture per enterprise over time. Three things management said on the earnings call stand out.

Traditional Continuous Threat Exposure Management, or CTEM, creates excess operational alerts without resolving underlying system flaws. Automated remediation solves that operational bottleneck by running security fixes without human intervention.

Large government customers are replacing fragmented security products with Qualys's Enterprise TruRisk Management, or ETM, to unify threat detection and automated patching under single contracts. Flexible subscription commitments through QFlex give enterprise buyers room to expand usage over time.

Third-party resellers and managed security providers now generate over half of total revenue as direct enterprise sales face modest friction. International expansion leads overall growth, supported by global partner integrations.

Enterprise security adoption accelerates as IT teams replace manual tracking with continuous automated remediation. Deep partner integrations and automated patch verification reinforce market positioning while management tracks enterprise billing discipline.

Federal agency contracts validate expanding multi-module customer commitments across public sector workflows.

#2921. Kawhi Leonard is back on the (practice) court: will his return be enough?

The Raptors have released another brief statement outlining Kawhi Leonard's return to playing NBA basketball, and while there are positive signs emerging, little can be confirmed about when the forward will be back on the court.

"He has begun individual on-court workouts following treatment to address left knee swelling," stated the press release issued earlier today, November 24th. Leonard has not seen the court for more than one month, last having suited up in game two for the Raptors on October 23rd in a win for Toronto against the Washington Wizards.

Without Kawhi, the Raptors have been in free-fall, punctuated by a six-game losing streak earlier in November. Some of these were close, against good teams; the Raptors would develop a lead, then give it away in the second half, breaking the hearts of Toronto fans who tuned in to watch. With his clutch genes and scoring prowess, Leonard's presence on the team would have been sure to change the outcome of some of these games, but the lack of his presence has revealed both flaws, and upsides with this roster.

The Raptors roster, as currently constructed, is not that different from what it was last year. Regardless of having Leonard or Brandon Ingram at small-forward, these two players were signed for the same job: take offensive responsibilities off of Scottie Barnes' shoulders so that he can focus on playmaking and defence.

All together, a sound idea for roster construction. But the state of the starters around the pairs in both years? Less than ideal. The Raptors are currently ranked 24th in three-pointers made, and are shooting 33.8% from deep this season. A big reason for this is because this year, 3 of 5 Raptors starers are not considered good three point shooters, those being Scottie Barnes, Jakob Poeltl, and RJ Barrett, while the two perceived marksmen, Immanuel Quickley, and Kawhi Leonard, have both been unreliable.

While the Raptors, ironically, have quite a few good three point shooting players or prospects coming off their bench, the Raps' starting lineup, the players who they have committed the most money to, are either non-shooters, in a shooting slump, or injured. While a lack of clutch play and poor defence is also certainly to blame for the Raptors' losses, being able to buy a 3 certainly would help the Raptors, especially in the fourth quarter of games.

It's not 2006 anymore, and the Raptors need to get with the times. Giving Ja'Kobe Walter more minutes is a step in the right direction, but with his certain relegation to the bench when Kawhi Leonard returns, the Raptors' rotations need to consistently sport players who can make long range shots.

As a whole, the Raptors have added Leonard to a problematic roster which in many respects, he was meant to, and seemed potentially able to fix. But, succeeding with Leonard always comes contingent with being able to survive the regular periods of absence he takes as a result of health issues. And the Raptors have shown themselves largely unable to do so.

One of the lone bright spots in the Raptors' season thus far has been the ascension of Scottie Barnes to hitherto unforeseen levels. Barnes has been thrust into a primary offensive creation role and has taken to it unexpectedly well. As of November 24th, Barnes is averaging almost 25 points per game and a career high in steals. He's doing it playing an antiquated style of basketball, too, barely making any threes, and living off of midrange buckets.

Barnes has never had a higher profile, nor has he ever been more fun to watch on the offensive side of the ball.

Allen Graves, the Santa Clara rookie drafted 19th overall, has done a lot to increase his volume from three, and had his first signature game, with a 20 point, 10 rebound performance to help the Raptors put away the Detroit Pistons. While it's unlikely for that same level of play to extend the rest of the season, Graves has shown that he's capable of doing what he was drafted to do, and could be the next developmental success story for the Raptors.

Getting Leonard back to pair with Barnes, Graves, and Collin Murray-Boyles gives the Raptors a disgusting forward rotation to lean on heavily, and Leonard's offensive abilities will likely be able to drag the Raptors back into a positive win/loss ratio. But, ideally, Leonard should not be dragging dead weight behind him. The Raptors, as an organization, need to take account of the state of their team without Kawhi Leonard. He won't always be available to bail out this roster, and when he isn't, it is irresponsible coaching and management to allow the roster to go on to lose games in the manner that they are currently losing them in.

Leonard is not being paid to help rookies ascend and find their place in the NBA. This is team headlined by professional, veteran players, and Leonard or no-Leonard, they should look like it on the basketball court.

The Raptors will give more information on Kawhi's health in a week. Stay frosty.

#2920. 3 Glorious AI Stocks to Buy Hand Over Fist If the Stock Market Crashes

A crash would put these dominant businesses on sale, and one already looks cheap.

By John Ballard – Nov 25, 2026 at 2:42PM EST

Crashes may feel scary for investors, but they're also the best time to buy certain stocks. Companies that provide essential services, earn substantial profits, and have ample cash on their books can keep investing through downturns. The strongest companies often come out of a downturn stronger than they went in.

The stock market falls by at least 10% around once every 16 months, on average, according to Hartford Funds. The S&P 500 (^GSPC -0.20%) recently closed 11.6% below its Oct. 6 record high on Nov. 20 and currently sits about 8% below that high. The tech-heavy Nasdaq Composite, meanwhile, is still down by about 14% from its recent peak.

We could have already passed the bottom of one of those typical corrections, or we could still be near the start of a bigger decline: No one knows. But if the market falls further, Alphabet (GOOGL -2.11%)(GOOG -1.98%), Taiwan Semiconductor Manufacturing (TSM -1.90%), and Amazon (AMZN -3.14%) would be artificial intelligence (AI) stocks worth buying. Here's why.

Alphabet is the company behind Google Search, YouTube, and the Gemini AI model. Billions of people use its products. Search is how much of the world finds information online, and YouTube is a go-to destination for video. And the Gemini app now has 1 billion users.

That reach allows Alphabet to rake in substantial advertising revenue across its platforms. Google Services, which includes Search, YouTube, and subscriptions, generated $96 billion in revenue in the third quarter and earned about $0.41 of operating profit per dollar of sales. A recession might cause advertising spending to decline, slowing Alphabet's revenue growth. Still, the massive reach of its services business means the company should see its revenue march higher over the long term.

Moreover, AI appears to be enhancing its long-term growth prospects. Alphabet said Search queries grew at their fastest pace in five years last quarter. That matters because earlier in the generative AI revolution, many investors and analysts feared that users asking AI chatbots questions would lead to a reduction in traffic to Google Search.

The stock trades at about 18 times next year's earnings estimates, in line with the S&P 500, which also trades at about that ratio, while analysts expect Alphabet’s earnings to grow about 17% annually. If a broader market crash took the tech stock down with it, that would turn a market leader trading at a fair price into a bargain.

Taiwan Semiconductor Manufacturing (TSMC) is the world's leading contract chipmaker. Its foundries physically produce the chips designed by other companies, including almost all cutting-edge AI processors. When companies need the most advanced chips in large quantities, TSMC is the partner they turn to, because no rival has its massive production capacity or manufacturing expertise.

That position gives TSMC pricing power. Its operating margin was 59% last quarter, meaning it kept about $0.59 of every dollar of sales as operating profit. Management expects revenue to grow by about 44% in U.S. dollars this year as demand for AI chips continues to climb.

The biggest risk to the company is geopolitical. Most of TSMC's leading-edge factories are in Taiwan, and a conflict involving China could disrupt its chip production.

But that's why TSMC is working to expand its foundry operations geographically. Its new manufacturing facility in Arizona is expected to begin volume production in the third quarter of 2027. The company is also evaluating expansion in Texas, which would help mitigate this risk over the long term.

Trading at about 15.5 times next year's expected earnings, below the market's roughly 18 times, the stock looks undervalued. Analysts expect about 44% annualized earnings growth in the coming years. A further drop in the share price would make the stock a steal.

Amazon runs a massive online store, an advertising business, and Amazon Web Services (AWS), its cloud platform. It just posted its highest operating margin ever, nearly 14% in the third quarter. Cash from operations rose 19% year over year to $42 billion in Q3, and grew by 29% to $168 billion over the last 12 months.

Amazon Bedrock, the AWS service that lets businesses run AI models, saw token volume (a key measure of AI usage) grow nearly 17 times year over year. This makes Amazon one of the best stocks for the average investor to profit from enterprise AI adoption, since AWS generates most of Amazon's profit.

The stock's valuation is not as low as the others. At about 23 times 2027 earnings, it trades at a premium to the market, yet analysts expect earnings to grow roughly 23% annually. Softer demand for cloud services could send the stock lower. That's the risk, but a sluggish year of growth wouldn't derail the company's long-term trajectory.

Amazon's margins are trending higher, and rising cash from operations would let it keep investing in growth through a downturn. A market crash wouldn’t keep tens of millions of people from shopping on Amazon.com, nor would it stop enterprises from needing to store their data on AWS. It would only bring the stock's valuation down to more attractive levels, making it potentially a bargain for patient investors.

#2915. Here's How Much a $10,000 Investment in McDonald's Pays You Every Single Year

For McDonald's stock, steady, consistently growing dividends are just one part of the recipe.

By Thomas Niel – Nov 25, 2026 at 2:53PM EST

10 stocks we like better than McDonald's ›

After recently implementing its 50th consecutive annual dividend increase, McDonald's (MCD +1.00%) has finally reached Dividend King status. This milestone has arrived as shares in the fast-food giant attempt to emerge from a multi-month slump.

Yet while the stock's 2026 performance has proven disappointing for longtime investors, for those entering or adding to a position today, this is a highly favorable setup. Since it's still sporting a higher-than-usual forward dividend yield, while at the same time positioned to implement further moderate dividend increases, this remains a top blue chip stock to buy and hold for dividend growth.

Following the recent dividend hike, which raised the quarterly payout by 4% to $1.93 per share, McDonald's now pays $7.72 in cash dividends annually. The stock has recently traded for around $275 per share, so a $10,000 investment gets you around 36 shares. Multiply 36 by $7.72, and you'll have the total annual dividend income generated from this investment: $277.92.

That's a forward annual dividend yield of around 2.8%. However, this is far from the only long-term economic return you can receive from investing in McDonald's. Beyond the prospect of further dividend growth, much suggests that McDonald's will keep climbing out of its mid-2026 slump, toward past price levels, and on to new all-time highs.

In recent years, dividend growth for McDonald's shares has averaged in the mid-single-digit range. The latest dividend increase came in below this average, but if the company's NEXT-branded turnaround efforts prove successful, greater dividend growth could follow.

Success with a turnaround, including a growth resurgence, could drive a market rerating. McDonald’s shares trade for around 20 times forward earnings, while faster-growing consumer staples blue chips, like Coca-Cola, trade for over 25 times forward earnings. McDonald's still has its work cut out, but it has successfully overcome headwinds before. If it resolves this latest crop, it could serve investors the perfect recipe for strong total returns.
