#2913. BBVA Argentina (BBAR) Q3 2026 Earnings Call Transcript

Margins compressed as rates fell, yet fee income and market share kept growing.

By Motley Fool Transcribing – Nov 25, 2026 at 2:54PM EST

Need a quote from a Motley Fool analyst? Email [email protected]

BBVA Argentina (BBAR -1.97%) reported a sequential decline in quarterly profitability as rapid interest rate reductions compressed net interest margins. Management indicated that while the bank faced a more gradual evolution of loans and deposits than originally anticipated, it successfully gained market share in both categories. Year-to-date performance remains positive, supported by double-digit growth in fee income and effective expense control. The bank observed early signs of improvement in asset quality, particularly in retail delinquency, and maintains a capital position significantly above regulatory requirements to fund future lending activities as the economic cycle gains momentum.

Operator: Morning, everyone, and welcome to BBVA Argentina's third quarter 2026 results conference call. Today with us are Mrs. Belén Fourcade, Investor Relations Manager, Diego Cesarini, IRO and Head of Assets and Liability Management, and Carmen Morillo Arroyo, CFO. This presentation and the third quarter 2026 earnings release are available on BBVA Argentina's investor relations website, ir.bbva.com.ar, and will also be available for download in the chat. First of all, let me point out that some of the statements made during this conference call may be forward-looking statements with the meaning of the Safe Harbor provision found in Section 27A of the Securities Act of 1933 under U.S. Federal Securities Law.

These forward-looking statements are subject to risks and uncertainties that could cause results to differ materially from those expressed in the forward-looking statements. Additional information concerning these factors is contained in BBVA Argentina's annual report on Form 20-F for the fiscal year 2025 filed with the U.S. Securities and Exchange Commission. During the company's presentation, all microphones will be disabled. At this time, we are going to open it up for questions and answers. If you have a question, please raise hand for audio questions. You will then receive a request to activate your microphone. Please activate it and pick up your headset to provide optimum sound quality when posing your question.

I will now turn the call over to Belén Fourcade. Please, go ahead.

Belén Fourcade: Good morning, everyone, and thank you for joining us today for BBVA Argentina's third quarter 2026 results conference call. During the third quarter of 2026, the bank continued to operate in a dynamic macroeconomic environment marked by some short-term challenges, including a moderation in economic activity, inflation above initial expectations, and a more gradual evolution of loans and deposits than originally anticipated. Nevertheless, we remain confident in the medium and long-term outlook for the Argentine economy, supported by ongoing structural reforms and deregulation, solid financial and fiscal fundamentals, positive developments in the trade balance, and increasing investment opportunities.

We believe these factors are the laying foundations for a more stable, predictable, and sustainable economic environment, creating favorable conditions for the continued development and growth of our business. In this context, BBVA continues to strengthen its competitive positioning, consolidating its place as the second-largest private bank, leading financial intermediation growth among its main peers in a challenging credit quality landscape. Moving on to our financial highlights for the quarter, BBVA Argentina posted an inflation-adjusted net income of ARS 58.9 billion for the third quarter of 2026. This represents a 57.7% decrease quarter-over-quarter and a quarterly ROE of 4.9%.

The decline was mainly driven by a decrease in net interest margins stemmed from interest rates dropping compared to the first half of the year, which negatively affected asset repricing and also from lower yields on inflation-adjusted assets. As a consequence, NIMs declined from 18.2% to 15.1% in the quarter. It is worth mentioning that NIM adjusted by inflation cost, which in our view best reflects the evolution of margin over time, fell 260 basis points in the quarter to 12.2% and 10 basis points compared to 12 months ago, even with liabilities rate level approximately 2,000 basis points lower.

Regarding efficiency, our quarterly efficiency ratio stood at 49.6%, mainly affected by lower operating income, while personnel benefits and administrative expenses still proved a better performance. Nonetheless, year-to-date results show a positive overview, with a net income of nine months of ARS 294.4 billion, 14.4% above the same period in 2025. These figures arise from a 16% growth in net interest margin, a 17% improvement in net fee income, and a 6% reduction in personnel and administrative expenses, which, although largely offset by a 44% increase in loan loss allowances, lets us glimpse the potential of the business if credit situation continues to normalize as expected. Now, let's look at the dynamics of our balance sheet and credit portfolio.

Total financing to the private sector closed the quarter at ARS 18.4 trillion, increasing 2% quarter-over-quarter. While local currency loans increased 0.8%, our foreign currency private loans grew by 5.6% sequentially, equivalent to a 9.2% increase in hard currency. On the retail side, mortgage lending continues to gain momentum alongside pledge loans. Furthermore, we are successfully capturing business, mainly driven by the commercial segment and foreign currency loans. Our consolidated loan market share stood at 11.93%, signaling a total gain of 40 basis points over the last 12 months. On the funding side, total deposits reached ARS 19.8 trillion. Private deposit market share increased from 9.90% to 10.22% in the quarter, and up 14 basis points year-over-year.

In terms of asset quality, we are beginning to observe favorable signs in portfolio evolution, particularly in early delinquency indicators. This improvement is progressively reflected in lower loan loss allowances, which recorded a 5% decrease in real terms during the period, although there is still room for a more significant reduction. BBVA's NPL ratio at the end of September was 5.55%, a 54 basis point decrease quarter-over-quarter, while the system presented a ratio of 7.4% as of August, last available data as of the moment of this recording. Cost of risk shows a similar decline from 7.13% in the second quarter of 2026 to 6.58% in the third quarter of 2026.

Looking at solvency and liquidity, our liquidity ratio closed at a very comfortable 44.4%. More importantly, our capital position remains robust, with a regulatory capital ratio of 17.9%, representing 117.6% excess over minimum regulatory requirements. For subsequent periods, our expectation is to consolidate an upward path in profits once again, driven by progressive improvement in risk indicators and intermediation margins within a context of expense control. In this context, we continue to strengthen our capabilities to capture opportunities that arise when the economic cycle regains momentum, creating value for our stakeholders, clients, investors, and the country's productive development. Thank you for your time and continued support.

I would like now to turn the call over to Carmen Morillo, our CFO, for some closing remarks.

Carmen Morillo Arroyo: Hi, good morning, everyone. Thank you for joining the meeting. Before moving to the Q&A, let me share our view on the business and how we expect it to evolve. On business activity, despite the lower-than-expected credit demand, we grew our loan portfolio in real terms and strengthened our position in both loans and deposits. Over the last few years, we have achieved significant scale, with a market share of around 12% in lending and above 10% in deposits. We are now in a very good position to build on that scale. Our priority is to make the most of it by deepening customer relationships, increasing transactional activity, and improving profitability.

In retail, we continue to focus on secured lending, particularly mortgages and auto loans, and customers where we have greater visibility and repayment capability. On the corporate side, we see interesting opportunities in the more dynamic sectors of the economy and across their value chains. Our expectations for loan growth for this year is now more moderate, reflecting the slower recovery in demand. As economic activity and real incomes recover, we expect lending to gradually regain momentum, always with a focus on profitability and asset quality. Moving to margins, lower interest rates have put pressure on asset yields, but going forward, we expect margins to remain stable.

However, in Argentina, it is important to look at the margins and inflation together. As inflation comes down, the negative impact of inflation accounting in our results should also decrease, partially offsetting the pressure that lower interest rates have had on the net interest income. Our focus remains on pricing, funding mix, and business volumes. On fees, despite a weaker quarter due to some specific reasons, we have achieved solid growth of around 17% year-to-date in real terms compared to last year. Going forward, we want transactional activity to become an increasingly important source of revenues. On asset quality, we are starting to see the improvement in early arrears that we mentioned last quarter reflected in our NPL ratio.

We have also started to rebuild coverage. The encouraging point in that, the quality is our new origination is significantly better. We expect a gradual improvement in asset quality, and impairments will take longer to normalize. On efficiency, we continue to make progress on our cost base. Our priority is to make these savings sustainable. As activity recovers, we should also benefit from operating leverage. Finally, on profitability, we expect to close 2026 with a similar ROE to the one reported for the first nine months of the year. Looking into 2027, we see several factors that should support a progressive recovery: higher business volumes, a lower impact from inflation accounting, continued cost discipline, and a gradual normalization of impairments.

We remain cautious about how quickly these factors will materialize. What is important is that we are facing this environment with a stronger competitive position and with a capital and liquidity to support future profitability and growth. Our strategy has not changed, although the pace of recovery has. With that, we can move to your questions.

Operator: We will now open the floor for questions. If you have a question, please click on the raise hand button for audio questions. Our first question comes from Carlos T. Gomez-Lopez with HSBC. You can open a line.

Carlos T. Gomez-Lopez: Hello. Thank you very much, Carmen and team. My first question is on asset quality. You have shown an improvement, and your provisions have declined, but they seem to be declining a little bit more slowly than at other banks. Is it fair to say that your cycle is a little bit lagging the others? What type of cost of risk should we expect for the next year? If I may add, on the margin, the decline this quarter was very sudden, 250 basis points. Can you explain the factors? Is it just a delayed repricing of the assets, or there was something else that made this quarter particularly low? Thank you.

Carmen Morillo Arroyo: Thank you, Carlos. Let me take the first two questions regarding NPLs and cost of risk. Diego, if you--

Carmen Morillo Arroyo: --want to answer on NIM. First of all, Carlos, I would like to highlight that our NPL ratio has declined from 6% to 5.5% during this quarter, and we are sure that this path is going to be continued next quarter and during 2027. You were mentioning that we are lagging our peers, but I believe that our peers had much higher levels of NPLs and cost of risk as we have. The recovery will come from a lower level in our case, but we believe we will see that in the following quarters. Regarding NPLs, what we think is that ending the year below, around 5%, should be achievable. For next year, we will show a better NPL ratio.

Regarding the composition, maybe it is useful to highlight that during 2025 and 2026, the retail portfolio, as you know, is the one that has suffered the most. For next year, as I was mentioning, this portfolio needs to recover. Maybe we can see, on the other hand, the commercial portfolio, where we have a really low level, 0.7% NPL ratio. Maybe we can see some deterioration there, but nothing too high, just to mention. Then in terms of cost of risk, we see this 6.5% to be maintained during the fourth quarter. This is due to the way our models work.

We see this 6.5%, as I was saying, and for next year, a better performance is going to be seen due to the new vintages that are coming much better.

Diego Cesarini: Hi, Carlos. This is Diego. I will take the follow-on question. As you mentioned, NIMs have fallen 300 basis points. Let me first address the net interest income. It has also decreased around 10% in the quarter. As far as we have seen, it is the smaller decline across the industry, but anyway, it was expected, and it is the main cause of the decrease in total results compared with the previous quarter. Regarding NIMs, yes, they have fallen 300 basis points. We like to mention NIMs without the impact of inflation, considering that the decrease is around 250 basis points. That, anyway, is a huge decrease. There are two main explanations for this.

The first one is that, as interest rates fall, you know that they decrease suddenly by the end of the first quarter, when TAMAR rate went from the levels of around 35% to levels of 25%. In the second quarter, we saw the benefit of liabilities repricing faster than assets. The second quarter, in a way, showed a benefit that in the third quarter reverted as asset prices caught up with the liability rates. The second explanation is that all our assets that are adjusted by inflation, like bonds or mortgages, well, they accrue interest with a delay.

You know that in the first quarter of the year, inflation was high, especially in February, March, and April, so we caught also that benefit in the second quarter. That benefit, we did not see it in the third quarter. I think that this fall in NIMs was expected. It should have come sooner than it finally came. Last year, TAMAR, on average, was around 36%-37%. This year, TAMAR is coming at 24%-25%, so lower rates necessarily imply lower NIMs. For us, we think that, having said this, the good news is that we think that this decrease in margins is done for the moment.

We are not expecting an additional decrease in NIMs for next quarter, neither for next year. I think that is the explanation I can give you.

Carlos T. Gomez-Lopez: That's clear. Would you say that the TAMAR in the 26% level that we have today, which is below inflation, is a normal level, or should we expect a normalization of that up next year, unless there is a big decline in inflation? By the way, I do not think you have given us your economic assumptions for next year.

Diego Cesarini: TAMAR right now is running at a 24% level. It is in line with the average inflation of the last two, three, four months. For next year, we are expecting a similar level. Probably, if inflation goes down in the coming months, it could go a little lower, but of course, we have to bear in mind that next year we have elections, and we do not know how the central bank reaction could be if the peso is under attack. We have seen an episode of high volatility last year in the third quarter.

We are not expecting an episode of such a magnitude, but, well, we have to bear in mind that we could have a little more volatility than we have seen in the last four or five months. We are expecting for next year, regarding our economic data, we are expecting growth around 2.5%, inflation running at 22%, 23% level, and rates, as I said before, around 22%, 23% in average.

Carlos T. Gomez-Lopez: Thank you so much.

Operator: The next question comes from Lisandro Lloveras from one618. You can open a microphone, sir.

Lisandro Lloveras: Well, my question is about, I am seeing that the BBVA is trading right now below 0.7x price to book. I was wondering if--

Carmen Morillo Arroyo: Lisandro, we cannot hear you properly. I don't know if something with your micro.

Lisandro Lloveras: I see now that the BBVA is trading at 0.7x price to book, so I was wondering if you are considering to do a repurchase stock program from here. Thanks.

Carmen Morillo Arroyo: Thank you. Thank you, Lisandro. Yes, good question. You see, what we most take a look on is the free float we have, and when we talk with analysts and investors, what they usually tell us is that our free float is lower than peers, and that is like something negative for them. So we are comfortable at this level of free float that we currently have. So that should be the answer to your question.

Operator: The next question comes from Tiago Pinho from Bradesco. You can open your line.

Tiago Pinho: Hi, Carmen. Hi, Belén. Can you hear me?

Tiago Pinho: Okay, thank you. I just wanted to follow up on your economic projections for next year. I think if you could share the expected growth for Argentine economy, and how should that translate into loan growth in the peso and dollar book? Also, one question regarding the NIMs. As you said, you don't expect to see further decrease on NIMs, but maybe the impact on the UVA and inflation-linked bonds should come as inflation goes down. I just wanted to understand, and how should we expect the financial margin on these bonds as inflation comes down?

Carmen Morillo Arroyo: Okay. Let me answer. Yes, you were talking about 2027 on projections. Diego was already mentioning macro guidelines regarding GDP, inflation, and interest rates. In this context, and knowing that next year is an electoral year and many things can happen, but what we have as a base scenario is a growth in activity in terms of, in real terms, around 10% or similar to that level, we will see what happens with peso and dollar. It's difficult to say by now in that context. This should be a booster for the P&L for next year, activity.

Then you can also see that we will focus on expenses and on being more efficient, keeping the trend we are showing this year. Also these impairments that should go down by next year will also help, together with a lower hyperinflation adjustment. All in all, we are expecting a positive trend in our P&L and on our ROE for next year. That's all I can tell you by now.

Diego Cesarini: Yes, Tiago. Regarding the other part of your question about our portfolio and inflation, most of our portfolio of public sector bonds is either very short term, or that is around 1/3 of it, and the remaining 2/3 are dual bonds, which pay the better of inflation and TAMAR, or TAMAR bonds. We are not directly exposed, for example, to an environment where real rates are again as high as they were last year, because in that case, our bonds would pay the higher of the variables, which would be the TAMAR. Of course, mortgages are just inflation-related, so mortgages would suffer, but they still represent a fraction of our loan book, less than 10%.

They are around 8% of our loan book. So our exposure to a scenario where real rates are really high is not big. We would be probably in a better position than the average of the financial system.

Operator: The next question comes from Pedro Offenhenden from Latin Securities. You can open your line.

Pedro Offenhenden: Hello, Carmen, Diego, Belén. I wanted to follow up on this deal you were mentioning on the securities portfolio. I saw that the mark-to-market part grew like 80% in real terms, so I wanted to ask what drove that increase and if we should expect for next year to have more share of the bonds mark-to-market.

Diego Cesarini: Hi, Pedro. No, it will not change too much. Our mark-to-market position is small compared to our held-to-collect and sell position. Held-to-collect and sell should represent around 90%-95% in general terms. Mark-to-market are just trading positions that are kept for just very short periods of times. As I said before, 2/3 of the portfolio are bonds that mature after elections. These positions have been built with very high spreads, around 9%-10% above TAMAR or inflation. We should keep those positions. We are very comfortable with them. The remaining bonds are kept for liquidity reasons, and the average duration of those positions is around three or four months, so they will react immediately if rates go up.

Most of them, we kept them in held-to-collect books too.

Pedro Offenhenden: Perfect, Diego. Thank you.

#2911. Mavericks vs Nuggets Recap: 3 things from the Mavericks 120-101 win against the Nuggets

The Dallas Mavericks picked up their most impressive win of the season Tuesday night, defeating the Denver Nuggets 120-101 in Denver. This was an NBA cup group game, and the win kept the Mavericks undefeated in group play.

This was a dominant showing from the Mavericks in one of the hardest road environments in the NBA. Dallas led nearly the entire game, taking the lead with 7:30 left in the first quarter and never letting Denver get back on top.

Dallas' defense was the star of the first half, limiting the Nuggets to 39 points, the Nuggets worst first-half scoring mark of the season. In the first quarter both teams started a bit cold, but in the second quarter the Mavericks defense really took over. Dallas' swarming, aggressive style flummoxed a Nuggets team that wanted to slow the game down in the half-court. The Mavericks played the passing lanes well, with nine first-half steals, leading to multiple transition opportunities.

In the second half the Nuggets were more successful in making this more a half-court game, but Dallas answered every single rally attempt. Denver closed the gap to as little as three in the third quarter, but would never get it closer than that. Kyrie Irving woke up in the second half, and started a run to start the fourth quarter that truly put the game away. This was a balanced game from the Mavericks, as while Irving and Cooper Flagg had their highlight plays, they struggled overall with their shot.

This is a landmark win for a Dallas team on the rise. Let's go over our takeaways.

Dallas still has a pretty funky roster, imbalanced with so many bigs and forwards and weak on guards. So much of that talk in the summer and preseason reflected more on what that imbalance would mean on offense, but it was just as important to talk about what that meant for the Mavericks defense. Dallas doesn't have a lockdown perimeter guy — a point-of-attack defender, as the kids say. So how do you overcome that in a league dominated by elite perimeter ball handlers and high-tempo, spread pick-and-roll play? The answer: use all those athletic wings and bigs to play like absolute mad men.

Credit to coach Dusty May and his staff, they have imbued a defensive identity on this team that it has sorely lacked over the last few seasons. Dallas might not have defensive-stopper archetypes, but they have so many rangey wings and bigs — Cooper Flagg, PJ Washington, Zaccharie Risacher, Caleb Martin, Naji Marshall, Daniel Gafford, and Morez Johnson Jr. Those athletes have been unleashed, playing up and shooting the passing lanes to cause havoc. It hasn't always worked, and we've seen teams exploit this aggressive defense with spacing, shooting, and off-ball movement. But boy, when it works, and when the Mavericks are locked in with their aggression and rotating well, it's awfully impressive to watch. The Mavericks had the Nuggets in absolute hell in that second quarter where Dallas outscored Denver 34-22.

Dallas had 13 steals tonight, scoring 26 points off the 18 Denver turnovers. This also led to the Mavericks having an overwhelming 35-7 fastbreak points advantage and an absolutely preposterous 68-38 points in the paint advantage. Dallas made Denver look old and slow. Kudos to this coaching staff and a roster of players that have absolutely bought in.

Morez Johnson Jr.'s rookie season has been rough to say the least, with lower-body injuries during the summer and preseason seemingly zapping all of explosiveness. We've seen glimpses of Johnson's talents at various points of this young season, but they've been few and far between. Against the Nuggets Tuesday night, Johnson showed off why he was a top-10 pick this past summer.

Johnson finished with nine points, 11 rebounds, and a steal in 19 points. He led the Mavericks in rebounds despite being eighth in minutes played. He held his own against Nikola Jokic, keeping Jokic in check during the second half when the Nuggets were furiously trying to rally. Johnson punctuated this effort with a great dunk right at the end of the third quarter, showing some leaping ability that just hasn't been there for most of the season. Johnson also banked in a three-pointer, which was also fun and funny.

With Daniel Gafford unable to grab any defensive rebounds (only four in 27 minutes), Dallas is desperate for any rebounding help. Hopefully Johnson can build off this performance.

Kyrie Irving's final stat line doesn't seem great — 11 points on 5-of-19 shooting, and only 1-of-8 from threes. If you didn't watch the game, you'd think it was one of Irving's worst. And weirdly it both was and wasn't.

The shooting was as bad as it looked for spurts of the first half, but Irving willed himself to some big buckets in the second half and had some spectacular assists in the third quarter. Also Irving continued his off-ball brilliance, moving well off the ball and creating opportunities for himself and his teammates.

If you have a chance, rewatch the second half and focus on Irving. Whenever he doesn't have the ball, he's almost constantly on the move. Obviously there are times when Irving is off the ball and standing in the corner, but his constant motion without the ball causes so much trouble for opposing defenses. Irving's cuts toward the basket seemingly get him either a layup or free throw attempts almost every time, and that happened again against the Nuggets.

It's rare you see someone of Irving's talent be so comfortable and useful as an off-ball player. Irving relishes the dirty work of making cuts and moving, knowing that movement doesn't always directly benefit him. But it benefits his teammates, and it's clear Irving cares. It's just nice to watch, and this performance was another example of that.

#2909. CAVA vs. Monster Beverage: Which Consumer Stock Is a Better Buy for 2027?

Monster Beverage trades at a lower forward P/E than CAVA, yet CAVA's restaurant chain keeps growing at a pace that has investors weighing a sharp trade-off.

By Anders Bylund – Updated Nov 25, 2026 at 2:57PM EST

10 stocks we like better than Cava Group ›

Growth-minded investors are currently weighing the rapid expansion of a Mediterranean dining star against the steady dominance of a global energy drink powerhouse. Choosing between CAVA Group (CAVA +2.02%) and Monster Beverage (MNST +1.02%) involves balancing high-octane growth with established cash flow.

CAVA operates a fast-growing restaurant chain focusing on healthy, Mediterranean-inspired bowls and pitas. Monster Beverage leads the energy drink market through a massive global distribution network. While one company is scaling a physical footprint across the country, the other is leveraging high net margins and a stable beverage portfolio to generate significant capital.

CAVA serves chef-curated or build-your-own bowls and pitas at nearly 500 locations across the United States. The company emphasizes a multi-channel approach that includes in-restaurant dining, digital pick-up, and drive-thru options. It also sells branded dips and spreads in grocery stores, supporting its vertically-integrated supply chain of over 50 grower and producer partners.

Expansion remains a central theme for the business as it enters new states. In the 12 months ended Oct. 4, 2026, revenue reached nearly $1.4 billion, representing 27% growth. The company reported net income of approximately $64.7 million for that same period, demonstrating its ability to maintain profitability while investing heavily in its growing store count.

The balance sheet appears robust enough to support its long-term goals for retail stocks. As of its December 2025 balance sheet, the debt-to-equity ratio was roughly 0.6x, a measure of financial leverage that compares total liabilities to shareholders' equity. A current ratio of 2.7x suggests the company has sufficient assets to cover short-term liabilities, and in the fiscal year ended Dec. 28, 2025, free cash flow was close to $26.1 million. Free cash flow is the cash remaining after a company pays for its physical investments and core business activities.

Monster Beverage develops and distributes energy drinks in roughly 80 countries worldwide. It relies heavily on a network of full-service beverage bottlers, including those within the Coca-Cola (NYSE: KO) system. This distribution model allows the company to reach retail grocery chains, convenience stores, and e-commerce platforms with minimal direct capital investment in bottling facilities.

Financial performance shows the benefits of significant scale in the global beverage industry. In the 12 months ended Sept. 30, 2026, revenue reached close to $9.6 billion, reflecting revenue growth of about 20.2%. For that period, the company recorded net income of roughly $2.2 billion, maintaining a healthy net margin of nearly 22.7%.

The company maintains an exceptionally clean balance sheet to support its international expansion. As of its December 2025 balance sheet, the debt-to-equity ratio was 0.0x, indicating that the firm carries no debt relative to its equity. The current ratio of 3.7x shows a strong ability to meet short-term obligations, and in the fiscal year ended Dec. 31, 2025, free cash flow was approximately $2.0 billion. Free cash flow is the cash that remains in the business after the company funds capital projects and core operations.

CAVA faces intense competition within the restaurant and food manufacturing sectors from national players like Starbucks (SBUX +1.27%) and various local competitors. Expansion into new locations brings operational risks, including the challenge of maintaining brand culture and managing the costs of rapid growth. Furthermore, the company depends on third-party delivery partners and faces potential food safety risks that could harm its brand reputation.

Monster Beverage is heavily dependent on its relationship with distribution partners like Coca-Cola Europacific Partners (CCEP +0.57%). The company faces stiff competition from global giants such as PepsiCo (PEP +0.88%) and emerging rivals like Celsius Holdings (CELH +1.86%). Recent write-downs in its alcohol segment also highlight risks related to product diversification and shifting consumer health perceptions regarding energy drinks.

Monster Beverage appears more attractive based on its lower Forward P/E, while CAVA offers a lower P/S ratio despite its higher earnings multiple.

Valuation metrics include sourcing from Financial Modeling Prep (FMP) and may differ from other data providers. Data is current as of Nov. 24, 2026.

Monster Beverage is the better pick over CAVA Group for 2027, though the two stocks suit different investors. I'm in Monster's corner, and it's not a close call.

CAVA is the fun one. It has 500 restaurants, trailing revenue up 27%, a Las Vegas opening on deck, and a Mediterranean brand that makes lunch choices feel like a personality. It's hard not to root for it. But the latest quarter showed same-restaurant sales up just 3.1%, net income down to $13.2 million from $14.7 million, and trailing free cash flow of only $39.1 million. And the stock, even after sliding from its highs, still trades as if everything will go right. Spoiler alert: that's a really rare long-term outcome.

Monster is the grown-up in the room, if grown-ups sell caffeine bombs in neon cans. It posted $9.6 billion in trailing revenue, up 20.2%, along with $1.9 billion in free cash flow and zero debt. It doesn't need a cane to lean on if the economy limps. Coca-Cola's bottlers handle the heavy lifting on distribution, so the growth comes with minimal capital spending on Monster's end. That global partnership still has plenty of room to run.

Investors who want a high-growth restaurant story can still make a case for CAVA. Those who want growth, strong cash generation, and a clean balance sheet have an easier choice. I would pass on CAVA at today's price and buy Monster stock instead.

#2908. Junk bonds are 'flashing yellow.' Watch these warning signs

Cracks are forming in the junk bond market as investors demand higher payouts for owning the market's riskiest debt. It isn't time to ditch high-yield bonds, but investors should pay attention to the warning signs.

High-yield bonds now yield 8.1%, up from 7.22% a month ago. The increase reflects a jump in yields across the curve as investors bake in more inflation from high energy prices and other pressures, including concern about the deficit — hitting nearly $2 trillion in the fiscal year that ended Sept. 30.

The high-yield market is also showing stress on the credit side with spreads recently widening to levels not seen since April, according to the Federal Reserve Bank of St. Louis. Credit spreads are the difference in yield between the bonds and Treasurys of similar maturities. Wider spreads mean investors are demanding higher yields for holding corporate debt, viewing it as riskier.

Spreads are at 315 basis points in the overall high-yield market, higher than a year ago but still below levels in March when they reached 346 bps. One basis point equals one one-hundredth of a percent, or 0.01%.

The high-yield market consists of bonds rated BB+ by S&P and Fitch and Ba1 and under by Moody's. The lowest-rated cohort, CCC and below, has seen the most movement with spreads climbing dramatically over the past year to roughly 1,250 bps.

Right now, the high-yield market is "flashing yellow" but is "far from red," said Michael Arone, chief investment strategist at State Street Investment Management.

It makes sense that investors are demanding more compensation for taking on additional credit risk as borrowing costs rise, he said.

Yields are elevated across the board with the 10-year Treasury reaching its highest level since 2002 earlier in the week.

"The bigger question is whether this is simply a repricing of interest rate risk, or the beginning of a more fundamental reassessment of credit quality," Arone said.

He's in a wait-and-see mode since earnings are still growing, interest-coverage ratios remain good, and while default rates have ticked up some, he believes it is not concerning.

That said, the starting point in spreads is likely weighing on investors' psyche, since they are still low by historical standards.

"There's a small margin of error here, which I also think raises the anxiety level," Arone explained. "The compensation that investors are receiving for taking on this credit risk isn't overwhelming relative to history, and therefore subtle changes in credit spreads can be concerning."

While there may be concerns about part of the lower-rated market, the overall high-yield market is in good fundamental shape.

In fact, credit quality is at a record high, with BB bonds making up over 60% of the market compared with 38% prior to the global financial crisis, said Kelley Gerrity, a fixed income strategist at Morgan Stanley Investment Management.

"We've had higher-quality companies coming in, and with higher rates now, you also have more discipline from companies that are more indebted ... just because of the higher cost of capital, so that actually is creating a bit of a healthier picture as well," she said.

The lowest tier of high yield has always been more speculative, since the bonds have a higher default risk. Therefore, it's no surprise that they are the ones seeing the most widening in spreads, said Collin Martin, head of fixed income research and strategy at the Schwab Center for Financial Research.

The overall move in high-yield spreads have been "orderly," he said. "Are there cracks forming? I think the cracks are logical cracks with the lowest-rated bonds and it's too early to say that it's spreading to the broad credit market."

In addition, fluctuations within the CCC-rated and lower spreads have been idiosyncratic, Gerrity added. Morgan Stanley recently broke down the spreads in the lowest cohort into two buckets: performing assets and non-performing assets, which are defined as spreads above 1,000 bps.

The non-performing spread to worst is 2,818 bps, while the performing bucket — the biggest piece of the CCC market — is 461 bps, she said.

"It is isolated," she said. "I'm not necessarily sure that that will go away anytime soon, but it doesn't tell the story of overall concern and flashing caution for credit markets right now."

For now, investors should remain selective within higher yield, Gerrity advised.

"We're looking for the best relative value opportunities, and we think that exists within that single-B cohort, and so we're leaning into that," she said.

Investors should be concerned if there is a steep widening in the broader high-yield market.

But there's scant evidence of stress in the BB cohort. Spreads in that group are at 194 bps, up from 179 bps a year ago — although the move hasn't been in a straight line.

"We're going to be focusing our attention on what are considered some stronger businesses," Martin said. "If we start to see the markets demand higher spreads there also, that's what we'll be looking at to see if risks are really rising."

It's also important to understand that the Federal Reserve is raising rates in a strong economic environment, said R.J. Gallo, chief investment officer of global fixed income at Federated Hermes. The central bank is concerned about an inflation problem amid high fossil fuel prices due to the Iran war, while growth has been stronger than expected, he noted.

"With growth being good as the reason that the Fed is hiking, then you wouldn't expect high yield to blow out because growth means revenues stay up, means cash flow and profitability stays up," he explained.

High yield becomes a disaster when the economy is heading into a sharp economic downturn, Gallo said.

"That's when spreads really widen out," he said. "But a recession is not the odds-on bet. Now, talk to me in six months if the Fed keeps hiking over and over, if oil prices stay high for longer, well, then maybe we will start to wonder."

#2907. Tech Stock Sell-Off: Here's Why Apple Has Been Immune

While rivals pour billions into data centers, Apple is putting AI into the devices people already own, and its cash flow shows the difference.

By Patrick Sanders – Nov 25, 2026 at 3:00PM EST

Artificial intelligence (AI) stocks have taken a beating in November. The tech-heavy Nasdaq-100 index is down 15% this month on fears that AI spending has gotten out of hand. But the world's largest company seems immune to the problem.

Smartphone maker Apple (AAPL +0.54%) is bucking the trend. Its shares are virtually unchanged for the month, and in 2026 it's posting generous gains, up more than 25%, while many of its AI peers slump.

And there's a good reason why. As other tech companies spend heavily on AI infrastructure, Apple is focused on bringing AI capabilities directly to its 2.5 billion devices currently in service.

That strategy is looking pretty smart right now.

Microsoft, Alphabet, and Amazon are investing heavily in enormous cloud computing networks. Meta Platforms is working on "personal superintelligence," and Tesla is focused on robotics and Cybercabs.

Apple's strategy, meanwhile, is focused on improving the experience for people who already own its smartphones, personal computers, tablets, and smart-home devices. Part of that strategy is integrating its devices with Siri AI, its improved personal assistant that launched this year. "Siri AI marks a new chapter in AI at Apple. It is a completely reimagined assistant that is powerful, personal, private, and deeply relevant to our users' daily lives," CEO John Ternus said.

That's why it doesn't need to dominate the AI infrastructure business to benefit from artificial intelligence. It needs to give customers compelling reasons to buy another iPhone, Mac, or iPad.

Apple's financial results suggest that its core business remains exceptionally healthy.

It generated approximately $136 billion in free cash flow during fiscal 2026 (ended Sept. 26), up nearly 38% from the previous year. That's money remaining after capital expenditures, giving Apple tremendous flexibility to return cash to shareholders, invest in new products, and strengthen its business. Meanwhile, revenue jumped 15% to $478.8 billion for the fiscal year.

Apple's balance sheet has allowed its stock to hold steady even as rising memory chip prices squeeze hardware margins. Management expects gross margins of 45% to 46% in the first quarter of fiscal 2027, down from 47.9% in Q4 2026. But its services business, which carries gross margins of around 76%, provides an important financial cushion.

Apple has something that many AI infrastructure investors are still waiting to see from their investments: enormous cash generation without the same requirement to spend billions building the infrastructure that makes it possible.

#2904. Nano-X (NNOX) Q3 2026 Earnings Call Transcript

Need a quote from a Motley Fool analyst? Email [email protected]

Nano-X (NNOX -12.28%) reported a 29% increase in total revenue for the third quarter of 2026, primarily attributed to the consolidation of its Health IT business and growth in teleradiology services. Management stated that the company is prioritizing operational efficiency and commercial execution in the U.S. market as it manages limited liquidity and works to restore compliance with Nasdaq listing requirements. The company is transitioning its sales strategy from a per-scan service model to capital-intensive equipment sales to accelerate cash generation and support long-term sustainability.

We acknowledge the disappointment this has caused shareholders and do not take your trust for granted. We are acting with urgency to narrow the gap between our technological progress and the commercial performance. We continue to focus on delivering measurable commercial and financial results. Our conviction in our technology has strengthened as we have advanced image quality, system performance, and clinical capabilities.

Nanox.ARC was selected as one of TIME's Best Inventions of 2026, named a finalist in Gartner's 2026 Eye on Innovation Awards for Healthcare and Life Science, and recognized with a Red Dot Award for product design. These capabilities are currently at various stages of development and reflect the continuous expansion of our clinical roadmap.

Our primary commercial focus remains the U.S., where we continue to strengthen and activate our network of channel partners and build our Nanox Imaging Network infrastructure. Through closer collaboration with our distribution partners, joint sales initiatives, and targeted engagement with healthcare providers, we are working to expand our market reach, develop qualified commercial opportunities, and improve the conversion of these opportunities into operational installations. Our focus is now on working closely with our partners to translate these foundations into system sales, installations, and over time, revenue-generating operations. Our channel partner strategy is designed to support agreements with a broader network of healthcare facilities, which we believe could give each deployment significant beyond a single hospital.

Alongside these efforts, we continue to support our installed base, extend clinical utilization, and strengthen the physician and customer relationship essential to sustainable commercial growth. Beyond the U.S., we are making measured progress in selected international markets. In Peru, we received the first payment for a Nanox.ARC system, and the system has been shipped. In Argentina, we obtained import approvals and have shipped the systems designated for the market, both under consignments arrangement. Our preliminary third quarter 2026 revenue reflects year-over-year growth. Nevertheless, we recognize that revenue from imaging system sales remain limited relative to our long-term ambitions. At the same time, our current liquidity position requires decisive action.

We are pursuing operating cost reductions, commercial initiatives, and financing and strategic alternatives aimed at strengthening our financial position and supporting our long-term objectives. Our commercial progress provides the foundation for the next phase. To accelerate execution while preserving the capabilities essential to long-term growth, we are focused on four priorities. First, Nanox.ARC and the Nanox Imaging Network, or NIN. Here, we are working to accelerate U.S. commercialization throughout our current channel partners, drive system installations, clinical adoption, and utilization, and activate our constructed NIN sites. On the network itself, we are seeing early scanning and reimbursement activity as we work to expand it.

As a reminder, NIN deploys Nanox systems in care segments such as workers' compensation and concierge practices where reimbursement can be more favorable. Our preliminary models suggest each site could potentially generate $0.5 million-$1 million in annual revenues, depending on utilization, reimbursement, and payer mix. At our first NIN site in Philadelphia, initial activity shows a minimum average of 9-10 patients per day, with average revenues per scan ranging from $163-$249 per patient, based on the scans already performed. Second, our commercial partnerships. We are strengthening our distribution relationships, exploring strategic opportunities for Nanox.AI and our OEM capabilities, and advancing our teleradiology and healthcare IT activities, all with the aim of supporting revenues growth.

Turning to Nanox.AI, we entered into a distribution agreement with Technologic s.r.l., an Italian company affiliated with Tecnologie Avanzate, for the commercialization of HealthOST, our bone health solution in Italy. This agreement is intended to support the expansion of our AI commercial presence in the Italian market. Nanox.AI signed a distribution agreement with Dosy Health, a Thailand-based healthcare technology company, for Nanox.AI cardiac solution, HealthCCSng, to support its planned introduction and commercialization in Thailand. In Poland, Nanox.AI entered into a distribution agreement with a local distribution partner for commercialization of HealthCCSng, our cardiac solution, and HealthOST, our bone health solution. This agreement further supports our European expansion through local distribution partners.

We continue to expand our commercial and clinical footprint, including new pilot programs with two additional hospitals in India, as we continue to drive broader adoption of Nanox.AI solutions. Moving on from Nanox.AI to our OEM business, revenue in Q3 grew compared to the prior quarter, largely due to our project with the U.S. department of energy and portable X-ray applications for the military. This specific project, if successful, has the potential to be commercialized in 2027. Rounding out our commercial partnerships, the Nanox Health IT acquisition has proven to be a valuable addition to Nano-X and continues to contribute meaningful revenue, providing nearly $1 million during the third quarter.

That brings me to our third priority, operational efficiency, where we are reducing operating costs while preserving our clinical innovation, regulatory, supply continuity, and commercial capabilities. To that end, we continue to align our cost base with our commercial priorities. As previously announced, the additional measures, together with the previously implemented initiatives, are designed to achieve an annualized reduction of 25%-30% in the company operating expenses, excluding related charges once the measures are fully implemented, which the company currently expects to occur by the end of Q1 2027. Fourth, financial flexibility. We are evaluating financing and strategic alternatives to support commercialization and liquidity.

Before I review the third quarter results, I will place them in the context of priorities described earlier. As we work to convert our commercial progress into recurring revenue, we continue to manage our cost base with discipline and to strengthen our financial position. During the quarter, we continued to execute the restructuring of our South Korea operation, including the transition of chip production to our OEM partners. Together with the earlier headcount reduction in Israel, we continue to expect these steps to generate annualized cost savings of approximately $2 million beginning in 2027. We continue to evaluate financing and strategic alternatives as we are addressing the applicable Nasdaq listing requirement with careful attention to impact on our existing shareholders.

All figures that I'm reviewing now relate to the third quarter ending September 30th, 2026, and all comparable figures are related to the third quarter of 2025, unless otherwise noted. The company generated revenues of $3.2 million from our teleradiology services, $0.9 million from our AI and software solutions, and $0.3 million from our sales of imaging system and the OEM services. Cash and cash equivalents and restricted deposits as of September 30th, 2026 were $26.6 million. This compares to a cash and cash equivalent short-term deposit and restricted deposit balance of $16 million as of December 31st, 2025. Converting our commercial agreements and pipeline into revenue is central to our focus on execution. Our objective is to balance cost reduction, revenue generation, and access to funding while preserving the product development and sales channel capabilities required for execution. At least what I know is that we are adding new customers all the time, and we are recruiting more radiologists that will enable us to cover more customers and give the services. One other thing which is important, what we see is that we see more MRI readings compared to X-rays, so this actually improved the quality of the revenues as well.

But we work diligently in order to grow this business as well. The one other thing that is important is that the Nanox Health IT business, that when we acquired them, it was actually losing money entity, and we have managed to not only adding customers, but also to get to break even and hopefully for net cash generation next year, if we really are able to grow the business.

Scott Robert Henry: Okay. Thank you for taking the questions.

Operator: Thank you. Our next question comes from Sarah James with Cantor Fitzgerald. Your line is open.

Sarah James: Thank you. Can you update us on where the RadNet expansion stands?

Erez I. Meltzer: RadNet is right now has the first unit which is operating and scanning more and more. We are waiting for the discussion and their approval for the extension to get the clinical trial for the lung cancer screening and the chest scans. As soon as we have it signed and implemented, we will be happy to share it with the market.

Sarah James: Great. Can you also update us on how many customers are, or how are the customers using the G0680 code, and how many of the five AI pilots have converted to paid?

Erez I. Meltzer: I have to check the exact number, but I think that right now we have a few of them already that signed. And what we will do is next earning call, we will update where it is right now. But in terms of the pilots that we have, we are adding pilots both in the U.S. but also in India that we mentioned even today. And we will update the number as soon as we have meaningful news to share about this.

#2903. Stats Rundown: 5 numbers to know from the Mavericks 120-101 win over the Nuggets

The Dallas Mavericks (11-7) took on the Denver Nuggets (10-6) in Emirates Cup action on Tuesday night. Carrying a three-game winning streak into Denver, Dallas continued the trend with a 120-101 victory.

The Mavericks were leading by 13 points with 1:16 remaining in the second quarter. Jamal Crawford, announcing on the Peacock broadcast, stated that the final minute-plus was crucial – the score could easily balloon to 17, or be cut to 8. The Mavericks reeled off six more as part of a 15-0 run to bump the lead to 19 points.

Perhaps the Mavericks had the release of Grand Theft Auto VI on their minds, as they were stealing everything in sight. Dallas had nine steals in the first half – five of which came from Caleb Martin – which they turned into 17 points. This was a textbook example of turning defense into offense. Turning the ball over limited the Nuggets' offense in a big way and the Mavericks doubled the damage by scoring on the other end.

Throughout the first half, the Mavericks' three-point defense left a bit to be desired, but they weren't badly hurt due in part to the Nuggets missing their attempts. The third quarter flipped that script in a significant way. Dallas' lack of close-outs often left shooters wide open and Denver shot themselves right back into the game. The Nuggets hit six threes in the frame, with Christian Braun alone hitting three in a row in under a minute.

The Mavericks showed a very well-balanced offense, which was critical on a night where both Cooper Flagg and Kyrie Irving struggled a bit with their shot. All five Mavericks starters – Irving, Flagg, Martin, Zaccharie Risacher and Daniel Gafford – were joined by P.J. Washington and Naji Marshall in scoring double figures. Morez Johnson, Jr. was a single point shy of joining the club.

Coming into the night, Flagg needed to connect on two three-pointers to achieve the rare milestone of scoring 1,500 or more points and hitting 100 or more three-pointers before turning 20-years-old. With only a single made three, he temporarily fell short of joining Kobe Bryant as the only teenagers in league history to achieve the feat. He has just under a month to get there, with his 20th birthday arriving on Dec. 21.

I invite you to follow me @_80MPH on X, and check back often at Mavs Moneyball for all the latest on the Dallas Mavericks.

#2899. Is Jensen Huang Really Worth $954 Billion to Nvidia?

Jensen Huang’s return sparked an epic rally, but investors are still left with more questions than answers.

By Daniel Foelber – Nov 25, 2026 at 3:15PM EST

Nvidia (NVDA -3.94%) popped 31.5% on Monday, Nov. 23. Nvidia closed with a market cap of $3.985 trillion -- $954 billion more than it closed at on Friday, Nov. 20. This would be the equivalent of creating the world's 16th most valuable company out of thin air.

The move came as Nvidia's board of directors voted unanimously to reinstate Jensen Huang as president, CEO, and board director.

Investors may be wondering if one of roughly 42,000 employees should be worth such a large percentage of the company's value.

Here's why the move may still be an underreaction to how invaluable Huang is to Nvidia.

Jensen Huang. Image source: Getty Images.

Huang's sudden departure exposed serious threats to Nvidia's investment thesis -- namely, that management was ill-prepared to run the company without him. And that the company's vision and culture centered on Huang.

Huang ran the semiconductor company as an extension of himself, with dozens of direct reports and client relationships built on decades of industry experience. The informality of Nvidia's structure allowed Nvidia to operate with the speed of a start-up with the financial muscle of an industry leader -- which has been a lethal combination that has allowed Nvidia to fiercely protect its lead over the competition and maintain pricing power with high margins.

Elon Musk, CEO of Tesla (TSLA -0.89%) and Space Exploration Technologies (SPCX +1.07%), has been one of the most public supporters for retaining Huang as CEO. In a conversation on the social media platform X, Elon Musk shed light on Huang's operating style and the vulnerabilities his departure created for Nvidia.

If Jensen gave me his word about compute delivery or I asked for components to be accelerated, he always came through. Sure, we’d put together a purchase order at some point later, but I never for a second thought it wouldn’t happen.

On SpaceX's second-quarter 2026 earnings call in August, which was its first earnings call as a public company, Musk mentioned that SpaceX would build exclusively on Nvidia because it believed that Nvidia's Vera Rubin architecture was the best computing platform. With SpaceX owning xAI and having plans to build orbital constellations of AI compute satellites in space, being exclusive to Nvidia is a massive vote of confidence in Nvidia's innovation. But also in Jensen Huang.

I doubt Musk would make such a clear commitment if he didn't believe in Nvidia's tech just as much as he does in its leadership. And if Huang were to leave and Nvidia's future computing platforms didn't produce as big a leap as Rubin did from Blackwell, or as big a leap as Blackwell did compared to Hopper, then Nvidia could be leaving business from top innovators like SpaceX on the table.

In the third quarter of fiscal year 2027, Nvidia reported a 103% year-over-year increase in quarterly revenue and a generally accepted accounting principles (GAAP) gross margin of 74.8%. Nvidia is currently in its fourth quarter of fiscal 2027. But management is already guiding for a 75% increase in year-over-year revenue in fiscal 2028 -- up from prior guidance of 70%.

Nvidia spent years developing Rubin but only began shipments in August. In Nvidia's latest quarter, Rubin contributed nearly $20 billion in revenue compared to $107.6 billion in total data center revenue, which made it the fastest product ramp-up in history in a product's first quarter of shipments.

Nvidia's upcoming fiscal year will effectively turn Nvidia's extensive backlog into realized order volumes. And again, it's hard to quantify just how much of that backlog was due to Jensen Huang, and how much of Nvidia's upcoming architecture after Rubin is due to talent attracted to Nvidia by Huang.

There are few CEOs as intertwined with a company's investment thesis as tech founders like Jensen Huang with Nvidia, Elon Musk with Tesla and SpaceX, and Mark Zuckerberg with Meta Platforms (META +3.40%). It's clear that Huang's ability to drive innovation and multi-year commitments is an inseparable element of Nvidia's valuation. So while Huang is definitely worth at least $954 billion to Nvidia, his sudden resignation exposed a gaping hole in Nvidia's investment thesis that needs to be plugged with a clear succession plan and improved governance.

There's also the ongoing risk that internal and external investigations expose facts that force Huang to resign down the road. Pay close attention to the wording of the Nov. 23 press release -- that Huang's reinstatement poses no national security risk. I take that to mean that Huang's return as CEO isn't a national security threat, not that Huang is totally in the clear on national security leaks and export violations.

Until investors get more clarity, Nvidia remains a hold rather than a buy.

#2897. Why Oracle Stock Slumped Today

There are growing doubts about the balance sheets of weaker players in the hyperscaler market.

By Lee Samaha – Nov 25, 2026 at 3:20PM EST

Oracle (ORCL -4.79%) stock was down by more than 8% at midday today. The slump comes on a day of weakness for AI -related stocks. In a nutshell, there are increasing concerns around the costs of AI enterprises developing frontier AI models and, in turn, their ability to pay AI infrastructure companies like Oracle.

The weakening sentiment is evident in the credit default swap (CDS) pricing on its debt. CDS pricing reflects thre price of insuring against a debt default. It's measured in basis points (bps), whereby 100 basis points equals 1%. So, for example, insuring $1 million in debt with a CDS at 100 bps (1%) would cost $10,000 per year.

In the middle of 2025, the CDS on Oracle's 5-year debt stood at just 35 bps, but it closed out 2025 at 154 bps. But here's the thing. Yesterday it spiked to 360 bps, according to data from S&P Global Market Intelligence.

To put that number into context, the bond market typically assumes that 40% of a defaulted bond is actually paid out, so the implied annual hazard rate can be calculated by dividing the CDS price by the 60% loss in default. In other words, .036/0.6=.06, or 6% a year.

Over 5 years, the cumulative rate equates to almost 26%. While it's important to note this is just the bond market pricing, there is still a lot of fear in the market over Oracle. Those fears are centered on its soaring capital expenditures to fund its AI build-out, which have turned its cash flow negative.

ORCL Net Total Long Term Debt (Quarterly) data by YCharts

The market is clearly worried about the sustainability of AI investment, and in such conditions it makes sense to focus on stronger players like Alphabet on weakness, rather than chase Oracle stock.

The source noted that Utah didn't have specific players in mind, but the Jazz have "cast a wide net."
