#2872. 2026 Week 12 rooting guide for Cowboys fans

Thanksgiving week is always a big one for the Cowboys and the NFL as a whole. Beyond any special halftime shows or other extra production, it's also generally when the regular season heats up as playoff scenarios begin to take clearer form.

Despite all of their tribulations so far this year, the Cowboys are still just a couple of games away from both a division title and the NFC's last wild card spot. Let's check out the current standings, and then see how the entire Week 12 slate could help or hurt Dallas.

1. San Francisco 49ers 9-1 (6-1 vs NFC)2. Detroit Lions 8-2 (5-1)3. New York Giants 6-3-1 (3-3-1)4. Carolina Panthers 5-5 (4-3)5. Chicago Bears 8-2 (6-2)6. Minnesota Vikings 7-3 (5-2)7. Green Bay Packers 6-4 (4-4)_______________________________8. Seattle Seahawks 6-4 (2-3)9. Los Angeles Rams 6-4 (4-2)10. Dallas Cowboys 5-6 (2-5)

With the Packers, Rams, and Seahawks all on a bye last week, Dallas was able to inch closer in the wild card race. It's hard to see the Cowboys leapfrogging all three of those teams into the seventh seed, but at least it's still on the table.

Even winning the NFC East isn't solely up to the Cowboys at this point. They'll need help for either lane to get into the playoffs.

How might some help come from the Week 12 schedule?

The Jaguars let us down last week, allowing New York to pull off yet another surprise win. We may have to stop calling them upsets soon, as John Harbaugh has the Giants consistently performing above expectations.

This week, the G-Men are anything but underdogs. The Colts appear to be in full tank mode now, and even a 3-3 home record doesn't give much room for confidence. If anything, we have to hope that New York is the team that's feeling itself and allows Indy to catch them by surprise.

You can go either way on Washington at this point. On the one hand, you'd like them to keep winning and hurt their draft position. On the other, you don't want them suddenly having something to play for when they meet Dallas in Week 18.

A loss to Arizona would certainly take the wind out of their sails, and keep the NFC East feeling like a two-horse race. That feels like the better option this week, but it's truly a 50/50 proposition.

Los Angeles winning the Wednesday night game is doubly good for Dallas. It pulls Green Bay down a peg, who is currently our biggest concern of those 6-4 teams with a head-to-head advantage. It also helps the value of the 2027 first-round pick they owe us. If someone's going to get that seventh seed aside from the Cowboys, we definitely don't want it to be Green Bay.

Speaking of NFC North teams, the best thing for Dallas is that the Bears, Lions, and Vikings all stay jumbled at the top and lock the Packers out of a possible division win. The Packers have division games left against the Bears and Lions, and we want to see their rivals motivated in those contests.

The NFC West is a different story, with the Niners looking ready to run right on through the Super Bowl. They can have the division, at least, and help the Cowboys by putting losses on our wild card competition like the Seahawks and Rams along the way.

In the one division that makes the NFC East look competent this year, the NFC South "winner" is guaranteed to be at least the fourth seed in the playoffs. If the Cowboys do slide into the tournament as NFC East champions, they'd rather be the third seed if possible. So whoever wins the South, may they have the worst record possible.

This week's only interconference game is a real nothing burger for Cowboys fans. The best thing we can say about a Saints loss is that it pushes them further up the draft order and helps block the Eagles. So there's that.

For purely draft-related reasons: Dallas is owed the Steelers' 4th-round pick and the Patriots' 7th-rounder in 2027. So the worse their records are, the better.

#2869. One Possession: Baylor Scheierman provides key late-game scoring for Celtics

One Possession: Baylor Scheierman provides key late-game scoring for Celtics originally appeared on NBC Sports Boston

Baylor Scheierman produced the prettiest pass of the Boston Celtics' entire four-game road trip on Tuesday night … but didn't even get the assist.

That's kind of the way things have gone lately for Boston's bench, which has been well intentioned throughout Boston's four-game trip, but simply misfired on an absurd amount of quality looks.

This time it was rookie Chris Cenac Jr. who smoked a cutting layup off Scheierman's pretty feed through traffic. But QB1 still found a way to leave his stamp on Boston's streak-busting win over the Wizards on Tuesday night.

Scheierman shook off his own rough shooting on this four-game road trip to score eight points in a 75-second span late in the fourth quarter. The Wizards had whittled Boston's 34-point lead down to five in crunch time but Scheierman, getting the late-game nod from coach Joe Mazzulla, hit a clutch 3-pointer off a feed from Jayson Tatum to put Boston up nine with 2:31 to go.

Some hustle hunting an offensive rebound on Boston's next offensive trip put Scheierman at the free throw line for two more points, then he capped his late-game outburst by knocking down a 31-foot bomb off another feed from Tatum.

Scheierman was a mere 3-of-19 shooting (15.8 percent) during Boston's four-game losing streak before finding his groove in the fourth quarter against the Wizards.

Boston desperately needs some of their young wings to differentiate themselves this season and Scheierman continues to be the steadiest of the bunch. Even as his shot defied him on this trip, Scheierman made good things happen with his basketball IQ.

That should have included the touchdown pass to Cenac Jr., with Boston's bench ready to explode after the highlight-worthy feed.

Still, as the Celtics risked letting another big lead evaporate, it was Scheierman who made some clutch late-game shots. And might have saved Thanksgiving for the Celtics.

#2865. Top Wall Street analysts are bullish on the growth potential of these 3 stocks

Fears around the artificial intelligence trade and higher pressure on oil prices are weighing on global markets, but a few stocks offer investors solid growth prospects even in choppy times.

Investors with a long-term horizon can look beyond the ongoing volatility and track the recommendations of top Wall Street analysts to pick attractive stocks with strong long-term growth potential.

Here are three stocks favored by some of Wall Street's top pros, according to TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

Health insurer UnitedHealth delivered better-than-expected results for the third quarter of 2026. The company also raised its full-year earnings outlook, reflecting continued progress on its turnaround plan.

Following the Q3 print, Morgan Stanley analyst Erin Wright reiterated a buy rating on UNH stock with a price target of $529. "UNH's beat & raise reinforces turnaround momentum, with improving MA [Medicare Advantage]/Optum Health supporting a higher '26 EPS baseline & upside to '27 consensus," said Wright.

The five-star analyst added that better-than-expected performance in the UnitedHealthcare and Optum Health businesses supported another upgrade to the company's 2026 adjusted earnings per share outlook to at least $20.50. Wright noted that UNH raised the earnings guidance despite incremental investments planned for the fourth quarter of 2026.

Furthermore, Wright highlighted management's view that the consensus 2027 EPS of $22.63 was too conservative. Reaching even the low end of the company's 13% to 16% long-term EPS growth target would imply an EPS of $23.17.

Wright raised her 2026 EPS estimate to $20.65 from $19.82 and 2027 EPS to $23.24 from $22.54, reflecting the latest upside and an improving 2026 medical loss ratio to 87.4% from 88.1%. Overall, she believes that UNH's operational performance continues to improve, increasing her confidence in its UHC strategy and Optum Health turnaround execution.

Wright ranks No. 264 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. Her ratings have been successful 64% of the time, delivering an average return of 15%. See UnitedHealth Ownership Structure on TipRanks.

Social media giant Meta Platforms has been making headlines since the launch of its Muse AI agent, which is seeing solid traction. Investors look forward to updates on the company's AI strategy when it announces its third-quarter results on Oct. 21.

Heading into Q3 earnings, Guggenheim analyst Michael Morris maintained a buy rating on Meta stock and increased his price target to $850 from $700, reflecting higher estimates for 2027. He expects Muse usage momentum and engagement levels to shape investor sentiment into the Q3 earnings call.

Specifically, Morris highlighted that, according to Apptopia, Muse reached 1.3 million daily active users in five weeks versus 300,000 in the first week. Given that Muse's current adoption represents less than 0.05% of Meta's 3.6 billion daily active user base, the analyst expects management to prioritize boosting usage over near-term monetization.

"Over time, we believe Muse monetizes primarily through businesses paying to reach consumers through their agents, consistent with the economics of Meta's core advertising business, rather than consumer wallets," said the five-star analyst. He added that the checkout integrations with Shopify and PayPal reflect this framework.

While there are concerns that a rival could overtake Muse or the recently launched Muse Wonder and push Meta into a prolonged cycle of overinvestment, Morris remains bullish due to the company's strong record in scaling WhatsApp and competing strongly with TikTok via Reels as well as free distribution to an extensive user base.

Morris ranks No. 629 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 57% of the time, delivering an average return of 13.2%. See Meta Platforms KPIs on TipRanks.

Next is semiconductor company Marvell Technology. Earlier this month, Marvell held its Investor Day, where it raised its total addressable market estimate to $400 billion by 2030, reflecting solid AI-led demand tailwinds.

After hosting a non-deal roadshow with Marvell's senior management following the Investor Day, Stifel analyst Tore Svanberg reiterated a buy rating on the stock with a price target of $370.

The five-star analyst said that a major focus of the roadshow was the XPU and XPU Attach mix, as Marvell's Custom business expands toward its FY31 revenue target of $30 billion. In this regard, management reiterated that it expects a balanced mix of XPU and XPU Attach revenue in FY31, though it noted that either segment could grow rapidly enough to shift the mix toward 60/40 or 40/60.

While XPU drove most of the nearly $1.5 billion Custom revenue in FY26, Svanberg expects Marvell's XPU Attach business to grow substantially through the end of the decade, leading to a more balanced mix in this business. At the Investor Day event, the company raised its FY29 Custom target from more than $10 billion to over $12 billion, mainly driven by strength in the XPU Attach business.

Svanberg said that another key topic in the roadshow was whether Marvell can secure enough capacity to meet its raised targets. Management said that it met with suppliers ahead of Investor Day to share its five-year goals and received commitments to support these targets. The analyst believes that Marvell's supply chain commitments give it a competitive edge over rivals as customers seek reliable partners for large-scale infrastructure buildouts.

Svanberg ranks No. 91 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 58% of the time, delivering an average return of 31.8%. See Marvell Technology Statistics on TipRanks.

#2864. NFL Predictions: Bears will get their first win in Detroit since 2021

Thursday is the biggest regular-season game between the Chicago Bears and the Detroit Lions in a really long time. The Athletic's Kevin Fishbain dug up that the only two years both the Bears and Lions made the playoffs were 1994 and 1991. With each team currently holding a 90% chance to make the postseason, the odds are pretty good that it happens again.

In 1994, both teams snuck into the playoffs at 9-7, but in 1991 the Lions won the NFC Central at 12-4, with the Bears right behind at 11-5. They split the series in '91, with the Bears winning at Soldier Field and the Lions winning ugly at the Pontiac Silverdome on Thanksgiving, 16-6.

The winner of this Thanksgiving Day game will move to 9-2, be the two-seed in the NFC, and claim the top spot in the division with just six games to go.

Our partners at FanDuel Sportsbook have the Bears as a 1.5-point favorite (it opened at 2.5, so money has been coming in on the Lions) with the over/under at 55.5, which is the highest point total this week. I went back and forth on my prediction all week, and I can see it going either way, but give me the Bears to win, 28 to 27.

So that's the Bears and the moneyline (-122), the Lions and the 1.5 points (-112), and the under (-112).

Not only is Ben Johnson looking for his first win over the Lions, but Caleb Williams is too. The last time the Bears beat Detroit, Justin Fields had 289 combined yards, Chicago sacked Jared Goff 4 times, and he had 3 turnovers.

The last time the Bears won in Detroit, Andy Dalton threw for 317 yards, with Darnell Mooney catching 5 for 123.

Did you realize the Lions are just 1-8 on Thanksgiving since 2017? The Bears are 3-1 against them on Turkey Day in that stretch, with the only loss coming in 2024 when Matt Eberflus was a deer in headlights in the waning seconds, forgetting to call a timeout, and was fired the next day.

#2861. NBA power rankings: How far will slumping Thunder, Celtics fall?

As the NBA calendar churns on, Thanksgiving will give fans the chance to catch their breath — and, perhaps, catch some football.

No games will be played Thursday, Nov. 26, though NBA Cup action is starting to ramp up.

Another thing ramping up are the stakes, as certain contenders are facing adversity and losing skids. Chief among them are the Oklahoma City Thunder, who have been besieged by injuries and lost six of their last eight. In the East, it's the Boston Celtics who tumbled on a four-game losing streak, though they snapped it Tuesday, Nov. 24 against the Wizards. How far will both those teams fall?

Here are the Week 5 USA TODAY Sports NBA power rankings:

Movement from previous rankings in parentheses

Tyler Herro has embraced efficiency and has been on a scoring flash. Milwaukee's first win of November (against the Raptors) was a nice boost.

With all their injuries, they've had six different players lead the team in scoring over their last six games. That said, points are still too tough to come by.

A gritty win against the Celtics in which the Kings held Boston to just 13 points in the third quarter was a glimpse of what this roster can do when playing well. The problem? That doesn't happen anywhere near enough.

The offense has moments when it struggles to make baskets, but no team allows more points per 100 possessions than Brooklyn does, a staggering 120.3.

The Clippers rank second in the NBA in 3-point percentage (38.1%) but are middle of the pack in attempts (37.8 per game; tied for 15th).

Losers of 11 of their last 12, the Hornets have serious offensive issues and rank last in scoring, averaging just 100.6 points per game.

New Orleans isn't much better and has lost 10 of 11. The Pels also rank dead last in 3-point attempts per game (30.3).

Scottie Barnes has been stellar, but this team simply won't compete as long as Kawhi Leonard is sidelined. Toronto has lost seven of eight.

The Bulls have been far better as of late, with wins over the last 10 days against three teams in the top 15 of this list: the Hawks, Timberwolves and Cavaliers.

In their last game, they beat the injured Thunder (Shai Gilgeous-Alexander did play, however). That was a positive, but the Pacers offense cannot afford to be without Tyrese Haliburton. The good news? He practiced with Indiana's G League squad.

Anthony Davis has been superb and has stayed healthy, but Washington's other offensive weapons haven't been as consistent. Still, three wins in their last four is solid for the Wizards.

Franz Wagner was keeping Orlando competitive, but it has become clear, as teams have started to scout the Magic, that they miss Paolo Banchero (ankle). Even when he returns, however, he'll need reps to figure out how he jells with Wagner in new coach Sean Sweeney's system.

Since Stephen Curry (oblique) went down, the offensive production simply hasn't been there to sustain competitive play. Golden State ranks last in points per game (94.5) over the last six games.

Even though he hasn't been out long, and even though his production has dipped this season, the Cavs have regressed on defense and miss the scoring of Evan Mobley (toe).

After a slow start in which the offense lagged, Phoenix has won eight of its last 10. The Suns are doing a nice job of sharing the ball and limiting turnovers.

The other night against the Heat, the Thunder dressed just nine players because of injury. Their 10th place standing in the West is the lowest they've been since April 9 … of 2023.

They let the Mavericks clamp them up to go on a 17-0 run Tuesday night to close out the first half and begin the second, leading to a 21-point deficit. Low effort on transition defense compounded everything.

This young team has had some solid wins this season. And, with his blend of scoring and play-making, Cooper Flagg is well on his way to his first (of many) All-Star appearances.

It's clear Deni Avdija and Damian Lillard are the offensive catalysts. But since Ja Morant (hamstring) went out, no player has emerged as a third option.

The Grizz have continued to be one of the top surprises this season, but their star rookie, No. 3 overall pick Cameron Boozer, missing a month may set them back a touch.

Very quietly, the Hawks have been building a terrific season. They're the only team to average more than 30 assists per game, they rank fourth in second-chance points per game (16.9) and they've been playing cohesive and collaborative team defense.

Live by the 3, die by it; during Boston's recent four-game losing streak, which it snapped Tuesday night against the Wizards, the Celtics shot just 28.3% from beyond the arc.

They own the NBA's longest winning streak (seven games), have incorporated more on-ball screening and pick-and-rolls and an encouraging sign was that the offense rolled against a very depleted Thunder squad while Giannis Antetokounmpo was out.

After a slow start, the defending champs have settled in. They're leading the league in rebound percentage (53.8%). Karl-Anthony Towns leads the NBA in rebounding (13.9 per game) and hasn't had a single game in which he didn't reach double figures in the stat.

One of the more positive signs for Detroit has been its defense, which ranks fourth in rating (108.3) and tied for second in blocks per game (5.8) — all without Jalen Duren.

Houston has dropped two of its last four, both against solid opponents, but the Rockets have embraced efficiency, ranking third in true shooting (59.9%) and effective field goal percentage (56.0%).

Ayo Dosunmu has been a revelation for the Timberwolves with Anthony Edwards (knee) out, and his steady scoring has kept the offense afloat.

With Autin Reaves (heel) out, things have been shakier lately, though just one loss in their last 12 shows this team has staying power. But L.A. could use a Reaves return.

Joel Embiid's illness has come at a tough time, as he had just started to find his offensive dominance, scoring 29.7 points over his last three.

Even without Dylan Harper (hip), the Spurs are thriving, and Victor Wembanyama is playing at an MVP level. San Antonio also leads the NBA in net rating (+12.3).

#2857. Packers Week 12 Opponent Preview: Who are this year’s Los Angeles Rams?

The Green Bay Packers and Los Angeles Rams head into Wednesday's Week 12 matchup with matching 6-4 records, but they arrive headed in opposite directions. The Rams have won four straight, and their defense has allowed three touchdowns in five games, the fewest in the NFL. Green Bay lost 30-10 at home to Minnesota in their last game. Both teams are still trying to find some identity and could best be described as playoff bubble teams at this stage of the season.

The Packers have a chance to close the gap on Minnesota in the division with the Vikings' loss to San Francisco last Sunday night, whereas the Rams likely won't even catch their division rivals in that regard as the 49ers continue to widen the gap on them.

The Rams rebuilt their pass rush around Myles Garrett and a returning Aaron Donald and rank 2nd in defensive EPA/play. On offense they are missing Puka Nacua (out for the season) and Davante Adams (IR). Micah Parsons is cleared to make his 2026 debut for Green Bay.

Los Angeles went 12-5 and reached the NFC Championship Game, where they lost in a shootout to Seattle. The offense was the league's best by EPA/play (0.11, 1st), with 0.21 EPA per dropback and a 50.7% success rate. The defense ranked 11th in EPA/play allowed (-0.08). Then the Rams spent the offseason going all in on defense.

Edge Jared Verse was traded to Cleveland in the Myles Garrett deal.

OC Mike LaFleur left to become Arizona's head coach.

Assistant head coach Aubrey Pleasant was not retained.

Senior offensive assistant Alex Van Pelt left for Atlanta.

CB Cobie Durant and CB Derion Kendrick signed with Dallas.

OT Rob Havenstein and CB Darious Williams retired.

CB Roger McCreary, LB Troy Reeder, CB Ahkello Witherspoon, T D.J. Humphries and TE Nick Vannett also left.

TE Mark Redman was traded to Green Bay for a 2028 seventh-round pick.

Edge Myles Garrett was acquired from Cleveland for Verse, a 2027 first, a 2028 second and a conditional 2029 third.

CB Trent McDuffie was acquired from Kansas City for picks and signed a 4-year, $124M extension.

DT Aaron Donald came out of retirement on a 1-year, $20M deal.

CB Jaylen Watson signed for 3 years, $51M.

LB Grant Stuard signed for 2 years, $4.45M.

WR Tutu Atwell was reacquired from Miami.

QB Matthew Stafford re-signed for 1 year, $55M.

S Kamren Curl re-signed for 3 years, $36M.

TE Tyler Higbee re-signed for 2 years, $6M.

OC Nathan Scheelhaase was promoted from pass game coordinator.

Assistant head coach Kliff Kingsbury joined the staff.

Special teams coordinator Bubba Ventrone joined the staff.

QB Ty Simpson was a 1st-round pick (No. 13).

Chris Shula remains defensive coordinator, and Sean McVay still calls plays.

Touchdowns allowed, last five games: 3 (fewest in NFL)

W 34-6 at ARI: the line had its best game despite losing guard Steve Avila, center Coleman Shelton and backup Dylan McMahon. McVay used 20 personnel 17 times with a 59% success rate.

Protect Love against the best front he'll see this season. Garrett, Donald, Kobie Turner (back from a groin injury), Byron Young and Braden Fiske give the Rams five real pass rushers. Love throws about 36 times a game, and the Packers can't run the ball, so they have to win with quick throws and help for the tackles.

Keep taking deep shots. Love leads the NFL in 20-plus-yard completions, and Christian Watson (916 yards) and Matthew Golden (853 yards) have combined for over 1,700 yards. A secondary built around McDuffie and Watson will make those throws hard, but the big plays are Green Bay's offense.

Make Stafford beat you without his best receiver. With Nacua and Adams out, Higbee is the Rams' leading receiver with only 304 yards. Stafford has thrown 10 interceptions. Parsons has 3 sacks in two career games against Stafford, and Jonathan Gannon should test a patched-up offensive line.

Don't get out-schemed in the run game. The Rams rank about 6th in EPA per rush and succeed on 49% of plays, 3rd in the league. McVay is now leaning on two-back looks, so stopping the run on early downs keeps Stafford in obvious passing downs.

Win the margins. At 44, this is the lowest total of any Packers preview this season. Against a defense this good, field position, takeaways and red-zone touchdowns instead of field goals will decide it.

Injuries to watch: For Los Angeles, Puka Nacua and TE Terrance Ferguson are out for the season, Davante Adams is on IR, and guards Steve Avila and Coleman Shelton are out. For Green Bay, Micah Parsons is expected to make his season debut.

#2850. Rookie Yaxel Lendeborg's maturation with Steph Curry out evaluated by Steve Kerr

Rookie Yaxel Lendeborg's maturation with Steph Curry out evaluated by Steve Kerr originally appeared on NBC Sports Bay Area

The Warriors are in the midst of a rough stretch.

Golden State started the 2026-27 NBA season at 0-3 before going on an eight-game winning streak. But star guard Steph Curry suffered an injury in a Nov. 11 game against the Houston Rockets, and the team lost five straight before beating the Boston Celtics on Sunday at Chase Center.

The Warriors need to get by without Curry, forcing players like rookie Yaxel Lendeborg to step up. The former Michigan Wolverine star's debut NBA season has been a mixed bag, but he has shown promise.

Coach Steve Kerr joined 95.7 The Game's "Willard & Dibs" on Tuesday and evaluated Lendeborg's maturation throughout the season, especially in Curry's absence.

"It's a great learning process for him," Kerr told Mark Willard and Dan Dibley. "It's very different when Steph's not out there and he's going through it in terms of having the best defenders on him and having defenses prepared for him. So it's a different scenario."

Lendeborg, who has played in all 17 games so far this season, was added to the injury report with an illness ahead of Wednesday night's game against the Sacramento Kings. He missed Tuesday's practice and while Kerr is hopeful he recovers in time to contribute, the team is taking a cautious approach.

"We don't know," Kerr told Willard and Dibley. "He missed practice [Tuesday]; the flu bug is going around so we're not exactly sure what's going to happen but hopefully we have him. We're going to need him but we'll wait and see for tomorrow [Wednesday]."

Lendeborg, the No. 11 overall pick in the 2026 NBA Draft, is averaging 19.2 points, 7.4 rebounds and 3.6 assists per game and is climbing in the NBA Rookie of the Year odds. He was a focal point on Michigan's national championship team last season.

Lendeborg is 24 and on the older side for a rookie but he entered the league as a more polished prospect. He played six seasons of college basketball — three at Arizona Western College, two at UAB and one at Michigan.

The Warriors and Kings face off in the NBA Cup Group Stage at 7 p.m. PT at Chase Center on NBC Sports Bay Area. Golden State is 1-1 in the tournament, while Sacramento is 0-3.

#2846. Furious second half rally falls short, and Wizards lose to Celtics

At halftime, I got started on an article about the Wizards based on a question from a friend. Because who wanted to read about the Wizards getting demolished? Again. I didn't want to write about another blowout. But, I decided to give it a few minutes into the second half before focusing on the other topic, and voila — the Wizards rallied.

The first half was putrid. The Wizards' offensive rating (points per possession x 100) was a woeful 93. Their defensive rating was a disgraceful 154. The Celtics hit 15-26 from three, good for 57.7%. Washington trailed, 80-49. Those 80 points were the most Boston has scored in a half this season. Folks, when the Celtics lost to the Golden State Warriors on Sunday, they scored 87 points total. This was a butt-kicking.

In the second half, the script flipped. Washington's offnesive rating was a superb 137. Their defensive rating was an excellent 104. And they whittled Boston's lead to as little as five with just over three minutes to play.

Alas, they couldn't close the deal, Boston hit some shots late, and the final margin ballooned back to 15.

Now, no one should get too chesty about the second half defense. Washington's overall effort seemed stronger, but Boston still got open looks. A lot of the better result was a matter of Boston missing shots they hit in the first — 7-25 from three in the third and fourth quarters vs. 15-26 in the first two.

The bright side is that Washington played hard in the second half after getting paddled in the first. This is a franchise that has a long tradition of giving up when the going gets tough, and last night they didn't.

I'm still not sure why Reggie Miller persists as a lead analyst in these games. He's a likable character, and he sometimes makes good points about the game. He also says things that have…well…let's call it a tenuous connection with reality. Last night, for example, he talked about how the Wizards should be better than they are (debatable, but that's his opinion, so whatever) and went on to say, "…they should be competing for the play-in…" uttered in a tone that suggested they weren't. Except, the Wizards entered the night ninth in the Eastern Conference, which means THEY'D BE IN THE PLAY-IN.

The final possession of the first quarter is an illustration of why it's so tough to give up on Trae Young. He drove against stout pressure from Boston's Hugo Gonzalez, seemed to be going up for a floater, before spotting a cutting Kyshawn George, whereupon he looked a defender out of position and then tossed a perfect lob for a George dunk.

Interesting to see Anthony Davis play the entire first quarter. Unfortunately, when he went to the bench, the game spiraled.

About the nine-minute mark of the second quarter, there as a defensive possession sure to get some time at the team's next film session. A whole mess of Wizards defenders got beat on a Paul George drive. Bilal Coulibaly spotted it from the weakside, teleported across the lane to help, and forced a George kickout pass to the weakside. Coulibaly looked back at his man (Peyton Pritchard) and was clearly startled to see that none of his teammates was even attempting a rotation. Coulibaly sprinted into a closeout, but Pritchard nailed the wide-open three.

On that same play, Deandre Ayton got eliminated from the play by a Derrick White screen. I can't remember the last time I saw a seven-footer show so little fight when getting screened. By no coincidence, Ayton did not make a second half appearance.

Another second quarter defensive mishap — Ayton helped at the three-point line for reasons that were not entirely clear. For reasons that were even less clear, AJ Dybantsa sprinted up from the low block, cut between Ayton and a Celtics player, and contested a three-point attempt, which missed. That left the lane open for Luka Garza to stroll down the lane for the putback. Just to be clear, Ayton should not have been helping in that spot — at most, he should have hedged and recovered to Garza or the paint. Dybantsa should not have been closing out — he should have either stayed put on the block or rotated onto Garza.

2:07 of the second quarter — Dybantsa had a highlight reel dunk. This kid!

At about the two-minute mark, more infuriating defense. On this play, Tatum got a dunk off a basic backscreen that got him a step on Dybantsa. The problem: Young was unaware his man was screening, didn't call out the screen, and then didn't pick up Tatum. The result: Tatum jogged into a dunk.

In the third quarter, there was more Young defensive cluelessness. Young's man set an off-ball brush screen on George's man. Young didn't notice his man was screening, didn't call it out, and didn't switch. Another easy at-rim score. George looked bad, but that was on Young.

At 8:41 of the third quarter, the Wizards tried zone. Sam Hauser walked up to the top of the circle from inside the paint. Davis had to follow because Hauser is a shooting threat. Meanwhile, Boston ran a pick-and-roll action up top, Neemias Queta rolled hard, which got him open because Dybantsa hesitated on the switch. Add in George rotating late from the strongside corner, and it was an easy at-rim finish for Queta.

Late in the third quarter, Boston followed the NBA-wide plan and conceded three-point attempts to Coulibaly. Last night, Coulibaly hit two in a row. It'd be nice if that somehow continued.

Earlier I gave an example of why it's tough to give up on Young. Around the 10-minute mark of the fourth quarter, Young gave an example of why you might want to. It was a terrible possession that consisted mostly of Young dribblimg around making moves, completely unable to shake Baylor Scheierman. He finally jacked up a long stepback two, which he missed.

With about 2:29 to play, Tatum was working on Khris Middleton, who was holding up in the post. Young stood in no-man's land for a few seconds before weakly jumping in to double for no discernible reason — Tatum was moving away from the basket, Middleton was in good position, and the shot clock was running down. That left Scheierman wide open at the three-point line. Good shooter that he is, he hit the shot. This was an example of needlessly helping from one pass away.

One last defensive blunder — with 1:16 remaining, Scheierman got another wide open three, which he made. Young was on him to start the possession. He seemed to think he was switching…onto Tatum…when Scheierman set a ghost screen. Dybantsa, who was on Tatum, seemed unaware of a switch (perhaps because there was no communication), and Scheierman got left alone.

Below are the four factors that decide wins and losses in basketball — shooting (efg), rebounding (offensive rebounds), ball handling (turnovers), fouling (free throws made).

eFG% (effective field goal percentage, which accounts for the three-point shot)

TOV% (turnover percentage — turnovers divided by possessions)

FTM/FGA (free throws made divided by field goal attempts)

PPA is my overall production metric, which credits players for things they do that help a team win (scoring, rebounding, playmaking, defending) and dings them for things that hurt (missed shots, turnovers, bad defense, fouls).

PPA is a per possession metric designed for larger data sets. In small sample sizes, the numbers can get weird. In PPA, 100 is average, higher is better and replacement level is 45. For a single game, replacement level isn't much use, and I reiterate the caution about small samples sometimes producing weird results.

POSS is the number of possessions each player was on the floor in this game.

ORTG = offensive rating, which is points produced per individual possessions x 100. League average so far this season is listed in the Four Factors table above. Points produced is not the same as points scored. It includes the value of assists and offensive rebounds, as well as sharing credit when receiving an assist.

USG = offensive usage rate. Average is 20%. Median last season was 17.7%.

ORTG and USG are versions of stats created by former Wizards assistant coach Dean Oliver and modified by me. ORTG is an efficiency measure that accounts for the value of shooting, offensive rebounds, assists and turnovers. USG includes shooting from the floor and free throw line, offensive rebounds, assists and turnovers.

+PTS = "Plus Points" is a measure of the points gained or lost by each player based on their efficiency in this game compared to league average efficiency on the same number of possessions. A player with an offensive rating (points produced per possession x 100) of 100 who uses 20 possessions would produce 20 points. If the league average efficiency is 115, the league — on average — would produced 23.0 points in the same 20 possessions. So, the player in this hypothetical would have a +PTS score of -3.0.

Players are sorted by total production in the game.

For release at 10:00 a.m. EDT Share

The Federal Reserve Board on Friday released the results of the 2025 Survey of Consumer Finances, which provides the public and policymakers with detailed insights into the economic condition of American families.

The data provide a representative picture of what Americans own, how and how much they borrow, and how they bank. The current version of the survey has been undertaken every three years since 1989 and provides the public with an extensive time series of thorough information on the economic conditions of American families. Alongside the survey data, the Board issued its summary report entitled Changes in U.S. Family Finances from 2022 to 2025 (PDF), which examines changes in income, net worth, assets, debt, and financial vulnerability.

Key findings from the 2025 survey include:

In addition to the summary report, an interactive chartbook displaying changes in these financial characteristics over time is available here. Additional data from the survey, including extensive data examining other economic conditions of American families, is made available to researchers and the public here.

The survey is conducted for the Board by NORC, a social science research organization at the University of Chicago. Participants in the study are chosen at random from 119 geographic areas, including metropolitan areas and rural counties across the United States, using scientific sampling procedures that aim to produce a sample representative of American families.

#2843. Top Wall Street analysts prefer these 3 dividend stocks for stable income

Global stock markets remain volatile amid persistent geopolitical tensions and concerns about the sustainability of AI trade. Given this backdrop, investors seeking steady income can add dividend-paying stocks to their portfolios.

Tracking recommendations from top Wall Street analysts can help investors pick attractive stocks, as these experts assess a company's ability to pay dividends consistently.

Here are three dividend-paying stocks that are highlighted by Wall Street's top pros, as tracked by TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

Integrated oil and gas giant Chevron (CVX) is a dividend king. The company has increased its dividend for 39 consecutive years. At a quarterly dividend of $1.78 per share (annualized dividend of $7.12 per share), CVX offers a yield of 3.14%. Chevron will announce its third-quarter results on October 30.

In an earnings preview note on U.S. oil majors, Goldman Sachs analyst Neil Mehta reiterated a buy rating on Chevron with a price target of $241. The analyst is focused on the company's international growth prospects across Latin America, the Middle East, West Africa, and the Eastern Mediterranean.

Specifically, Mehta highlighted management's commentary on solid execution in Venezuela, with gross production expected to more than double to nearly 600 kbd (kilo barrels per day) by 2031 via three joint ventures. He also noted a recent oil and gas condensate discovery in offshore Angola and is awaiting commercial updates on the West Qurna 2 and Nasiriyah projects in Iraq.

Furthermore, Mehta expects CVX to report improved productivity and capital efficiency across domestic shale assets. He expects the improvement mainly in the Permian Basin, where Chevron projects advanced chemical treatments to drive about a 10% recovery uplift in new wells and bring down capital spend per barrel by about 25% year-over-year. Overall, Mehta expects Chevron to report Q3 2026 EPS of $4.95, above the consensus of $4.89.

"While unfavorable timing effects and volatile commodity prices may present quarterly earnings headwinds, we highlight healthy U.S. refining margins and strong asset utilization as partial offsets," said Mehta.

Mehta ranks No. 381 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 63% of the time, delivering an average return of 12%. See Chevron KPIs on TipRanks.

Kinetik Holdings (KNTK) is a fully integrated, pure-play, midstream company operating in the Delaware Basin. Earlier this month, the company declared a dividend of $0.81 per share, payable on October 30, 2026. With an annualized dividend of $3.24 per share, KNTK offers a yield of 5.82%. Kinetik will announce its Q3 earnings on November 4, with a conference call scheduled the next day.

Ahead of Q3 2026 earnings, Goldman Sachs analyst John Mackay reiterated a buy rating on Kinetik stock and slightly raised his price target to $58 from $57. He expects the company to report Q3 2026 EBITDA of $279 million, higher than the Street's expectation of $270 million but lower than the second quarter's EBITDA of $281 million.

The 5-star analyst expects Kinetik's Q3 performance to gain from higher gas volumes after notable curtailments in the second quarter. Mackay also expects the quarter to benefit from higher production and steady commodity margins, even though some lower-margin contracts and weaker Waha spread marketing gains could weigh on results.

Overall, Mackay increased his Q3 2026 estimates to reflect higher volumes, driven by rapid Permian egress pipeline commissioning, stronger throughput at Kings Landing I, and higher M2M commodity prices, offset by increased costs.

"As natural gas takeaway constraints ease across the Permian, investor focus centers on KNTK's YE26 volume trajectory and durability of its multi-year growth," said Mackay.

Mackay ranks No. 569 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 64% of the time, delivering an average return of 16.9%. See Kinetik's Ownership Structure on TipRanks.

Moving to Crescent Energy (CRGY), an exploration and production company with activities focused in the Eagle Ford, Permian, and Uinta Basins. With a quarterly dividend of $0.12 per share (annualized dividend of $0.48 per share), CRGY's dividend yield stands at 3.56%. Earlier this month, Crescent announced an agreement to acquire Eagle Ford assets from Devon Energy (DVN).

Heading into Q3 earnings on Nov. 2, Evercore analyst Stephen Richardson reiterated a buy rating on Crescent stock and slightly lowered the price target to $17 from $18, citing dilution from new shares.

"CRGY's recent acquisition of DVN's Eagle Ford assets marks a strategic, growth-oriented move, potentially enhancing cash flow performance," said Richardson.

The 5-star analyst noted that the acquisition of the Eagle Ford assets at a net purchase price of $3.85 billion will add about 68 MBoe/d of net production and over 600 net Tier 1 locations. Richardson added that a solid track record of synergies from past deals in the Eagle Ford region increases confidence about Crescent achieving efficiencies with this deal.

Additionally, Richardson noted that the financial implications of the acquisition seem impressive, with a projected increase in cash flow per share of about 9% for 2027 to 2029.

The analyst believes that Crescent is not just expanding its business through acquisitions but positioning itself as a more durable company, regardless of market conditions.

Richardson ranks No. 478 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 63% of the time, delivering an average return of 13.2%. See Crescent Energy Statistics on TipRanks.

#2839. Open Thread: A second generation of NBA players has taken over the league

Today's NBA is loaded with talent. Children today grow up competitive in the sport from a young age. For many, the familiarity begins at birth. Stephen Curry and Klay Thompson, two of the best shooters to play the game, both grew up on the hardwood. Curry's father Dell played sixteen seasons in the NBA. In 1994, he was the league's Sixth Man. Mychel Thompson, Klay's dad, was the #1 draft pick in 1978. He played thirteen seasons and picked up two rings with the Showtime-era Lakers.

Today, the Spurs have Dylan Harper, son of five-time NBA star Ron Harper. He was the second overall pick in 2025. Cameron Boozer, this year's third overall draft pick, is the son of 2-time NBA All-Star Carlos Boozer. But the ties of current players whose fathers played in the NBA is a much deeper well.

Current NBA players including Jalen Brunson, Andrew Wiggins, Domantas Sabonis, Devin Booker, Jaren Jackson, Jr., Al Horford, Jerami Grant, Luke Kornet, Gary Payton II, Tim Hardaway, Jr., Scotty Pippen, Jr., Jabari Smith, Jr., Gary Trent, Jr., Ron Harper, Jr., Trayce Jackson-Davis, Cameron Carr, Devin Carter, Reed Sheppard, Nic Claxton, Larry Nance, Jr. and his brother Pete, Jase Richardson, Jabari Walker, Darius Garland, and Bronny James all have fathers who played (or in Bronny's case still play) in the NBA.

And if you look at a younger generation, you see the trend is most likely to comnintue:

With so many of today's NBA stars growing up around the game through their fathers, it's a good time to ask — could the children of the San Antonio Spurs classic Big 3 line-up join forces someday in the NBA?

Tim Duncan's son Draven is nineteen and currently playing for Pomona-Pitzer. If that name sounds familiar, Gregg Popovich served as the head coach of their basketball program from 1979-1987. And a former basketball player by the name of Mike Budenholzer also belongs to the alma mater's Hall of Fame.

Recently, Manu Ginobili's twins Dante and Nico, age sixteen, were seen shooting hoops for their high school:

The duo, who currently play for Saint Mary's Hall, are in their junior year. Depending on their talent, they could be NBA prospects in a couple of years.

Finally, Tony Parker's sons Josh and Liam are age 12 and 10 respectively. While neither have shown an interest in basketball (and Parker does his best to keep them out of the public eye), one has to believe that the apples don't fall far from the tree. A player as talented as Parker has to have passed on some of those basketball jeans. But then again, not every offspring gets their father's talent for the game…just ask Michael Jordan.

But, just for fun… imagine a Spurs lineup ten years from now featuring the next generation of Duncan, Ginobilis, and Parkers. A San Antonio Spurs being elevated by the sons of the three stars who helped bring four championships together. I realize that even if all five kids grew up with the skills to be in the NBA, it would be a long shot to get even three of them on one team. After all, there are twenty-nine other teams who'd forbid that from ever happenin….again.

Welcome to the Thread. Join in the conversation, start your own discussion, and share your thoughts. This is the Spurs community, your Spurs community. Thanks for being here.

Our community guidelines apply which should remind everyone to be cool, avoid personal attacks, not to troll and to watch the language.

#2837. Robinhood shares fall as crypto slump weighs on profits, offsetting gains in new businesses

Robinhood shares were down around 8% in pre-market trading on Wednesday as investors digested weaker-than-expected third-quarter earnings despite growth in options trading and prediction markets.

Here's how the brokerage performed in its quarter ended Sept. 30, compared with Wall Street estimates, based on a survey of analysts by LSEG:

Robinhood's net income fell to $468 million, or 51 cents per share, from $556 million, or 61 cents per share, a year ago. Revenue represented a 12% increase from $1.27 billion in the same quarter last year.

Despite that gain, transaction-based revenue of $867 million came in below Wall Street estimates from StreetAccount of $879 million, putting focus on whether Robinhood can sustain momentum across its trading businesses and reduce its reliance on volatile market activity.

Robinhood has been diversifying its business as it tries to expand its brokerage app into a broader financial services platform. Options trading and event contracts have become increasingly important sources of transaction revenue, while the company has continued to invest in wealth management, subscriptions and other offerings.

Transaction-based revenue grew 19% from the same period last year to $867 million, driven primarily by a more than 500% increase in event contracts revenue. Additionally, options revenue grew 31% to $399 million and equities revenue rose 41% to $121 million.

Those gains were partially offset by cryptocurrency revenue, which fell 55% to $120 million.

Chief Financial Officer Shiv Verma told CNBC event contracts on crypto movements did well in the quarter, offering investors another way to express a view on crypto despite the slump in prices and spot trading.

Net deposits on the Robinhood platform totaled $15.7 billion for the quarter, equal to a 17% annualized growth rate based on total platform assets at the end of Q2 2026. Over the past year, net deposits totaled $71 billion, representing 21% growth compared with total platform assets at the end of Q3 2025.

—With reporting from CNBC's Robert Hum and Kate Rooney

The Committee decided to maintain the target range for the federal funds rate at 3-3/4 to 4 percent, in support of the Federal Reserve's dual mandate. The Committee is continuing its policy of maintaining ample reserves in the banking system.

#2827. Matisse Thybulle Is the Lakers’ Secret Weapon

One of the biggest keys to the Lakers' stupendous 15-3 start has been defensive standout Matisse Thybulle.

The Lakers acquired Thybulle over last offseason on a $3.3 million deal. So far, he has been worth every penny.

Eighteen games in, the Lakers are hovering near the top 10 in defensive rating, thanks in part to them ranking first in steals per game and second in blocks.

A large part of their success on that end of the floor is Thybulle's defensive prowess.

Against the Charlotte Hornets on Nov. 17, Thybulle's defense helped L.A. win in overtime. With 11.5 seconds left in the game, the Lakers led 117-114. Hornets guard Royce O'Neale tried to inbound the ball when Thybulle flew in for a deflection that sealed the victory.

On a night when Quentin Grimes exploded for 47 points, Thybulle was the unsung hero. Making big defensive plays is nothing new for the former Naismith Defensive Player of the Year.

He ranks second in steals per game in the NBA (among those who have played at least five games), and boasts the most games with at least two steals and one block. He is on pace to be the first Laker ever to average two steals and a block for a season.

Thybulle's defensive tendencies impressed his teammates before the season even started. Back in training camp, rookie Cameron Carr named Matisse as the player who stood out the most, touting his defense as "ridiculous." With each game, Thybulle has continued to amaze.

"Honestly, he has those instincts of getting to the ball; it's like 'What, how did he get there?'" said teammate Collin Sexton. "So I think just being out there with him, you have to communicate with him; sometimes he communicates with you as well, like, 'I got it. I'm gonna get the steal,' and he goes out there and does it. He's one of those players that has great instincts, but also can read the game before it happens on the defensive side, so it's cool to be out there."

Just as a coach gives star players the green light offensively, the same can be true for the defensive end. Thybulle's knack for jumping passing lanes and getting in gaps earns him more trust from coaches to take risks.

"It's no different than a highly unique offensive player and giving them freedom to be creative; you've got to be able to do it within our system," Redick said. "He's got the freedom to be himself, and we know he's going to make more good happen than bad."

A major part of Thybulle's resounding impact on the Lakers isn't just his defense; it is also his offense.

The 29-year-old got off to a slow start offensively this season; however, he has since found a rhythm. He is shooting 40.5% from three on the highest attempts of his career — 4.4. Injuries have previously hindered his playing time at his previous stop in Portland, where he played a combined 45 games over the last two seasons.

Thybulle has started all 18 games for the Lakers, and joins former Defensive Player of the Year Victor Wembanyama as the only players to rack up at least 15 blocks, 20 steals and 30 threes this season. The Spurs and Lakers are tied for the best record in the league, and will square off Friday for the first time this season.

Many thought the Lakers took a risk in signing Thybulle over the summer.

But just as he continues to prove on the basketball court, some gambles are worth taking. And he was a steal.

#2826. What does life after Kevin Durant look like for the Rockets?

Alperen Sengun is 24. Amen Thompson and Jabari Smith Jr. are both 23. Tari Eason is 25. Kevin Durant is…

So is Fred VanVleet, but that feels inconsequential. Depending on where you stand, the Houston Rockets either need to replace him with a more dynamic offensive guard, solidify a succession plan that puts Reed Sheppard in his place, or replace him with a comparable low-turnover, game-managing point guard.

No matter where you land on VanVleet, replacing him is not a pressing imperative. It will be relatively easy.

Durant is averaging a career-low in field goal attempts per game by a considerable margin. He's shooting 13.4 shots per game, and his next-lowest career mark was 16.0 in 2022-23 with the Phoenix Suns.

That's either intentional or serendipitous.

For reference, that's effectively the same as, for example, the Minnesota Timberwolves' Jaden McDaniels (13.5). Let's use him as an example. Here's the existential question the Rockets need to be asking themselves:

If they replaced Durant with McDaniels, right now, how much worse would they be?

That's the type of player they can acquire relatively easily. Not McDaniels specifically: he's the premium model. He'll likely never be a Rocket, and he's just an example. More broadly, with their assets, the Rockets could find a high-end three-and-D wing tomorrow.

The offense might not be as different as you'd think. In 2026-27, Durant has a 65.4% True Shooting% (TS%) to McDaniels' 63.8%. The most devious devil in the details is that there's no massive gap between the two in shot creation this year. In 2026-27, 60.2% of Durant's two-point field goals have been assisted, and 86.7% of his threes, compared to 55.7% on twos and 93.1% on threes for McDaniels.

The Rockets don't seem to need Durant to create offense at a high volume. The Rockets' 121.1 Offensive Rating is the best in the NBA. Surprisingly enough, defense has been an issue this year, as their 113.0 Defensive Rating ranks just 11th. Wait a minute:

Durant has been the best (and most expensive) decoy in the NBA in 2026-27. His gravity is important to Alperen Sengun's functionality as a hub, especially when so many of the team's actions revolve around Amen Thompson cutting while he's in the high post. That partly works because there's a guy on the perimeter that the defense absolutely cannot leave.

So, let's assume the Rockets don't need Durant's creation, but still need his gravity. This gets tougher. Defenses won't guard McDaniels the way they guard Durant. They would scramble to make sure that, say, Corey Kispert isn't open, but he can't defend well enough to find regular minutes anywhere.

We know the Rockets will lean defense. Suppose they draft the next McDaniels in 2027, or sign someone, or whatever: Imagine they bring in a high-end three-and-D who doesn't create much offense to replace Durant when he retires.

They will take a step back, but I propose that it won't be too substantial a step. I can live with that:

Fundamentally, that's what this boils down to. If the Rockets can stomach a quick fix, replacing Durant with a young(er), low-usage, high-impact wing should suffice. If not, they'll need to be star-hunting as Durant approaches the end of his career. By that logic, he'll need to be replaced both positionally and functionally, so they could get that low-usage wing and a star guard, or vice versa.

Only then can this room be elephant-free.

#2822. Federal Reserve Board announces pricing, effective January 1, 2027, for payment services the Federal Reserve Banks provide to banks and credit unions, and changes to streamline and adjust their check services

An official website of the United States Government

Official websites use .govA .gov website belongs to an official government organization in the United States.

Secure .gov websites use HTTPSA lock (LockLocked padlock icon) or https:// means you've safely connected to the .gov website. Share sensitive information only on official, secure websites.

The Federal Reserve, the central bank of the United States, provides the nation with a safe, flexible, and stable monetary and financial system.

Review of Monetary Policy Strategy, Tools, and Communications

Mergers, Acquisitions, and Other Applications

Financial Stability Coordination & Actions

Financial Market Utilities & Infrastructures

For release at 1:30 p.m. EST Share

The Federal Reserve Board on Thursday announced pricing, effective January 1, 2027, for payment services the Federal Reserve Banks provide to banks and credit unions, such as the clearing of checks, automated clearing house (ACH) transactions, instant payments, and wholesale payment and settlement services.

By law, the Federal Reserve must establish fees to recover the costs, including imputed costs, of providing payment services over the long run. The Federal Reserve expects to recover 101.5 percent of actual and imputed expenses in 2027, including the return on equity that would have been earned if a private-sector firm provided the services. Overall, price changes for 2027 will result in an estimated 1.4 percent average price increase for established, mature services.

In addition, the Federal Reserve Banks announced changes to streamline and adjust their check services. As described in the request for information on check services issued last year, the volume of checks processed by the Federal Reserve Banks is declining, but checks remain an important payment instrument for many consumers and businesses. The changes were informed by public feedback and ensure that the Federal Reserve Banks continue to provide check services efficiently. The adjustments include optimizing check processing windows during low-volume periods, increasing fees that banks and credit unions pay to process checks, and other operational updates. These changes do not impact consumers' and businesses' ability to pay with or receive checks and only impact how banks and credit unions process checks with Federal Reserve Banks.

The entire 2027 fee schedule for all of the Federal Reserve's payment services is published on FRBservices.org®. Additional information on the Federal Reserve's payments services, including service usage statistics, is available on the Board's website.

#2821. NFL's first 'Thanksgiving Eve' game stars Packers, Rams and U2

The NFL hosts its first-ever "Thanksgiving Eve" game when the Green Bay Packers take on the Los Angeles Rams with a special halftime show by the rock band U2.

The game is Wednesday night, Nov. 25, (6 p.m. MT/ 7 p.m. CT on Netflix) from SoFi Stadium in Inglewood, California, and is the start of a slate of NFL games over the Thanksgiving holiday.

Packers (6-4) and Rams (6-4) face off in a push for playoff positions.

NFL: Mexico NFL game special for Vikings RB Aaron Jones of El Paso

The NFL and Netflix are banking that bringing in a big-name musical act attracts viewers who otherwise might not tune in for a football game.

The special U2 halftime show will include a tribute to the late Dolly Parton, whose final vocal recording was the Bono duet "Torn," reported USA Today, which was granted access to the final full dress rehearsal of Netflix's "U2 Touchdown" on Tuesday, Nov. 24.

U2 already has a place in NFL history with the band's stirring halftime show at Super Bowl XXXVI in 2002, honoring the victims of the Sept. 11 terrorist attacks. U2 was also part of the emotional reopening of the Louisiana Superdome in 2006 following Hurricane Katrina.

The addition of a superstar musical artist can attract huge ratings for special NFL games.

On Christmas Day 2024, the "Beyoncé Bowl" halftime attracted more than 27 millions U.S. viewers during halftime of the Baltimore Ravens-Houston Texans game, surpassing the game's initially reported average audience of 24.3 million viewers, USA Today reported.

Green Bay Packers pass rusher Micah Parsons (formerly of the Dallas Cowboys) is expected to make his 2026 season debut against the Rams as he was added to the active roster after recovering from an ACL injury.

"That's what I'm excited for," Parson said, reported Field Level Media. "I came back for the greatest time of football, and that's November and December football. Let the show begin."

The Packers enter the game in last place in the NFC North, where the Chicago Bears and Detroit Lions are tied at 8-2 going into their head-to-head showdown on Thanksgiving.

College football: UTEP, NIU generates buzz for ESPN's Bottom 10 showdown

#2820. Aaron Rodgers to be released by Steelers, per reports

Aaron Rodgers' time with the Pittsburgh Steelers has about to officially come to an end following his benching and comments on Wednesday's episode of "The Pat McAfee Show."

According to Jordan Schultz and Pittsburgh Post-Gazette's Gerry Dulac, the team will be releasing the soon-to-be 43-year-old quarterback once his contract situation is finalized. Rodgers signed a one-year, $22.5 million deal in May.

The situation went from the Steelers announcing late Tuesday evening that Rodgers would be benched to him airing his feelings about Mike McCarthy less than 24 hours later.

On "McAfee," Rodgers claimed that McCarthy fell asleep and snored during a quarterbacks meeting after arriving late. He also said that the Steelers head coach broke down the team during halftime of Sunday's loss to the Philadelphia Eagles by saying, "Alright, 1-2-3-Packers!"

The four-time NFL MVP added that he found out he was being benched in favor of Mason Rudolph from Steelers' tight end Pat Freiermuth.

"I was talking to Muth and a couple of the guys, and he said, 'I can't believe they're doing this.' And I said, 'Doing what?' I didn't know," Rodgers said. "And he told me, and obviously, there had been a lot of things in motion at that point. I didn't hear from Mike [McCarthy]."

"Mike and I talked for a long time the last few days, and that word [benching] never came up."

What comes next for Rodgers? That's to be determined. He said on "McAfee" that the "ship has sailed" in regards to a return to the Steelers. Now, he will be free agent once he clear waivers.

Rodgers had already announced in May that the 2026 season would be his last in the NFL.

In 10 games this season, Rodgers has thrown for 2,241 yards, 16 touchdowns and 6 interceptions.

#2819. Top Wall Street analysts are bullish on these 3 stocks for the long haul

The ongoing earnings season, Middle East tensions and buzz ahead of the U.S. midterm elections have been keeping investors busy. Further, worries about elevated artificial intelligence spending are weighing on investor sentiment.

Investors looking beyond the short-term noise and focusing on long-term returns can track top Wall Street analysts to pick the right stocks, backed by strong research.

Here are three stocks favored by some of Wall Street's top pros, according to TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

This week's first stock pick is Datadog, an AI-powered observability and security platform. The company will announce its results for the third quarter on Nov. 4. Datadog is seeing strong demand for its offerings, thanks to cloud migration and rapid AI adoption. TipRanks' KPIs tool for DDOG shows the growth in its customer base over the past few years amid increasing market penetration and growing adoption of its products.

Heading into Q3 FY26 earnings, Bank of America analyst Koji Ikeda reiterated a buy rating on Datadog stock and raised his price target to $335 from $305. The analyst noted that industry checks and data indicate that infrastructure software demand is strengthening, with AI increasingly driving usage and growth.

Ikeda called Datadog and Atlassian his top picks, saying that companies that can show that AI is a tailwind to growth could see their valuation rerate, even at premium multiples. He added that the total addressable market for observability is growing faster than anticipated, driven by accelerated growth of enterprise AI and the rapid ramp of consumer AI agents.

The five-star analyst believes that focus will be on revenue growth, which he estimates to be 28.7% for Q3 FY26 versus the Street's consensus of 29.1%. Ikeda thinks that the market is underestimating the growth prospects of DDOG's security products. Meanwhile, he cautioned investors about concentration risk related to large customers.

"We think results will be supportive of our long-term thesis that Datadog is a strong share gainer in the rapidly expanding observability opportunity," said Ikeda.

Ikeda ranks No. 673 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 55% of the time, delivering an average return of 10.9%. See Datadog Statistics on TipRanks.

The next pick, semiconductor giant Nvidia, is also benefiting from AI-driven demand for its products. After attending Nvidia's GTC Berlin conference, Morgan Stanley analyst Joseph Moore reaffirmed a buy rating on NVDA stock with a price target of $300, calling it a top pick in the semiconductor space.

The five-star analyst said that the conference supported his thesis that Nvidia continues to effectively navigate bottlenecks in the supply chain, land/power/shell, and funding. In fact, Moore is "convinced that all 3 aspects are opportunities for NVDA."

While Nvidia's GTC Berlin offered no major updates, Moore noted the conference reflected that the company continues to add to its significant advantages heading into 2027. The analyst said that Vera Rubin is gaining momentum, though details on next year's Rubin Ultra were lighter than expected.

With demand outpacing supply, Moore said that focus is on closing the gap. In this regard, he highlighted the supply chain deals on substrate, packaging and testing. While the analyst continues to view TSMC 3 nm wafer supply as the biggest challenge, he expects it to ease over time.

Moore ranks No. 142 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 59% of the time, delivering an average return of 23.4%. See Nvidia Ownership Structure on TipRanks.

Aerospace company SpaceX recently announced solid third-quarter results, with its AI revenue surging to $7.56 billion from $813 million in the prior-year quarter.

Impressed by the results, Needham analyst Ryan Koontz reiterated a buy rating on SPCX stock with a price target of $250. Koontz highlighted that SpaceX's Q3 results significantly exceeded expectations, proving that the company's vertically integrated business model is driving massive scale.

Furthermore, Koontz emphasized the 195.2% sequential growth in SpaceX's AI revenue, fueled by wholesale spot compute deals. He noted management's increasing focus on its AI strategy, given that it is optimistic about adding 10 gigawatts of incremental capacity during 2027.

"With increasing confidence on Starship re-use, we meaningfully increase our estimates," said Koontz.

Koontz ranks No. 357 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 42% of the time, delivering an average return of 23.7%. See SpaceX Technical Analysis on TipRanks.

#2818. Agencies extend comment period for proposed third-party risk management guidance

An official website of the United States Government

Official websites use .govA .gov website belongs to an official government organization in the United States.

Secure .gov websites use HTTPSA lock (LockLocked padlock icon) or https:// means you've safely connected to the .gov website. Share sensitive information only on official, secure websites.

The Federal Reserve, the central bank of the United States, provides the nation with a safe, flexible, and stable monetary and financial system.

Review of Monetary Policy Strategy, Tools, and Communications

Mergers, Acquisitions, and Other Applications

Financial Stability Coordination & Actions

Financial Market Utilities & Infrastructures

For release at 4:15 p.m. EST Share

The Federal Deposit Insurance Corporation, the Federal Reserve Board, the National Credit Union Administration, and the Office of the Comptroller of the Currency today announced that they will extend, until December 16, the comment period for proposed guidance on managing risks associated with third-party relationships. Comments on the proposal were originally due November 16.

The proposed guidance reflects the agencies' supervisory experience and lessons learned from examining financial institutions' third-party risk management practices. It is intended to help banks and credit unions tailor their risk management practices to the risks associated with individual third-party relationships. The proposed guidance takes a principles-based approach and, like all supervisory guidance, is non-binding.

When finalized, the federal bank regulatory agencies plan to rescind existing third-party risk management guidance and replace it with the finalized guidance to promote consistency and prudent innovation in the banking industry. NCUA also plans to rescind its existing third-party risk management guidance and will conduct its own reviews to update related guidance as needed.

Also today, the Federal Reserve Board separately announced that it will extend, until December 16, the comment period on a proposed third-party risk management guide specifically for Federal Reserve-supervised traditional community banks.

Federal Register notice: Proposed Third-Party Risk Management Guide for Traditional Community Banking Organizations

Pittsburgh Steelers quarterback Aaron Rodgers (8) reacts after a game at Lincoln Financial Field in Philadelphia on Sunday, Nov. 22, 2026. IMAGN IMAGES via Reuters Connect

The Federal Reserve Board on Friday released results from a survey of senior financial officers at banks about their strategies and practices for managing reserve balances. The Senior Financial Officer Survey is used by the Board to obtain information about banks' reserve balance management strategies and practices, their expectations around the size of their balance sheets and deposit balances their activity in money markets, and their views on other matters, such as operations on weekends and holidays.

The survey was conducted in collaboration with the Federal Reserve Bank of New York between September 18, 2026, and September 28, 2026, and includes responses from banks that held around three-fourths of total banking system reserve balances at the time of the survey.

#2812. Federal Reserve announces College Fed Challenge winners

An official website of the United States Government

Official websites use .govA .gov website belongs to an official government organization in the United States.

Secure .gov websites use HTTPSA lock (LockLocked padlock icon) or https:// means you've safely connected to the .gov website. Share sensitive information only on official, secure websites.

The Federal Reserve, the central bank of the United States, provides the nation with a safe, flexible, and stable monetary and financial system.

Review of Monetary Policy Strategy, Tools, and Communications

Mergers, Acquisitions, and Other Applications

Financial Stability Coordination & Actions

Financial Market Utilities & Infrastructures

For release at 4:15 p.m. EST Share

The University of California, Los Angeles won the 23rd Annual National College Fed Challenge.

The University of California, Los Angeles (UCLA) won the 23rd annual national College Fed Challenge on Friday, a team competition that encourages undergraduate students to learn about the U.S. economy, monetary policymaking, and the role of the Federal Reserve System. Teams analyze economic and financial conditions and formulate a monetary policy recommendation, modeling the Federal Open Market Committee. The team, from Los Angeles, California, represented the San Francisco District and included Aarushi Maheshwari, Annika Chan, Atul Prasanth, Caitlyn Cox, Finn Karish, Eli Brooks (alternate), Kennedy Yang (alternate), Manasi Vartak (alternate), Ronojoy Borpujari (alternate), and Sahil Sudani (alternate). The team's advisers were Chris Surro and Ali Haider Ismail.

"I am very happy to congratulate the students who competed in this year's College Fed Challenge, especially the winners and finalists," said Federal Reserve Chairman Kevin Warsh. "They took on the same big questions we policymakers face. I'm impressed by their talent, their rigor, and their eagerness to think hard about monetary policy and the economy."

This year's College Fed Challenge included 141 schools from 34 states across the nation, the most in the competition's history. Teams submitted video presentations or participated in local competitions last month. Twenty-one semi-finalist teams then participated in question-and-answer sessions held earlier this month, and seven finalists were invited to the Federal Reserve Board for the national finals. The other national finalists were the University of Chicago in second place and the University of Maryland, College Park in third place. Teams with honorable mentions include Cleveland State University, Harvard College, Pennsylvania State University, and St. Lawrence University.

Teams were evaluated on their economic analysis, responses to the judges' questions, teamwork, and presentations.

#2811. Will Whitt Newbauer or Bowe Bentley be Oklahoma's starting QB in 2027?

The Oklahoma Sooners will have a major position to fill on their offense next football season. The Sooners will be one of many teams looking for a new starting quarterback.

Current starter, John Mateer, has had a stellar career. He's thrown for 8,713 yards, 64 touchdowns and 27 interceptions, while rushing for 1,744 yards and 28 scores. Mateer has been backed up by Whitt Newbauer and Bowe Bentley this season. Newbauer has played sparingly in Norman, completing just 3-of-5 passes for 12 yards in two seasons. He came after one season at Mercer, completing 56.4% of his passes for 1,398 yards, 12 touchdowns and four picks. Bentley is a true freshman, but was a top-10 quarterback in the 2026 class.

Head coach Brent Venables said both guys will get the chance at starting next season.

"Certainly there's a chance for that," Venables said. "They've had a good offseason, in the season. You get the practice reps that they share. We've tried to take some things off of John over the course of the week, just to be smart with his arm. They've been able to, a couple of times a week, get even more reps than what they'd normally get."

Venables' comments indicate that Oklahoma is already working to prepare both quarterbacks for a larger role. The additional practice reps should help them develop while giving the coaching staff a better look at what each can bring to the position. Replacing a quarterback with Mateer's production will be a big challenge, but the Sooners have two young options who will have an opportunity to make their case when the competition begins.

#2809. Agencies announce dollar thresholds for smaller loan exemption from appraisal requirements for higher-priced mortgage loans

An official website of the United States Government

Official websites use .govA .gov website belongs to an official government organization in the United States.

Secure .gov websites use HTTPSA lock (LockLocked padlock icon) or https:// means you've safely connected to the .gov website. Share sensitive information only on official, secure websites.

The Federal Reserve, the central bank of the United States, provides the nation with a safe, flexible, and stable monetary and financial system.

Review of Monetary Policy Strategy, Tools, and Communications

Mergers, Acquisitions, and Other Applications

Financial Stability Coordination & Actions

Financial Market Utilities & Infrastructures

For release at 3:00 p.m. EST Share

The Consumer Financial Protection Bureau, the Federal Reserve Board, and the Office of the Comptroller of the Currency today announced that the 2027 threshold for higher-priced mortgage loans that are subject to special appraisal requirements will increase from $34,200 to $35,500.

The threshold amount will be effective January 1, 2027, and is based on the 3.9 percent annual percentage increase in the Consumer Price Index for Urban Wage Earners and Clerical Workers, known as CPI-W, as of June 1, 2026.

The Dodd-Frank Act added special appraisal requirements for higher-priced mortgage loans to the Truth in Lending Act, including that creditors obtain a written appraisal based on a physical visit to the interior of the home before making a higher-priced mortgage loan. The rules implementing these requirements contain an exemption for loans at or below a threshold amount that is adjusted annually to reflect CPI-W increases.
