#2808. Who soared in 2026? Biggest movers in the Official World Golf Ranking

Although a handful of tournaments will still impact the Official World Golf Ranking before the end of the calendar year, with the conclusion of the PGA Tour season last week at the RSM Classic, this week offers a perfect time to gain some perspective on the players who made the biggest jumps and those who took the biggest tumbles in the ranking over the last 11 months.

While Scottie Scheffler and Rory McIlroy held down the same two spots atop the ranking, there were some big movers and shakers underneath the dynamic duo.

Here's a look at a few of the biggest climbers thus far in 2026:

End 2025: 46Current OWGR: 5How come: After missing the cut at the PGA Championship, Clark caught fire, winning the CJ Cup Byron Nelson and the U.S. Open as he put together a stretch of six top-15 finishes in a row. He added a win at the BMW Championship during the FedEx Cup playoffs and is poised for big things in 2027. He credited a new mental health outlook and even launched an app in the field.

End 2025: 84Current OWGR: 6How come: This one's a little easier to figure. Rahm was just as good in '26 as he was in '25 (he won the league's overall points standings in each season), but since LIV Golf started receiving OWGR points (for top-10 finishes) in 2026, Rahm easily jumped up the rankings over the course of the season. He's been atop the ranking for a total of 52 weeks during his career.

End 2025: 81Current OWGR: 17How come: Perhaps the most unlikely of this group, Bridgeman started and ended the season on fire, with a better-than-average middle portion of the schedule. He opened the campaign with eight straight top-20 finishes, including a victory at the Genesis Invitational. After falling back in the pack a bit, he posted four top-10 showings at season's end, including a T-4 at the Tour Championship, a win in Asheville and a T-2 at the Baycurrent. How'd he do it? He leads the PGA Tour in Strokes Gained: Putting.

The OWGR system is calculated by awarding points from eligible tours and events to participating players over a rolling two-year period. Players are ranked weekly based on the points they earn, divided by the number of events they played, to reach an average total. Points hold their full value for a 13-week period and are then reduced in equal decrements. The current ranking for the Top 50 golfers in the world appears below.

OK, now here's a look at the entire top 50 as of Nov. 25.

#2807. Agencies announce dollar thresholds for applicability of truth in lending and consumer leasing rules for consumer credit and lease transactions

An official website of the United States Government

Official websites use .govA .gov website belongs to an official government organization in the United States.

Secure .gov websites use HTTPSA lock (LockLocked padlock icon) or https:// means you've safely connected to the .gov website. Share sensitive information only on official, secure websites.

The Federal Reserve, the central bank of the United States, provides the nation with a safe, flexible, and stable monetary and financial system.

Review of Monetary Policy Strategy, Tools, and Communications

Mergers, Acquisitions, and Other Applications

Financial Stability Coordination & Actions

Financial Market Utilities & Infrastructures

For release at 3:00 p.m. EST Share

The Federal Reserve Board and the Consumer Financial Protection Bureau today announced the dollar thresholds used to determine whether certain consumer credit and lease transactions in 2027 are subject to certain protections under Regulation Z (Truth in Lending) and Regulation M (Consumer Leasing).

By law, the agencies are required to adjust the thresholds annually based on the annual percentage increase in the Consumer Price Index for Urban Wage Earners and Clerical Workers, known as CPI-W. Transactions at or below the thresholds are subject to the protections of the regulations.

Specifically, based on the 3.9 percent annual percentage increase in the CPI-W as of June 1, 2026, Regulation Z and Regulation M generally will apply to consumer credit transactions and consumer leases of $76,300 or less in 2027. However, private education loans and loans secured by real property, such as mortgages, are subject to Regulation Z regardless of the amount of the loan.

Federal Register notice: Consumer Leasing (Regulation M) (PDF)

Federal Register notice: Truth in Lending (Regulation Z) (PDF)

#2806. Top Wall Street analysts like these 3 dividend stocks for consistent income

Democrats' midterm election win has put the artificial intelligence trade in question, as voters clearly expressed opposition to the rapid buildout of data centers. The stock market could remain volatile amid AI trade concerns, macro uncertainties, and geopolitical tensions.

In this scenario, investors can add dividend-paying stocks to their portfolios to earn steady income. However, the extensive universe of dividend stocks can make selection challenging. Top Wall Street analysts' recommendations can help investors shortlist attractive dividend stocks, as these experts assign ratings after in-depth financial analysis.

Here are three dividend-paying stocks that are highlighted by Wall Street's top pros, as tracked by TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

Viper Energy (VNOM) is a subsidiary of Diamondback and owns and acquires mineral and royalty interests, primarily in the Permian Basin. The company reported upbeat third-quarter earnings, supported by recent acquisitions and continued development activity from Diamondback and third-party operators. Viper announced a base cash dividend of 50 cents per share for Q3 2026, payable on Nov. 19. Based on the base and variable dividends announced over the past 12 months, VNOM's dividend yield stands at 5.7%.

Following the Q3 results, TD Cowen analyst Aaron Bilkoski reiterated a buy rating on Viper Energy stock with a price target of $61. The analyst noted that the company delivered solid Q3 results, with production, including oil and natural gas liquids, exceeding both TD Cowen and consensus estimates.

The 5-star analyst highlighted that the key update was the announcement of two royalty acquisitions for $809 million in cash, including deferred consideration, and $82 million of newly issued shares.

Given the recently announced base dividend hike, Bilkoski expects Viper to direct incremental free cash flow toward buybacks. While the acquisitions increase leverage, the analyst believes that Viper has enough capacity for opportunistic buybacks.

Bilkoski noted that Viper has historically delivered one of the highest production per share growth profiles in his royalty coverage, but still trades at a notable discount to the royalty peer average and the company's historical FCF yield range. "In our view, its above-average growth warrants a higher valuation," said the analyst.

Bilkoski ranks No. 525 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 57% of the time, delivering an average return of 12.80%. See Viper Energy Stock Buybacks on TipRanks.

Exxon Mobil (XOM) reported robust Q3 2026 profits, as its U.S. refineries boosted production to capitalize on the high diesel fuel prices sparked by the conflict in the Middle East. The oil and gas giant announced a quarterly dividend of $1.07 per share, payable on Dec. 10, reflecting a 3.9% increase. This marks Exxon's 44th consecutive year of dividend increases. At an annualized dividend of $4.28 per share, XOM offers a yield of 2.53%.

Impressed by the Q3 print, TD Cowen analyst Jason Gabelman reiterated a buy rating on Exxon stock and raised his price target to $193 from $180 to reflect higher downstream earnings, with the expectation of increased mid-cycle margins.

Gabelman noted that the strength in Exxon's refining business continues to offset Middle East-related headwinds. He added that the company increased its ownership in LNG projects, which should reduce its Middle East concentration over time from 75% to 50% and lower the likelihood of diversification-focused mergers and acquisitions. Beyond LNG, Gabelman expects Exxon's growth in the 2030s to come from Guyana, Kashagan, Permian, and possibly Venezuela.

Gabelman noted that Exxon announced a dividend hike of nearly 4% and plans to maintain buybacks at $20 billion for 2027. "The decision to maintain reduces the pro-cyclicality nature of the buyback," said the analyst.

Gabelman ranks No. 347 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 66% of the time, delivering an average return of 16.90%. See Exxon Mobil Ownership Structure on TipRanks.

Williams (WMB), which owns and operates energy infrastructure to deliver natural gas, reported strong third-quarter results. The company announced a quarterly dividend of $0.525 per share, or $2.10 per share annualized, payable on Dec. 28. WMB offers a dividend yield of 3.09%.

In reaction to the Q3 2026 results, CIBC analyst Robert Catellier reaffirmed a buy rating on Williams stock with a price target of $82. The analyst noted that Q3 results were essentially in line with expectations and reflected continued strength in execution across WMB's infrastructure portfolio.

Catellier said that Williams' Q3 performance was driven by higher transmission, storage, and power generation revenues, as well as contributions from the Momentum Midstream acquisition.

He noted that Williams maintained its full-year EBITDA guidance of $8.3 billion to $8.5 billion. In fact, results are tracking toward the higher end of the guidance range, backed by contributions from recently completed projects and the Momentum Midstream deal. WMB continues to target long-term EBITDA compound annual growth rate of at least 11% through 2030.

Catellier expects WMB's long-term targets to be supported by incremental transmission and storage opportunities, including Transco's Southeast Supply Enhancement II project and an 84 MMcf/d expansion of the Garden Connector project.

Catellier ranks No. 667 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 66% of the time, delivering an average return of 9.60%. See Williams Technical Analysis on TipRanks.

#2805. Milwaukee Bucks vs. Brooklyn Nets Preview & Game Thread: Start stackin’ wins!

Fresh off their second win of the season and with the 13-game losing streak in the rearview mirror, the Milwaukee Bucks will try to make it two in a row tonight as they host the Brooklyn Nets at home in what will also be their third NBA Cup game. The Nets are slightly ahead of the Bucks in the standings with three wins themselves, though frustrations have been mounting for Julius Randle and company. Brooklyn is coming off a 142-116 blowout loss in Minnesota, where Mikel Brown Jr. got his first start of the season and put up 17 points and seven assists on 6/12 shooting.

While the Nets have one more win than the Bucks, things have arguably been bleaker for them over the last two weeks; during that stretch, they have the worst defense in the league, averaging 130.9 points per 100 possessions. They are currently on a five-game losing skid, and four of those losses have been by double digits, including a 30-point rout against Chicago; in that game, fans saw offseason addition Julius Randle tell his teammates they were competing with "no pride." Brooklyn then lost their next game at home to the Magic, 113-111, after a Franz Wagner go-ahead jumper—brutal. As if that wasn't bad enough, Nolan Traore, who had been settling into a nice rhythm, injured his hamstring during the first quarter of that game and will miss 8-10 weeks.

While things seem to be going against the Nets, the vibes around the Deer District are steadily improving: if not for a missed call in the Saturday matinee game, the Bucks may have pulled off the biggest upset of the year by beating Philadelphia on the road. While there is no such thing as a moral victory in sports, Milwaukee can at least look back at this 18-game stretch and take a lot away from it. For starters, they have had far and away the toughest schedule of any Eastern Conference team and the toughest November schedule in the entire league. But despite the brutal schedule, the Bucks have kept things close, playing in seven clutch-time games this year (and making a habit of it over the last five games). This is largely due to a burgeoning new bench mob—the Bucks currently rank second in the NBA in bench points at 45.2. While Milwaukee cannot afford to take anything for granted at 2-16, it feels as though they enter this game in the driver's seat.

Joshua Jefferson: Out (G League assignment)

With Nolan Traore out, Mikel Brown Jr. will likely get the start again, and the sixth overall pick in this year's draft is looking to build on his last game in Minnesota. Turnovers (2.4 per game to just 3.8 assists per game) have been an issue, and the shooting has been streaky (36.4% from the field), but if Brown can put things together, it will help energize an offense that could match the pace the Bucks have played at over the last few games.

Who: Milwaukee Bucks (2-16) vs. Brooklyn Nets (3-13).

Kel'el Ware will finally have his breakout game. Brooklyn ranks 22nd overall in paint points allowed at 48.9. If Ware's three-point shooting is on, it could spell doom for the Nets by opening lanes for the Bucks' crafty backcourt; if not, the Kryptonian will make his presence felt near the rim. 15 and 10 with five blocks as the Bucks notch their third victory of the year!

#2800. Major League Baseball sues players’ union, asking court to declare lockout is legal

Major League Baseball sued its players' association in federal court on Wednesday, asking that its contemplated lockout next week be declared legal and trying to head off any attempt for the union to decertify and challenge the work stoppage with an antitrust case.

In a 38-page complaint filed in U.S. District Court in Manhattan, MLB and the corporate entities owning its 30 teams sued the Major League Baseball Players Association and the eight players on the union's executive subcommittee, including record-setting pitcher Tarik Skubal.

"MLB brings this action to keep the dispute over the next collective bargaining agreement ('CBA') at the bargaining table, where federal law says it belongs," the lawsuit suit. "MLB has a reasonable and imminent fear that the Union will attempt to use the threat of antitrust liability to derail the collective bargaining process altogether in favor of antitrust litigation."

Baseball's five-year labor contract expires at 11:59 p.m. Dec. 1 and owners are expected to immediately impose a lockout, which would be the sport's 10th work stoppage since 1972. Owners have proposed a salary cap for the first time since 1994, when players fought off the proposal with a 7 1/2-month strike that led to the first cancellation of the World Series in 90 years.

Owners asked for a declaratory judgment determining a lockout doesn't violate antitrust laws and doesn't amount to an unreasonable restraint of trade. They asked for a ruling that the federal courts lack jurisdiction to enjoin a lockout and for an order that the players be defined as a legal class, which if granted could block individual lawsuits.

"The lawsuit filed today by MLB is completely frivolous," the union said in a statement. "We will respond appropriately, but MLB's decision to run to court in the middle of negotiations rather than bargain in good faith is incredibly disappointing."

Among the lawyers representing MLB are former Solicitor General Paul D. Clement and Keker, Van Nest & Peters, which worked on behalf of the union in litigation against government seizure of drug tests.

"Today's filing is a defensive measure simply intended to keep the parties at the bargaining table to work towards a new collective bargaining agreement and a full 2027 season," MLB spokesman Glen Caplin said in a statement. "We believe the union is preparing to follow the playbook its new leadership has used against other major professional sports leagues by pretending to 'disclaim' its status as the players' bargaining process."

Caplin referred to decertification as a "nuclear option," mirroring union head Bruce Meyer's description of the salary cap proposal as a "nuclear bomb."

#2799. 2026-27 NBA Rookies Report Card 2.0, Grades Nov. 25: Two players get an A: Cameron Boozer, Yaxel Landeborg

No one is going to confuse me with a Harvard professor, but it feels like "grade inflation" has hit Rookie Report Cards 2.0. Turns out, this highly touted draft class is largely living up to its billing, with many of these players not just showing promise (if not efficiency yet), but also contributing to winning. There are a lot of good grades, and anything below a B feels a bit harsh this early in the season (and their careers). With that intro, here's our second round of Rookie Report Cards.

2026-27 Stats: 16.6 points, 9.2 rebounds, 5.3 assists, 41.7% shooting

What Boozer is doing with Memphis is what he has done at every stop on his basketball journey: The stats are impressive, but they don't begin to tell the whole story. Boozer simply contributes to winning, doing whatever the team needs on a given night, and he has been completely at ease as the face of the franchise since he walked in the door. He has been everything the Grizzlies hoped for when they drafted him.

Boozer has been a big part of Memphis' fast start, as has their depth — and that depth will be put to the test for the next month with Boozer out due to a hamstring strain. Injuries suck.

2026-27 Stats: 19.2 points, 7.4 rebounds, 3.6 assists, 45.2% shooting

Heading into last June's draft, the league-wide consensus was that the top four picks were in a class of their own, and everyone else was a tier below. Lendeborg has blown that logic up early this season and should be considered a frontrunner for Rookie of the Year. He ranks near the top of this rookie class in pretty much every statistical category — points (second), rebounds (second), blocks (fourth) — and his lowest is sixth in assists. He's shooting 36.7% from 3-point range and has been incredibly efficient for a rookie.

Things have been tougher for Lendeborg since Stephen Curry went out and he became the guy at the top of opponent scouting reports — asking him to lead the offense and be the focus of the defense is a lot for a rookie. That said, Lendeborg has still played well and is exactly the guy the Warriors needed.

2026-27 Stats: 7.9 points, 2.7 rebounds, 4.2 assists, 48.8% shooting

It took just nine games for Flemmings to replace veteran CJ McCollum in the starting lineup in Atlanta, a sign of how good he's looked to start the season — and how good he's looked in practices to coach Quin Snyder and the staff. He has just returned from missing a few games with a back issue.

He is a two-way player who brings physicality and defends well. In Summer League, his decision-making off the pick-and-roll showed promise — and he has carried that over to the regular season, especially since moving into the starting lineup. What was unimpressive at Summer League was his shooting, but he has found his form to start the season, and while the volume is low, he is knocking down 40% of his 3s so far this season. Atlanta may just have the point guard they were seeking.

2026-27 Stats: 11.4 points, 5.7 rebounds, 2.5 assists, 45.5% shooting

I am higher on Burries play so far than many others, and this early-season grade might be optimistic, but I like what I see. Maybe nobody is noticing because few people are watching Milwaukee right now, but once Taylor Jenkins moved Burries back to the bench a couple of games ago, it seemed to unlock something, he's playing much more free and much more efficiently. On the season, he has a below-average 52.4 true shooting percentage as a starter, but that jumps to an impressive 63 as a reserve.

There is legitimate concern about his 3-point shot (now up to 31.3% for the season), but just watching him play, Burries is a reason for hope for a Bucks fanbase that needs something to get behind at the start of a rebuilding process.

2026-27 Stats: 12.7 points, 2.7 rebounds, 1.8 assists, 47.2% shooting

Acuff's running mate at Arkansas, who fell to the No. 34 pick, looks like the steal of the second round. This grade is based partly on the expectations vs. production the Cavaliers have gotten from him — he's started three games and is getting 18+ minutes a night, something impressive for any rookie.

Thomas is a shooter, knocking down 40.7% on 3-pointers. We should have seen this coming when he led Summer League in scoring, averaging 27.3 points per game. He's got room to grow — he isn't much of a playmaker yet and isn't driving and drawing a lot of fouls — but he is knocking down shots in a league where that is a highly prized skill.

2026-27 Stats: 16.5 points, 6.9 rebounds, 2.8 assists, 42.2% shooting

It's a little hard to grade Dybantsa because it feels like he's being limited by his environment. This man is a strong, physical driver who has to wait his turn as the third guy in the pecking order behind Anthony Davis (great start to the season) and Trae Young (um... not as great a start to the season, let's say). Plus, Alex Sarr is a quality young big, but he's not spacing the floor or creating driving lanes. Still, we see Dybantsa's undeniable talent shine through at moments.

Dybantsa seems to be breaking through of late, with three double-doubles in his last four games and 19.8 points and 10.3 rebounds in those contests. The Wizards need to build on that and feature Dybantsa more and let him grow and learn through some mistakes. His grade is this low only because a left ankle sprain delayed his start to the season and, with that, his development is maybe a little behind where it could be, but he's starting to really demand more time on the ball and more opportunities.

2026-27 Stats: 15.9 points, 4.5 rebounds, 4 assists, 40.1% shooting

Kind of like Dybantsa in Washington, the challenge with Peterson in Utah has been fit. You can try to label Peterson and Utah's other young guard, Keyonte George, as point and shooting guards (in whatever order you wish), the reality is they are both scoring guards who want to attack. Then, take Lauri Markkanen and Jaren Jackson Jr. out of the picture in Utah due to injuries and it's just hard to get a read on Peterson at times.

That said, Peterson is improving — he had a stretch of five 20+ point games a few weeks ago — and more importantly, he's getting to the rim more and not settling for midrange jumpers as he did earlier in the season. He is at his best when he is an aggressive, downhill attacker reading the defense, and the Jazz need to encourage more of that and less of him pulling up or taking floaters. Let him be himself, working through the growing pains.

2026-27 Stats: 24.6 points, 3.4 rebounds, 7.2 assists, 40.3% shooting

While some high draft picks are having to stand in line to get touches and opportunities, that is not the case with Acuff — he is at the front of the line for the all-you-can-eat buffet of shots in Sacramento. He's third in the league in shot attempts per game. He actually leads the NBA in average time of possession — he has the ball for 7.2 minutes per game.

The Kings' record this season is what you get when you turn the keys to the offense over to a 6'2" rookie guard, and no doubt his efficiency and ability to lift up teammates must get better. What you have to like is his relentlessness, and he shows he knows how to read the pick-and-roll. There is a lot to build on, but Acuff also has a long way to go. As do the Kings.

2026-27 Stats: 10 points, 7.1 rebounds, 1.9 assists, 64.9% shooting

Thrust into a larger role with Chet Holmgren out, what you have to like about Mara is that he plays within himself — he knows what he does well, where he can impact games, and he stays in that box. He's not going to space the floor (which is why the double-big lineups with him and Isaiah Hartenstein can have issues). What he can do is protect the paint, set screens and score around the rim. His defense has been fantastic, and the Thunder have needed that as they battle through a rash of injuries.

Caleb Wilson, Grade: Incomplete (B- in games he played)

2026-27 Stats: 18.9 points, 6.2 rebounds, 1.9 assists, 42% shooting

He's only played in 10 games due to a fractured hand, which is a setback in his development and the reason for the incomplete grade (something that will change later in the season). It's also a freak injury beyond his control, a fluke.

If we base the grade on the 10 games he played, you see a player with immense talent — maybe the highest ceiling of any player in this class — who has already put together an impressive highlight reel.nBut he is so raw and making so many rookie mistakes — turnovers, missed 3s that he should pass on — it's hard to grade him. Do we grade him on the potential? Then it's an "A," no question. However, if we're judging him as a rookie player, as I have done, the grade drops, maybe B- is too harsh, but it's where I landed. For now. I expect that by the end of the season his grade will be much higher. He will figure it out.

2026-27: 11.2 points, 3.5 rebounds, 2.9 assists, 34.4% shooting

Part of the challenge in grading Wagler is that he comes off the bench at a stacked position for the team. Wagler is in the same space as veterans Derrick Jones Jr., Kris Dunn and Max Strus, and Tyronn Lue needs to showcase those veterans for potential trades. Lue has admitted he needs to find Wagler more minutes.

There are a lot of things Wagler does well, he can knock down 3s (he's been impressive as a shooter of late after a slow start), and he knows how to get to the free throw line. The concern is the 34% shooting on two-pointers, a lot of them from floater range. All of that should improve, and his game will come around if he gets enough minutes on the court.

2026-27: 6.3 points, 4.7 rebounds, 1.4 assists, 42.2% shooting

This grade may be a little harsh for Johnson, who had injuries slowing him in training camp and is just 20 years old (Johnson is more on Cooper Flagg's timeline than the 24-year-old Lendeborg). It's far, far too early to write off Johnson. My questions are: Did the Mavericks draft the third-best player on coach Dusty May's championship Michigan team, and would Mara have been a better fit in Dallas?

That said, he has struggled with fouls on the defensive end, and is not converting enough of his offensive opportunities. There are flashes, but he has a long way to go to be the long-term forward pairing with Cooper Flagg that the Mavericks drafted him to be. It's early in the season and his career, but Johnson has a lot of work to do.

Others receiving consideration: Mikel Brown Jr., Brooklyn (injuries and a minutes limit have held him back so far, but he shows real promise); Trevon Brazile, Denver (the best defensive rookie in this class, already a plus defender); Christian Anderson, Charlotte; Allen Graves, Toronto; Bennett Stirtz, Oklahoma City.

The Michigan senator said voters don't want "nibbling at the edges."

#2793. Zac Taylor looks back on Bengals disappointing loss at Washington

Of the Cincinnati Bengals' three losses so far in 2026, the Nov. 23 loss to the Washington Commanders was really the one that got away.

The Bengals (7-3) were competitive in all three losses to varying degrees, but at Washington, they led most of the night, recaptured the lead with under a minute to play and couldn't get one final stop after that in a 22-20 walk-off field goal win for the Commanders (4-5-1).

Bengals Commanders Jason Williams Hey, Zac Taylor, Joe Burrow. What happened to running the ball?

Cincinnati has a short week to prepare for their Nov. 29 home game against the New Orleans Saints (3-7), but spent most of his Nov. 25 news conference at Paycor Stadium revisiting key aspects of the costly loss to the Commanders.

Following the Bengals' Sunday Night Football win over AFC North rival Pittsburgh on Nov. 15, one Bengals' captain essentially chalked up the state of the Bengals' running game to: "When we're passing it like we are, why force a running game."

That wasn't a bad way to think about the win over the Steelers, which the Bengals dominated. But at Northwest Stadium against Washington, Cincinnati amassed just 31 rushing yards. That sum flirted with the 23 yards they gained on the ground in the loss to the Jacksonville Jaguars on Oct. 4, which stands as the worst rushing game by the team under Taylor.

The working theory within the Bengals' offense has a few prongs related to the run game, and one was conveyed by the Bengals' captain. Another prong is that the Bengals have passing plays that essentially function as running plays using dump-offs around the line of scrimmage. And maybe those plays develop in ways that are more advantageous for Cincinnati's backs, Chase Brown and Samaje Perine.

The Bengals are going into Week 12, and Taylor seemed to say the Bengals are who they are when it comes to running the ball.

With that could come difficulties in games like the one they lost as Washington.

"I think 'Pitch' (offensive coordinator Dan Pitcher) said it best: 'We'll be whoever we need to be as the year goes on," Taylor said. "He said that in training camp and in the offseason. I still feel very strongly about that as the season goes."

On the stroke of the two-minute warning in the fourth quarter, the Bengals were perched on the Commanders' 33-yard line with a fresh set of downs following a 32-yard reception by Mike Gesicki.

From there, the go-ahead field goal by Evan McPherson would have been 50 yards, well within his makeable range. But the Bengals also had the game in their hands in every respect, and a further first down would move McPherson even closer for what could have been a walk-off field goal. That was the dream scenario, but it didn't come to fruition for several reasons.

There was an injury timeout that stopped the clock, but in what many would assume as a running situation, the Bengals had Burrow drop back to pass twice. First, he was almost intercepted. The second time, he was sacked, moving McPherson's eventual go-ahead kick back to 55 yards.

McPherson made the kick, but Washington got the ball back with enough time to move into range for its own walk-off field goal.

"It played out the same either way and I understand that was because of an injury timeout," Taylor said. "But the point is, I wanted to kick a field goal with three seconds left and win the game so you don't give them any opportunity to do what they just did.

"I felt very strongly putting the ball in (Burrow's) hands and throwing the ball and seeing if we can get the first down."

Of the Burrow sack on third down, Taylor said he could have given the offense a better chance at converting via his play-calling.

As ugly as Cincinnati's offense looked early on against Washington, the Commanders looked worse. A lot of that had to do with the Bengals getting to quarterback Jayden Daniels.

Boye Mafe tallied three sacks by himself, and the Bengals were really making life difficult for the Commanders at the line of scrimmage and in their backfield.

But then the pressure on Daniels disappeared. Washington's offense found a groove on its final drive of the first half, eventually scoring on each of its last six drives.

The Commanders' last five drives ended in field goals by kicker Drew Stevens — each from 41 yards and closer.

To the defense's credit, Washington had minimal success in the red zone and scored just one touchdown. And you might have liked Cincinnati's chances if you were told ahead of time Washington would only score 22 points in the game.

But the Bengals might need to win tight, low-scoring games like this down the road, and do so against higher-caliber opponents.

When Cincinnati could not longer get to Daniels, it also couldn't get off the field.

Taylor agreed some rotation along the defensive line could have helped but for what he called a strange start to the game, plus the fact that the defense was getting off the field for almost the entire first half.

"You've got to find some opportunities for Shemar (Stewart) and Cedric (Johnson) to mix it in… We'll rotate those guys a little more," Taylor said.

This article originally appeared on Cincinnati Enquirer: Zac Taylor looks back on Bengals disappointing loss at Washington

#2791. Aaron Rodgers released by Steelers after benching, scathing criticism

Aaron Rodgers has been released by the Pittsburgh Steelers, according to multiple reports.

Earlier Wednesday, Rodgers went on "The Pat McAfee" show and blasted Steelers head coach Mike McCarthy for "habits" he has displayed dating back to their time together with the Green Bay Packers (2008-18).

The Steelers announced late Tuesday night that Rodgers would be inactive for the team's game Friday, Nov. 27, against the Denver Broncos. Mason Rudolph will start at quarterback.

Rodgers, who will turn 43 next week, was in his second season with the franchise. He won four MVPs and Super Bowl 45, along with McCarthy, with the Packers and spent two seasons with the New York Jets.

"At this point, I'm not going back to Pittsburgh," Rodgers told McAfee. "That ship has definitely sailed."

Rodgers will now be subject to the waiver system and can be claimed anytime until 4 p.m. ET on Thursday, Nov. 26. If he clears waivers, he will be a free agent and can sign with any team.

Rodgers has thrown 16 touchdowns and six interceptions this season. He was 27th in success rate and 29th in expected points added per play, however.

The Steelers currently maintain the seventh and final playoff spot in the AFC postseason race. Drew Allar and Will Howard are the other quarterbacks on Pittsburgh's roster in addition to Rudolph.

Rodgers has said the 2026 season, his 22nd in the league, is his final go.

"I didn't want this to be the way that it ended," Rodgers said. "I was very clear with the ultimatum. And it wasn't a veiled ultimatum. It wasn't posturing."

He also thanked the Steelers' faithful fans for embracing him. Rodgers led the Steelers to the 2025 AFC North title in former head coach Mike Tomlin's final season.

"It was special," Rodgers said. "There's nothing like being on the sidelines listening to 'Renegade' in the fourth quarter."

Rodgers currently ranks fifth on the all-time passing yards list and third on the all-time passing touchdowns list. Only five players have thrown more passes than him in NFL history. He has the league's lowest interception rate (1.4%) ever and no quarterback has taken more sacks than Rodgers.

This article originally appeared on USA TODAY: Aaron Rodgers released by Steelers after benching, scathing criticism

#2777. Tokenization platform Securitize gains 10% postmarket on Q3 results, move into asset-backed finance

Tokenization platform Securitize climbed about 10% postmarket after reporting third quarter growth alongside increased operating expenses, and announcing an expansion into asset-backed finance through a strategic acquisition.

Miami-based Securitize, which made its public market debut in July, operates in the relatively new corner of the crypto equity universe involved in institutional tokenization infrastructure. Tokenized assets on the platform grew 9% in the third quarter, to $4.8 billion, from the same period a year ago. Aggregate transaction volume rose 20% from a year ago, to $4.3 billion. Revenue of $11.5 million was up 28% from last year.

The growth came as bullish sentiment around tokenization gained traction throughout the third quarter, ahead of the Security and Exchange Commission formalizing the long awaited Innovation Exemption on Sept. 17, which created a temporary pathway for trading of certain tokenized stocks and strengthened the regulatory outlook.

Profitability is still meaningfully below year-ago levels, due in part to costs associated with going public over the summer, as well as the scaling up of the tokenization business at an active time for the entire sector, Securitize CEO Carlos Domingo told CNBC in an exclusive interview.

Securitize reinforced "certain areas of the company like finance, compliance, legal [and] cybersecurity," which increased corporate costs "compared to a year ago when we were privately held," Domingo said. "We're in in a very good position. The adjusted EBITDA … is improving significantly from previous quarters."

Securitize reported an adjusted EBITDA loss of $2 million, compared with positive adjusted EBITDA of $3.6 million a year ago, better than a $5.5 million loss in the second quarter. The company posted a 183% increase in operating costs and expenses.

Also on Monday, the Securitize board authorized a $50 million share buyback and announced the acquisition of River One, an institutional asset-backed financing platform developing technology to connect stablecoin capital with traditional lending markets. Terms were not disclosed.

Securitize sees an opportunity to modernize the $9 trillion market for asset backed finance by using stablecoins to finance real world assets, generate yield and tokenize investment positions and credit products, Domingo said.The acquisition also marks an expansion beyond Securitize's core niche markets at a time when tokenization's competitive landscape is growing, with traditional asset managers like BlackRock and Franklin Templeton, and established financial market infrastructure providers like DTCC and Broadridge, entering the industry.

"We have been very active in tokenized funds, treasuries [and] yield … And we've been obviously recently very active in tokenized stocks, so this adds another piece of the puzzle in the emerging tokenization space for us to capture revenue growth."

#2776. Ex-F1 driver Martin Donnelly confirms memoir release with Lewis Hamilton and Brad Pitt contribution

More than three decades after one of Formula 1's most harrowing accidents cut his grand prix career short, Martin Donnelly is finally putting the full story in his own words.

Retailer listings from Penguin Books UK and Transworld confirm that the former Lotus driver's autobiography, Unbroken: A Memoir of Speed and Survival, will launch on 3 June 2027, across a 288-page hardback (£22.00), ebook, and audio download under the Bantam imprint.

Even before page one, the book carries major star power. Seven-time world champion Sir Lewis Hamilton has contributed the introduction, while Brad Pitt has penned the foreword - reuniting the duo who helped reintroduce Donnelly's survival story to millions of moviegoers worldwide.

In 2025's F1: The Movie - which grossed $634.1million globally to become Pitt's highest-earning theatrical release before winning Best Sound at the 2026 Academy Awards - Sonny Hayes's career-halting crash backstory was modelled directly on Donnelly's 1990 accident, even incorporating archival crash imagery as a pivotal plot device.

Donnelly's involvement began with an unexpected phone call from Hamilton, who co-produced the film. Speaking to CNN last year, Donnelly joked that he initially assumed it was "one of those cold calls for central heating or double-glazed windows", admitting, "It's not every day you receive a call from a seven-time world champion."

Watching filmmakers launch a yellow-overalled mannequin from a chassis 15 times to recreate his crash gave the Belfast-born driver a jarring "reality check", while seeing the finished sequence on screen left him telling the BBC it felt like "such a surreal moment. It was something only I could appreciate."

Born in March 1964, Donnelly entered 15 grands prix and made 13 starts across 1989 and 1990 with Arrows and Camel Team Lotus, peaking with seventh place at the 1990 Hungarian Grand Prix. According to Transworld's synopsis, the morning of 28 September 1990, at Jerez opened with Donnelly weighing four separate racing contracts that could have reshaped his future.

Hours later in Friday qualifying, a suspension failure sent his Lamborghini-powered Lotus 102 into the barriers at roughly 160 mph. "The carbon fibre tub shattered like a car bomb, and I went with the energy," Donnelly recalled to CNN. "I got thrown out by about 60 metres [almost 200 feet] and travelled through the air and along the ground like a rag doll."

Left strapped into his seat on the asphalt with head and lung contusions, two broken legs, and a swallowed tongue, Donnelly endured an induced coma and organ failure before fighting back through years of surgeries and rehabilitation. As Penguin's listing highlights, the aftermath spurred landmark medical and track-safety reforms across motorsport - while Donnelly's chapters also revisit his era racing alongside Ayrton Senna, Nigel Mansell, and Alain Prost.

Defying medical doubts, Donnelly climbed back into an F1 cockpit less than three years later. "On 23 February 1993 I drove a Jordan car around Silverstone, and that was me giving my surgeons the pledge to say, 'You know your books, but you do not know me, and here I am,'" he told the BBC.

Now 62, Donnelly has built a lasting second act as an FIA race steward, chief driving instructor, and Lotus Cars brand ambassador - occasionally racing in events such as the British Touring Car Championship in 2015, driving a Lotus 102 at Goodwood in 2011, and finally piloting an Arrows A10B around Adelaide in February 2026, 36 years after his crash kept him from ever racing F1 there. With Unbroken now available for pre-order ahead of its June 2027 debut, fans who discovered Sonny Hayes on the big screen will soon get the unvarnished real-world story behind him.

To read more Motorsport.com articles visit our website.

→ Baca artikel asli

#2772. What's ahead for AI financing after Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang departs

Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang's departure hasn't produced a broad shock to the credit market, but it is putting the company's role as a financier of artificial intelligence infrastructure under scrutiny.

Huang, who stepped down Thursday amid an espionage and adultery scandal, built Nvidia into an AI powerhouse in the 33 years since he co-founded the company. Chief financial officer Colette Kress has been named interim CEO as the company searches for a permanent chief executive.

Nvidia not only supplies its graphics processing units to hyperscalers and tech giants, it also provides financing to other companies to help the industry expand. That could all change.

"Although we expect the company to fulfill all of its previously announced commitments, we believe that going forward, it may be more difficult for the company to negotiate new multi-party investments among ecosystem partners at the same scale it has done up to this point," Goldman Sachs analyst James Schneider said in a note Thursday.

The news of Huang's resignation comes after investors were already concerned about the massive amount of debt being taken on to fund AI infrastructure.

Tech bond yields have risen and spreads have widened on credit default swaps, most notably in lower-rated names. Credit default swaps are contracts in which investors pay for protection in case the borrower can't repay its debt. Wider spreads indicate investors view the bonds as riskier.

On Friday, the yield on Nvidia's 4.5% bond due in 2031 sat at 5.717%, nearly 5% higher week to date. Bond yields move inversely to prices.

Tech stocks slumped Friday with the Nasdaq 100 closing down 1.2% as markets continued to digest the Nvidia news. Yields on hyperscaler bonds ticked slightly higher on Friday, signaling the broader concern about AI debt lingers.

It's the smaller AI companies that could ultimately see an impact if Nvidia makes any changes to its AI financing. The company had $36 billion in off-balance-sheet AI cloud agreements, or backstops, for neoclouds, according to its last quarterly financial filing in July.

Neoclouds CoreWeave, Nebius and IREN are all in Nvidia's portfolio. Their shares were down sharply Thursday.

CoreWeave, which has become the poster child for AI debt fears, saw its bond yields retreat after its quarterly earnings on Tuesday eased investor concerns. The yield on its 9.75% bond due in 2031 was 14.79% on Friday after climbing to 16.7% last week. The spread on its five-year CDS also dropped more than 17% week to date, although it remained above 1,000 — which is generally considered a sign of distress.

It remains unclear how Nvidia's support for those companies may change, said CreditSights analyst Andy Li.

"We don't know what the ultimate strategy for Nvidia is going to be coming out the other side," he said.

"However, I think directionally, just given the fact that Jensen was so crucial in selling the Nvidia story and being a very aggressive deal maker, it's likely that a lot of this creative deal-making and circular financing and equity and credit support is going to moderate," he added.

CoreWeave CEO Michael Intrator told CNBC Wednesday, before Huang's departure, that Nvidia "is not one person."

"There are thousands of people over there that interface with thousands of people at my company every day. They integrate on the technology. They integrate on the physical infrastructure. They integrate on the networking," he said in an interview with "Squawk on the Street." "They have been a great partner for us for years. They will continue to be a great partner for us."

Bob Lang, founder and chief strategist at Explosive Options, doesn't see any big changes in terms of Nvidia's relevance or its ability to finance for now. As CFO, Kress was involved in the financing deals, and the search for a permanent CEO is going to take some time, he noted.

The real issue is just how much money the company will want to lend for AI financing, rather than, say using its hundreds of billions in free cash flow for other purposes, like share buybacks, capex, or acquisitions.

"Big companies like Nvidia and Meta, they're holding all the cards," he said. "When they decide that the game is over, that's it — it's going to be curtains for some of these smaller companies."

— CNBC'S Kif Leswing and Isabel O'Brien contributed reporting.

#2768. Chamblee Demands End To PGA Tour Safety Nets For LIV Returnees

Brandel Chamblee believes the PGA Tour must stop protecting players who left for the breakaway circuit. The analyst argues that returning golfers need to earn their spots through the same grueling path as everyone else. This stance comes as the tour prepares to split into two tiers for the 2028 season. The new structure will focus heavily on promotion and relegation to keep the field competitive. Chamblee insists that existing exemptions for former LIV members should vanish immediately. He sees the current system as a loophole that undermines the meritocratic nature of professional sports.

Speaking on The Big Swing with Jimmy Roberts, Chamblee outlined a harsh reality for stars like Bryson DeChambeau and Jon Rahm. Both men won major championships in the last three years and will likely face one-year suspensions before returning. Exemption for the Championship Series in 2028 would apply to them under current plans. Chamblee rejects this idea entirely. "I wouldn't be surprised if some of these players have got to go back to the Korn Ferry Tour, got to go to the [Challenger Series], and then work their way into the Championship Series, and as they should," he said. "You've got to come back and prove yourself." He argues that the tour has too many safety nets that let players stay at the highest level long after they have lost their edge.

Chamblee views the removal of these protections as essential for the health of the game. He believes the tour can easily get rid of these safety nets to create a fairer environment. "If you really want to make golf more meritocratic, you need to get rid of these safety nets," he told reporters. This approach contrasts sharply with the recent handling of players like Marco Penge, who nearly lost his card at The RSM Classic. Penge's victory at Sea Island secured his top-100 status and a spot on the Championship Series for 2028. After their major wins this season, Aaron Rai and Ryan Fox gained exemptions until the end of 2031. The difference in treatment between those who stayed and those who left is the core of Chamblee's argument.

The analyst also defended outgoing commissioner Jay Monahan against critics who blame him for the LIV Golf disruption. According to GolfMagic, Chamblee compared LIV's business model to a carmaker giving vehicles away for free. "The only thing that was unique about it [LIV] was it was a business model that did not care about their return on investment," Chamblee said. He noted that no one could have predicted an irrational actor entering the arena. "Well, no, because nobody could have foreseen an irrational actor coming into the arena. So, you know, I'm not quick to blame Jay Monahan," he added. Monahan will step down at the end of 2026, with Brian Rolapp taking over on January 1. Chamblee hopes history will be kind to Monahan given the unique challenges he faced.

Chamblee remains skeptical about the long-term value of the LIV Golf product. He argues that the circuit failed because it tried to manufacture passion without the element of national pride. "Well, it took some 70 years for the Ryder Cup to develop the passionate following that it had, but the players in the Ryder Cup are playing for their country," Chamblee said. He dismissed the idea that team golf could replicate that emotion. "LIV golfers were playing for greed and that was it." This financial motivation, he suggests, is why the breakaway could not sustain the same emotional connection as traditional team events.

Over the past four years, the analyst continues to worry about the harm done to the sport. "I think people look back and at least over the last 40 or 50 years, remember this is probably the worst period in golf's history and I'm not so sure that it won't echo unfortunately into the future," he added. Despite these bleak assessments, the focus now shifts to the 2027 season and the changes coming in 2028. The tour will likely see a more rigid structure where performance dictates status, and past contracts offer no shield from the qualifying process.

#2765. How To “Zag” When The Zigs Aren’t Easy

Luring good free agent starting pitchers to spend a year in West Sacramento? Tough sell. It remains to be seen if there is a current core position player Taylor Smith and Cam Castro are willing to deal in the name of improving their starting pitching.

Left to be creative, here are some possible ways Smithtro might zag, finding different ways to get more solid starting pitching into the fold for 2027 and beyond. Some of it they are already doing…

While the Mets were announcing the signing of Tarik Skubal and the Cubs were boldly adding both Trevor Rogers and Teruaki Sato, the A's were busy as well — behind the scenes.

Smith and Castro are no longer the "newbies" in the organization with Trevor Amicone (assistant hitting coach), Casey Weathers (pitching coach/staff guide), Ian Walsh Director of Pitching), and Max Gordon (Director of Hitting) joining the A's while other teams were adding players.

This could be impactful if the new crew can tap into the very real potential that lies in the arms of Jack Perkins and Luis Morales. On the hitting side, perhaps fresh voices can convince Jacob Wilson to be more disciplined in what he swings at or help Denzel Clarke to lift the ball instead of hitting so many left side ground balls.

The A's could try to bolster the rotation in a roundabout way, going hard on improving the bullpen and then moving Luis Medina into the rotation. Ever since he mastered his changeup to go with his fastball/slider combination, Medina has been excellent and has a repertoire that could play as a SP (which was his role throughout the minors and initially with the A's).

Andrew Kittredge, Jakob Junis, Bryan Abreu, and Alex Vesia are among the relievers the A's could reasonably hope to land if they make a good enough offer. Sign two of them to join Hogan Harris for the late innings and you could afford to move Medina into the rotation.

Starting pitching is so expensive that it's very possible even the "tier 2" targets such as Clay Holmes or Michael King will be too hard to lure. Pitchers simply do not love the idea of pitching in Sutter Health Park and they are going to have many suitors.

Position players can be pricey as well but perhaps slightly less prohibitive and an easier sell for the A's right now. One example of a "zag" move the A's could make would be to sign, say, Brandon Lowe and then have the depth to deal Zack Gelof or Jacob Wilson for the pitching help they need. MLBTR's guess on Lowe's contract is 3/$69M, not cheap, but essentially you would be paying "Severino money" for a player that allows you to go out and trade for a SP or two.

There's also Luis Arraez, who turned into a plus 2Bman under the tutelage of Ron Washington. He is projected by MLBTR to get 3/$48M, well within the A's budget. Or you could sign Jazz Chisholm Jr., projected for 2/$40M. That would also free you up to move an infielder for pitching.

Similarly, in theory you could sign Ian Happ, projected by MLBTR to get 3/$45M, to patrol LF. Happ has averaged 3.2 fWAR over the past 5 seasons and would be a decent replacement for Tyler Soderstrom if you wanted to spin him for pitching.

Finally, while the free agent catching market is thin, Carson Kelly is still out there projected by MLBTR to get 2/$20M. He's a step down from Shea Langeliers but Langeliers would bring you back considerable pitching talent if he were made available.

So there are some ways the front office could be thinking in their quest to improve the A's pitching. What "zags," if any, do you suppose they are considering? Or how, in your opinion, should they be approaching this final week before a looming lockout? No easy answers, but some interesting possibilities…

#2763. Sixers look to get back on track as they resume NBA cup play vs. Miami

It appears the Sixers' next test without Joel Embiid will be a challenging one. Philadelphia hosts the Miami Heat the Wednesday before Thanksgiving, tipping off at 5 p.m. to start an entire evening of NBA Cup action. Miami leads Eastern Conference Group B at 2-0 coming into this one. The Sixers are in third place at 1-1 and have a minus-19 point differential.

The Heat have gotten out to a lead in the group while riding a seven-game winning streak entering play today. After a slow start to the season, their average margin of victory has been 11.2 points over those seven games. Like the Sixers, this new Heat team hasn't looked quite as expected to start the year. They're 6th in points scored per 100 possessions but 17th in points allowed per CleaningTheGlass.

They come to town at a bad time for the Sixers, likely to be without Joel Embiid for the second straight game. Embiid is listed as doubtful with an illness. For Miami, their big acquisition Giannis Antetekounmpo is questionable with groin tendinitis. Antetekounmpo did miss their last game against the Oklahoma City Thunder, but seems set to return.

The pairing of Antetekounmpo and Bam Adebayo projects to give an undersized Sixers' frontcourt plenty of problems. In the six games Antetekounmpo has played during this winning streak, he's averaged 29.5 points per game with a 68.4% effective field goal percentage.

After playing 11 minutes in his debut, Barlow did get up to 16 in his second game back, but was favoring his back after taking a couple of spills.

The Heat have been hot from outside as of late, shooting 39% from three or better in each of their las three games. That could be a problem for a Sixers team that has not been sharp defending the point of attack as of late. The good news is after shipping out some of their younger wings, namely Jaime Jacquez Jr., this Miami team isn't the offensive rebounding machine they have been in years past. They rank 21st so far this season in offensive rebounding percentage.

The key for the Sixers might be a big game for Tyrese Maxey, who's been quiet for his standards offensively. In his last three games he's averaged 21 points per game.

Jaylen Brown did look fantastic in the first half of his first game back. He shot 8-of-10 from the floor for 19 points, easily getting to whatever spot on the floor he wanted. When he picked up his fourth foul he had to sit for nearly the entire third quarter and that greatly disrupted his rhythm. Brown didn't make a field goal in the second half and admitted after the game he felt physically different.

Getting more of that first half Brown would greatly help the Sixers' chances against Miami, but more importantly, Sixers fans should hope that isn't a sign of Brown's hamstring injury lingering.

#2757. 49ers QB Brock Purdy misses practice and is day to day with an ankle injury

SANTA CLARA, Calif. (AP) — San Francisco 49ers quarterback Brock Purdy missed practice on Wednesday after hurting his ankle during last week's win against the Minnesota Vikings.

Coach Kyle Shanahan said that Purdy hurt himself in the second quarter of Sunday night's 35-10 win over the Minnesota Vikings but was able to remain in the game. He was not available for practice to begin the week of preparation for Sunday's division showdown against Seattle and is listed as day to day.

"He can't go today so it's always a possibility," Shanahan said if Purdy could miss the game against the Seahawks. "It's not a high ankle so it's day to day with it. We'll see how it goes."

Purdy is coming off a stellar performance against the Vikings after throwing for 255 yards and three touchdowns against one of the top defenses in the league to win NFC Offensive Player of the Week.

Purdy leads the NFL with 118.2 passer rating and 9.0 yards per pass attempt, to go with his 23 touchdown passes and only five interceptions.

Mac Jones handled the first-team work in practice on Wednesday. Jones started eight games last season when Purdy was sidelined by turf toe and helped San Francisco post a 5-3 record during that time.

Receiver Mike Evans also sat out of practice on Wednesday as the 49ers are managing the Achilles tendinitis that sidelined him for three games. Linebacker Dre Greenlaw (neck), receiver Jordan Watkins (groin), and defensive back Siran Neal (knee, foot) also didn't practice.

Defensive backs Deommodore Lenoir (foot) and Upton Stout (shoulder), and offensive linemen Austen Pleasants (knee) and Carver Willis (illness) were limited. Pleasants had his 21-day practice window opened Wednesday after starting the season on injured reserve.

The 49ers also activated cornerback Nate Hobbs off the injured reserve list and placed right tackle Colton McKivitz on IR with a season-ending ankle injury. Receiver Brandin Cooks was re-signed to the practice squad after being cut last week.

Defensive end Nick Bosa said he wasn't surprised by the boos he got in Mexico City after his remarks last week about how they cheered.

Bosa was booed loudly when he was showed on the video board before the game on Sunday after saying the Mexican fans needed to be taught about how to cheer at NFL games. Bosa said he didn't want the fans to cheer constantly as they do in soccer but to reserve their loud chants to when Minnesota was on offense.

Bosa said his intentions were "pure" and he didn't mean to offense anyone but the local fans were upset and made him know about it. Bosa said he expected that reaction but enjoyed his overall trip.

"I thought it was good," he said. "I definitely didn't mean any harm with my comments. The environment was amazing like it was the last time I was there."

#2756. China’s Xiamen Airlines to buy 50 Boeing jets in $7.4 billion deal

China Southern Airlines said in a filing late Tuesday that its subsidiary Xiamen Airlines had agreed to purchase 50 Boeing 737-8 aircraft with a combined catalog price of approximately $7.4 billion.

The announcement comes after President Donald Trump told CNBC on Monday that he expected China to purchase another 250 Boeing aircraft, following his meeting with Chinese President Xi Jinping at the APEC summit last week.

"By the way, I sold 250 Boeings to China, and I think they're going to do another 250," Trump said.

In May, China approved an initial purchase of 200 Boeing aircraft for Chinese airlines following Trump's visit to Beijing, marking the country's first commitment to purchase U.S.-made Boeing aircraft since 2017, according to the White House.

It was not immediately clear whether Xiamen Airlines' latest purchase was part of that commitment.

China Southern, which owns a 55% stake in Xiamen Airlines, said the actual purchase price would be lower than the combined list price because of discounts offered by Boeing. The company did not disclose the final purchase price, citing confidentiality provisions in the agreement.

The aircraft are scheduled to be delivered in batches between 2031 and 2034, with the purchase financed through a combination of internal funds and external financing, the filing showed. The transaction remains subject to shareholder and relevant Chinese government approvals.

China Southern said the purchase would support Xiamen Airlines' planned relocation to the new Xiamen Xiang'an International Airport and the expansion of its route network.

The agreement follows the group's recent purchase agreement for 137 Airbus A320neo-family aircraft, the filing showed. China Southern said sourcing aircraft from different manufacturers would help diversify its fleet and improve operational flexibility.

Other Asian carriers, including Philippine Airlines and Malaysia Aviation Group, have also announced Boeing aircraft orders this month.

#2755. Aaron Rodgers released by Steelers: Revisiting his high school football career

Aaron Rodgers has never been one to shy away from speaking his mind.

The four-time NFL MVP made headlines Wednesday, Nov. 25, after appearing on The Pat McAfee Show, where he publicly criticized Pittsburgh Steelers coach Mike McCarthy following the team's decision to bench him ahead of Friday's game against the Denver Broncos.

Rodgers accused McCarthy of repeatedly missing team meetings, falling asleep during a quarterback meeting and falling behind on film preparation. He also detailed disagreements over the team's offensive approach and said his relationship with the Steelers had reached its breaking point.

Rodgers made it clear he does not expect to return to Pittsburgh. Shortly after appearing with McAfee, the Steelers announced they would be releasing the soon-to-be Hall of Fame quarterback.

But long before becoming one of the most accomplished quarterbacks in NFL history, Rodgers was a relatively overlooked high school prospect in Northern California.

Here's what to know about his high school football career:

Rodgers attended Pleasant Valley High School in Chico, California, where he played quarterback for the Vikings.

A two-year varsity starter from 2000-01, Rodgers established himself as one of the top quarterbacks in Northern California. He passed for 4,419 yards during his junior and senior seasons and earned all-section honors twice. As a senior, Rodgers set a school record with 2,303 passing yards.

Despite his production, Rodgers did not attract significant attention from major college football programs. He was considered undersized at the time and did not receive a Division I scholarship offer coming out of high school.

Pleasant Valley went 17-7 during Rodgers' two seasons as its starting quarterback, reaching the CIF Northern Section playoffs in consecutive years. He also played basketball and baseball, while pitching for Pleasant Valley as a senior.

Without a major college scholarship offer coming out of Pleasant Valley, Rodgers took the junior college route. He enrolled at Butte College in Oroville, California, where he became the starting quarterback for the Roadrunners.

Rodgers led Butte to a 10-1 record, a NorCal Conference championship and a No. 2 national ranking in 2002.

His performance caught the attention of California coach Jeff Tedford, who offered Rodgers an opportunity to play for the Golden Bears. Rodgers transferred to Cal and became the team's starting quarterback during the 2003 season. He played two seasons in Berkeley before entering the 2005 NFL Draft.

The Green Bay Packers selected Rodgers with the No. 24 overall pick in the first round of the 2005 NFL Draft. He spent 18 seasons in Green Bay, succeeding Brett Favre as the franchise's starting quarterback in 2008.

Rodgers went on to win four NFL MVP awards, earn 10 Pro Bowl selections and lead the Packers to a victory over the Pittsburgh Steelers in Super Bowl XLV. He later played for the New York Jets before joining Pittsburgh in 2026.

Rodgers returned to his high school roots in April 2026 when he was inducted into the Pleasant Valley High School Athletics Hall of Fame.

The four-time NFL MVP was part of the inaugural class honored at the school's Viking Legacy Dinner.
